(a) (Existence and uniqueness of solutions) For all  $\alpha \geq 0$ , if  $\pmb{\Sigma}$  is invertible and  $\sigma^2 > 0$ , then Eqs. (48a) and (48b) have a unique solution.  
(b) (Boundedness of solutions) Consider  $\alpha_{\mathrm{max}} \geq 0$  and the  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso with  $\alpha \leq \alpha_{\mathrm{max}} / \sqrt{n}$ . Assume (A1). For  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ -dependent constant  $C$ , if  $p > C$  then there exist  $0 < \tau_{\mathrm{max}} < \infty$  and  $0 < \zeta_{\mathrm{min}} < 1$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  and  $\alpha_{\mathrm{max}}$  such that the unique solution  $\tau_{\alpha}^{*}, \zeta_{\alpha}^{*}$  to Eqs. (48a) and (48b) satisfies  $\sigma \leq \tau_{\alpha}^{*} \leq \tau_{\mathrm{max}}$  and  $\zeta_{\mathrm{min}} \leq \zeta_{\alpha}^{*} \leq 1$ .

Note that Lemma 4 is the  $\alpha_{\mathrm{max}} = 0$  instance of Lemma A.2. We prove Lemma A.2 in the next section.

A.4. Control on fixed-point parameters: proofs of Lemma 4 and Lemma A.2. Lemma 4 is the  $\alpha_{\mathrm{max}} = 0$  instance of Lemma A.2. Thus, we only prove Lemma A.2. First, we prove Lemma A.2(a).

PROOF OF LEMMA A.2(A). Define functions  $\mathcal{T},\mathcal{Z}:L^2 (\mathbb{R}^p;\mathbb{R}^p)\to \mathbb{R}$  by

$$
\mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {v}) ^ {2} := \sigma^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2},
$$

$$
\mathcal {Z} (\boldsymbol {v}) := \left(1 - \frac {\langle \boldsymbol {g} , \boldsymbol {v} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}}}{\sqrt {n} \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {v})}\right) _ {+},
$$

where  $\pmb{g}$  is interpreted as the identity function in  $L^2$ . Define  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}: L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{p};\mathbb{R}^{p}) \to \mathbb{R}$  by (49)

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathscr {E} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) := \frac {1}{2} \left(\sqrt {\sigma^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}} - \frac {\langle \boldsymbol {g} , \boldsymbol {v} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) _ {+} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g})\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\} \\ =: \mathcal {F} (\boldsymbol {v}) + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g})\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Let us emphasize the argument of  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}$  is not a vector but a function  $\pmb {v}:\mathbb{R}^p\to \mathbb{R}^p$

Each of the two terms in the definition of  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}$  are convex and continuous. Moreover, for all  $g$  we have, by Eq. (44),

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g})\right) \geq \left\| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g}) \right\| _ {1} - \frac {p \alpha}{2} \right. \\ \geq \| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g}) \| _ {1} - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} - \frac {p \alpha}{2} \geq \kappa_ {\max } ^ {- 1 / 2} \| \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g}) \| _ {2} - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} - \frac {p \alpha}{2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

For any  $M > 0$

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle \boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {g} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}} | = | \mathbb {E} [ \langle \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g}), \boldsymbol {g} \mathbf {1} _ {\| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} > M} \rangle ] + \mathbb {E} [ \langle \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g}), \boldsymbol {g} \mathbf {1} _ {\| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} \leq M} \rangle ] | \\ \leq \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {L ^ {2}} \mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {\| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} > M} ] ^ {1 / 2} + M \mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g}) \| _ {2} ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

Take  $M$  large enough that  $\mathbb{E}[\| \pmb {g}\|^2\mathbf{1}_{\| \pmb {g}\| >M}]^{1 / 2} <   \sqrt{n} /2$  . Then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {E} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq \frac {1}{2} \left(\frac {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {L ^ {2}}}{2} - \frac {M}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g}) \| _ {2} ]\right) _ {+} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda \kappa_ {\max } ^ {- 1 / 2}}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g}) \| _ {2} ] - \frac {2 \lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} - \frac {p \lambda \alpha}{2 \sqrt {n}} \\ \geq \min  \left\{\frac {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}}{3 2}, \frac {\lambda \kappa_ {\operatorname* {m a x}} ^ {- 1 / 2}}{4 M} \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {L ^ {2}} \right\} - \frac {2 \lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} - \frac {p \lambda \alpha}{2 \sqrt {n}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality holds by considering the cases that  $\| \pmb{v}\|_{L^2} / 4$  is no smaller and no larger than  $M\mathbb{E}[\| \pmb {v}(\pmb {g})\| _2] / \sqrt{n}$ , respectively. We see that  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}(\pmb {v})\to \infty$  as  $\| \pmb {v}\|_{L^2}\rightarrow \infty$  whence by [4, Theorem 11.9],  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}$  has a minimizer. Let  $\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}$  be one such minimizer.

Consider the following convex function in  $L^2$  parameterized by  $\tau, \zeta \geq 0$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\mathcal {E}} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}; \zeta , \tau) := \frac {\zeta}{2} \left\| \boldsymbol {v} - \frac {\tau}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} \right\| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{n} \mathbb {E} \left\{\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\} \\ = \mathbb {E} \left\{\frac {\zeta}{2} \left\| \boldsymbol {v} - \frac {\tau}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \right\}. \\ \end{array}
$$

For fixed  $\zeta, \tau \geq 0$ , the function  $\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}$  minimizes  $\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}$  if and only if  $\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}(\pmb{g})$  minimizes the objective inside the expectation for almost every  $\pmb{g}$ . That is, if and only if

$$
\boldsymbol {v} _ {\alpha} ^ {*} = \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\eta_ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \tau \boldsymbol {g} / \sqrt {n}; \zeta\right) - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}\right) \text {a l m o s t s u r e l y}. \tag {50}
$$

For any  $\pmb{v}_0, \pmb{v}_1 \in L^2$  fixed, we have by differentiation of  $\mathcal{F}$  with respect to  $\varepsilon \in \mathbb{R}$  that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\mathcal {E}} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {0} + \varepsilon \boldsymbol {v} _ {1}; \mathcal {Z} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {0}), \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {0})) - \mathcal {E} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {0} + \varepsilon \boldsymbol {v} _ {1}) = \frac {\mathcal {Z} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {0})}{2} \left\| \boldsymbol {v} _ {0} + \varepsilon \boldsymbol {v} _ {1} - \frac {\mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {0})}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} \right\| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} - \mathcal {F} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {0} + \varepsilon \boldsymbol {v} _ {1}) \\ = \tilde {\mathcal {E}} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {0}; \mathcal {Z} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {0}); \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {0})) - \mathcal {E} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {0}) + O (\epsilon^ {2}). \\ \end{array}
$$

Thus,  $\pmb{v}_{1}$  is a descent direction of  $\pmb{v} \mapsto \tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}(\pmb{v}; \mathcal{Z}(\pmb{v}_{0}), \mathcal{T}(\pmb{v}_{0}))$  at  $\pmb{v}_{0}$  if and only if it is also a descent direction of  $\pmb{v} \mapsto \mathcal{E}_{\alpha}(\pmb{v})$  at  $\pmb{v}_{0}$ . In particular,  $\pmb{v}_{0}$  minimizes  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}$  if and only if it minimizes  $\tilde{\mathcal{E}}(\pmb{v}; \zeta, \tau)$  for  $\zeta = \mathcal{Z}(\pmb{v}_{0})$  and  $\tau = \mathcal{T}(\pmb{v}_{0})$ . By (50), we conclude that  $\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}$  is a minimizer of  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}$  if and only if

$$
\boldsymbol {v} _ {\alpha} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {g}) = \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\eta_ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \mathcal {T} \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\alpha} ^ {*}\right) \boldsymbol {g} / \sqrt {n}; \mathcal {Z} \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\alpha} ^ {*}\right)\right) - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}\right) \text {a l m o s t s u r e l y .} \tag {51}
$$

That is, if and only if  $\tau_{\alpha}^{*} = \mathcal{T}(\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*})$ ,  $\zeta_{\alpha}^{*} = \mathcal{Z}(\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*})$  is a solution to equations (48a) and (48b). Because  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}$  has minimizers, solutions to equations (48a) and (48b) exist.

To complete the proof, we only need to show that the minimizer  $\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}$  of  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}$  is unique. First, we claim  $\mathcal{Z}(\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}) > 0$  for all minimizers  $\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}$ . Assume otherwise that  $\mathcal{Z}(\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}) = 0$  for some minimizer  $\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}$ . Then, by property (51),

$$
\boldsymbol {v} _ {\alpha} ^ {*} = \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} (\eta_ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {v} _ {\alpha} ^ {*}) \boldsymbol {g} / \sqrt {n}; 0) - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}) = - \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}.
$$

Thus, we have  $\mathcal{Z}(\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}) = \left(1 - \frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\mathcal{T}(\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*})}\langle \pmb{g}, - \pmb{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\pmb{\theta}^{*}\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)_{+} = 1$ , a contradiction. We conclude  $\mathcal{Z}(\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}) > 0$  for all minimizers  $\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}$  of  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}$ .

The function  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}$  is strictly convex on  $\mathcal{Z}(\pmb {v}) > 0$  . Indeed, for any  $\pmb {v}\neq \pmb{v}^{\prime}$  , the function

$$
t \mapsto \sqrt {\| (1 - t) \boldsymbol {v} + t \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} = \sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} - 2 t \langle \boldsymbol {v} , \boldsymbol {v} - \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}} + t ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}}
$$

is strictly convex by univariate calculus. Because  $x \mapsto x_{+}^{2}$  is convex and strictly increasing on  $x > 0$ , strict convexity of  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}$  on  $\mathcal{Z}(\pmb{v}) > 0$  follows. Because all minimizers  $\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}$  satisfy  $\mathcal{Z}(\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}) > 0$ , strict convexity on  $\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*} > 0$  implies the minimizer is unique.

To prove Lemma A.2(b), we require the next lemma, which states that the degrees-of-freedom of the estimator cannot be large without also the risk or noise-variance also being large.

LEMMA A.3. For any  $\tau, \zeta, \delta > 0$  and  $\alpha_{\max} / \sqrt{n} \geq \alpha \geq 0$  and if the eigenvalues of  $\pmb{\Sigma}$  are bounded as  $0 < \kappa_{\min} \leq \kappa_j(\pmb{\Sigma}) \leq \kappa_{\max} < \infty$ , then

$$
\kappa_ {\max} \left(\tau \kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}} ^ {1 / 2} + \sqrt {\tau^ {2} \kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}} + \frac {n}{p} \mathsf {R} _ {\alpha} (\tau^ {2} , \zeta)}\right) ^ {2} \geq \frac {n}{p} \frac {\lambda^ {2}}{\zeta^ {2}} \frac {\mathsf {d f} _ {\alpha} (\tau^ {2} , \zeta)}{n} - \frac {\alpha_ {\max} \lambda \kappa_ {\max}}{\zeta}.
$$

We prove Lemma A.3 in at the end of this section.

PROOF OF LEMMA A.2(B). By general properties of proximal operators [4], the Jacobian matrix  $\nabla \widehat{\pmb{y}}_{\alpha}(\pmb{y}^{f},\zeta)$  of  $\widehat{\pmb{y}}_{\alpha}(\cdot ,\zeta)$  is positive-semidefinite. Therefore,  $\mathrm{df}_{\alpha}(\tau_{\alpha}^{*2},\zeta_{\alpha}^{*})\geq 0$  Also,  $\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}\leq 1$  is immediate from Eq. (48b). Further,  $\tau_{\alpha}^{*}\geq \sigma_{\min}$  is immediate from Eq. (48a).

Establishing the bound  $\tau_{\mathrm{max}}^2$ . By Assumption (A1)(d), there exists  $\bar{\theta}^{*}\in \mathbb{R}^{p}$  such that  $n / p\geq \mathcal{G}(\boldsymbol {x},\boldsymbol {\Sigma})^2 +\Delta_{\mathrm{min}}$  for  $\boldsymbol {x} = \mathrm{sign}(\bar{\theta}^{*})$  and  $\| \bar{\theta}^{*} - \theta^{*}\|_{1} / p\leq M / \sqrt{n}$ . Let  $\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}$  be such that  $x_{j}^{\prime} = x_{j}$  for  $x_{j}\neq 0$  and

$$
| \operatorname {s u p p} (\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\prime}) | = \left\lfloor p \left(\omega^ {*}\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\sqrt {\left(n / p - \Delta_ {\min } / 2\right) / \kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}}}\right) \right\rfloor \vee \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {0}
$$

and assume  $p(\omega^{*})^{-1}\big(\sqrt{\Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / (2\kappa_{\mathrm{cond}})}\big) \geq 1$ . In particular,  $\pmb{x}^{\prime} \in \partial \| \bar{\pmb{\theta}}^{*}\|_{1}$ , so that for any  $\pmb{w} \in \mathbb{R}^{p}$  we have  $\| \bar{\pmb{\theta}}^{*} + \pmb{w}\|_{1} - \| \bar{\pmb{\theta}}^{*}\|_{1} \geq \langle \pmb{x}^{\prime},\pmb{w}\rangle$ . Denote  $S^{\prime} = \operatorname{supp}(\pmb{x}^{\prime}) \subset [p]$ . Because  $p(\omega^{*})^{-1}\big(\sqrt{(n / p - \Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / 2) / \kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}}\big) \geq p(\omega^{*})^{-1}\big(\sqrt{\Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / (2\kappa_{\mathrm{cond}})}\big) \geq 1$ , we conclude that  $|S^{\prime}| / p \geq (\omega^{*})^{-1}\big(\sqrt{\Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / (2\kappa_{\mathrm{cond}})}\big) / 2 =: \nu_{\mathrm{min}}$ . If  $\lfloor p(\omega^{*})^{-1}\big(\sqrt{(n / p - \Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / 2) / \kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}}\big)\rfloor < \| \bar{\pmb{\theta}}^{*}\|_{0}$ , then by assumption  $n / p \geq \mathcal{G}(\pmb{x}',\pmb{\Sigma})^{2} + \Delta_{\mathrm{min}}$ . Otherwise, by Lemma 1,  $\mathcal{G}(\pmb{x}',\pmb{\Sigma}) \leq \sqrt{\delta} /2$ . Otherwise, by assumption we have  $n / p \geq \mathcal{G}(\pmb{x}',\pmb{\Sigma})^{2} + \Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / 2$ . In summary, we have

$$
\frac {n}{p} \geq \mathcal {G} (\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {\Sigma}) ^ {2} + \frac {\Delta_ {\mathrm {m i n}}}{2}, \quad \frac {\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {0}}{p} \geq \frac {(\omega^ {*}) ^ {- 1} \big (\sqrt {\Delta_ {\mathrm {m i n}} / (2 \kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}})} \big)}{2}, \quad \mathrm {a n d} \quad \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\prime} \in \partial \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {1}.
$$

We may equivalently write the objective in (49) as a function of  $\boldsymbol{w} \coloneqq \Sigma^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{v}$ . Note that

(52)

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {w}) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \geq \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {1} - \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {1} - 2 \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} - \frac {p \alpha_ {\max}}{2 \sqrt {n}} \\ \geq \sum_ {j \in S ^ {\prime}} \left(x _ {j} ^ {\prime} w _ {j} + \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {S ^ {\prime c}} \| _ {1}\right) - \frac {2 M p}{\sqrt {n}} - \frac {p \alpha_ {\max}}{2 \sqrt {n}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality uses the relation (44). Let  $\tilde{\pmb{g}}\coloneqq \pmb{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\pmb{g}$ . If  $n / p\leq 2$ , let  $\epsilon = \sqrt{n / p}\Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / 16$ , so that  $\mathcal{G}(\pmb{x}',\pmb{\Sigma}) + \epsilon \leq \sqrt{n / p - \Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / 2} +\sqrt{n / p}\Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / 16\leq \sqrt{n / p}(1-$ $\Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / 16)$ . Otherwise, let  $\epsilon = (\sqrt{2} -1) / 2$ , so that  $\mathcal{G}(\pmb{x}',\pmb{\Sigma}) + \epsilon \leq (\sqrt{2} +1) / 2\leq (3 / 4)\sqrt{n / p}$  where we have used that  $\mathcal{G}(\pmb{x}',\pmb{\Sigma})\leq 1$  in the first inequality and  $n / p\geq 2$  in the second inequality. Using Lemma A.1, Eqs. (36) and (37), we have that either

$$
\frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \langle \tilde {\boldsymbol {g}}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}} \leq \left(1 - \frac {\Delta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}{1 6} \wedge \frac {1}{4}\right) \mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\Sigma} ^ {2} ] ^ {1 / 2},
$$

or

$$
\frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left[ \sum_ {j \in S} x _ {j} ^ {\prime} w _ {j} + \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {S ^ {c}} \| _ {1} \right] \geq c \mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {2} ] ^ {1 / 2},
$$

where  $c = \nu_{\mathrm{min}}^{1/2} (\Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / 16 \wedge (\sqrt{2} - 1) / 2) / (\kappa_{\mathrm{max}}^{1/2} (2 + \kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}))$ . Then, Eq. (52) gives

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {E} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) = \frac {1}{2} \left(\sqrt {\mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {2} ] + \sigma^ {2}} - \frac {\langle \tilde {\boldsymbol {g}} , \boldsymbol {w} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) _ {+} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {w}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\} \\ \geq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{2} \left(\frac {\Delta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}{1 6} \wedge \frac {1}{4}\right) ^ {2} \mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {\Sigma} ^ {2} ], \lambda c \| \mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {x} \| _ {\Sigma} ^ {2} ] ^ {1 / 2} \right\} - \frac {2 \lambda M p}{n} - \frac {p \alpha_ {\operatorname* {m a x}} \lambda}{2 n}. \\ \end{array}
$$

As in the proof of Lemma A.2 (see Eq. (50)), let  $\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}$  be the minimizer of  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}$ . Because  $\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}(\pmb{\theta}^{*}) \leq \| \pmb{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}$ , we bound  $\sigma^2 / 2 = \mathcal{E}_0(\mathbf{0}) \geq \mathcal{E}_{\alpha}(\mathbf{0}) \geq \mathcal{E}_{\alpha}(\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*})$ . Combining this bound with the previous display applied at  $\pmb{w} = \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\pmb{v}_{\alpha}^{*}$ , some algebra yields

$$
\frac {\| \pmb {v} _ {\alpha} ^ {*} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}}{n} = \frac {\mathbb {E} [ \| \pmb {w} \| _ {\pmb {\Sigma}} ^ {2} ]}{n} \leq \max \left\{\frac {(\frac {\sigma^ {2}}{2} + \frac {2 \lambda_ {\max} M}{\Delta_ {\min}} + \frac {\alpha_ {\max} \lambda_ {\max}}{2 \Delta_ {\min}})}{\frac {1}{2} (\frac {\Delta_ {\min}}{1 6} \wedge \frac {1}{4}) ^ {2}}, \frac {(\frac {\sigma^ {2}}{2 \lambda_ {\min}} + \frac {2 M}{\Delta_ {\min}} + \frac {\alpha_ {\max}}{2 \Delta_ {\min}}) ^ {2}}{c ^ {2}} \right\}.
$$

Recalling the fixed point equation (48a), we may set  $\tau_{\mathrm{max}}^2$  to be the sum of  $\sigma^2$  and the right-hand side above.

Establishing the bound  $\zeta_{\mathrm{min}}$ . If  $\mathrm{df}_{\alpha}(\tau_{\alpha}^{*2},\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}) / n\leq 1 / 2$ , then by Eq. (47),  $\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}\geq 1 - \mathrm{df}_{\alpha}(\tau_{\alpha}^{*2},\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}) / n\geq 1 / 2$ . Alternatively, if  $\mathrm{df}_{\alpha}(\tau_{\alpha}^{*2},\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}) / n\geq 1 / 2$ , then by Lemma A.3, it is guaranteed that

$$
\kappa_ {\mathrm {m a x}} \tau_ {\mathrm {m a x}} ^ {2} \left(\kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}} ^ {1 / 2} + \sqrt {\kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}} + n / p}\right) ^ {2} \geq \frac {\lambda_ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {2} \Delta_ {\mathrm {m i n}}}{2 \zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {* 2}} - \frac {\alpha_ {\mathrm {m a x}} \lambda_ {\mathrm {m a x}} \kappa_ {\mathrm {m a x}}}{\zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}.
$$

Note that because  $\mathrm{df}_{\alpha}(\tau_{\alpha}^{*2},\zeta_{\alpha}^{*})\leq p$  , in this case we have  $n / p\leq 2$  , so that we may replace  $n / p$  with 2 on the left-hand side. Because the right-hand side of the preceding equation diverges to  $+\infty$  and  $\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}\downarrow 0$  , the inequality implies a lower bound  $\zeta_{\mathrm{min}}$  on  $\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}$  which depends only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  . The proof is complete.

PROOF OF LEMMA A.3. The KKT conditions for the  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso in the fixed-design model (46) are

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {y}} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}, \zeta\right) - \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} = \frac {\tau}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n} \zeta} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\delta}, \tag {53}
$$

where  $\pmb{y}^{f} = \pmb{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\pmb{\theta}^{*} + \tau \pmb{g} / \sqrt{n}$  and  $\delta \in \partial \mathsf{M}_{\alpha}(\eta_{\alpha}(\pmb{y}^{f},\zeta))$ . Therefore,

$$
\| \widehat {\pmb {y}} _ {\alpha} (\pmb {y} ^ {f}, \zeta) - \pmb {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \pmb {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \geq \frac {\lambda^ {2}}{\zeta^ {2} \kappa_ {\mathrm {m a x}}} \frac {\| \pmb {\delta} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{n} - \frac {2 \lambda \tau}{\zeta \kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {1 / 2}} \frac {\| \pmb {g} \| _ {2} \| \pmb {\delta} \| _ {2}}{n}.
$$

Taking expectations and applying Cauchy-Schwartz yields

$$
\mathsf {R} _ {\alpha} (\tau^ {2}, \zeta) \geq \frac {\lambda^ {2}}{\zeta^ {2} \kappa_ {\mathrm {m a x}}} \frac {\mathbb {E} [ \| \pmb {\delta} \| _ {2} ^ {2} ]}{n} - \frac {2 \lambda \tau}{\zeta \sqrt {n / p} \kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {1 / 2}} \frac {\mathbb {E} [ \| \pmb {\delta} \| _ {2} ^ {2} ] ^ {1 / 2}}{\sqrt {n}},
$$

Solving the resulting quadratic equation for  $\frac{\lambda\mathbb{E}[\|\pmb{\delta}\|_2^2]^{1 / 2}}{\zeta\sqrt{n}}$ , we conclude

$$
\frac {\mathbb {E} \left[ \| \nabla \mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\eta_ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f} , \zeta\right) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \right.}{\sqrt {n}} \leq \frac {\zeta \kappa_ {\max } ^ {1 / 2}}{\lambda \sqrt {n / p}} \left(\tau \kappa_ {\text {c o n d}} ^ {1 / 2} + \sqrt {\tau^ {2} \kappa_ {\text {c o n d}} + (n / p) \mathrm {R} _ {\alpha} \left(\tau^ {2} , \zeta\right)}\right). \tag {54}
$$

Now we divide the analysis into two cases. First, consider the case  $\alpha > 0$ . Then  $\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}$  is differentiable and  $\delta = \nabla \mathsf{M}_{\alpha}(\eta_{\alpha}(\pmb{y}^{f}, \zeta))$ . We compute

$$
\nabla \mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\pmb {\theta}) = (\pmb {\theta} - \eta_ {\mathrm {s o f t}} (\pmb {\theta}, \alpha)) / \alpha , \quad \nabla^ {2} \mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\pmb {\theta}) = \mathrm {d i a g} ((\mathbf {1} _ {| \theta_ {j} | \leq \alpha}) _ {j}) / \alpha .
$$

Because  $|\theta - \eta_{\mathrm{soft}}(\theta, \alpha)| / \alpha = 1$  for  $|\theta| \geq \alpha$ , we bound

$$
\left\| \nabla \mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \geq | \{j \in [ p ] \mid | \theta_ {j} | \geq \alpha \} |.
$$

The KKT condition (53) are alternatively written

$$
\zeta \Sigma^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {y}} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}, \zeta)) = \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \nabla \mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\eta_ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}, \zeta)).
$$

Differentiating with respect to  $y^{f}$ ,

$$
\zeta \Sigma^ {1 / 2} - \zeta \Sigma^ {1 / 2} \nabla \widehat {\boldsymbol {y}} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}; \zeta) = \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \nabla^ {2} \mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\eta_ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}, \zeta)) \Sigma^ {- 1 / 2} \nabla \widehat {\boldsymbol {y}} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}; \zeta).
$$

(More precisely,  $\widehat{\pmb{y}}_{\alpha}(\pmb{y}^{f},\zeta)$  and  $\eta_{\alpha}(\pmb{y}^{f},\zeta)$  are continuous and piecewise linear in  $\pmb{y}^{f}$ , and the above identity holds in the interior of each linear region.) We therefore get

$$
\nabla \widehat {\boldsymbol {y}} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}, \zeta) = \left(\mathbf {I} _ {p} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n} \zeta} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \nabla^ {2} \mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\eta_ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}, \zeta)) \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) ^ {- 1}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \left(\mathbf {I} _ {p} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n} \alpha \zeta} \left(\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) _ {., S ^ {c}} \left(\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) _ {S ^ {c},}\right) ^ {- 1} \\ = \mathbf {I} _ {p} - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n} \alpha \zeta} (\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2}) _ {., S ^ {c}} \left(\mathbf {I} _ {| S ^ {c} |} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n} \alpha \zeta} (\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2}) _ {S ^ {c},.} (\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2}) _ {., S ^ {c}}\right) ^ {- 1} (\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2}) _ {S ^ {c},.}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $S = \{j\in [p]\mid |\eta_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{y}^{f},\zeta)|\geq \alpha \}$ . Thus,

$$
\operatorname {d i v} \widehat {\boldsymbol {y}} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}, \zeta) = \operatorname {T r} (\nabla \widehat {\boldsymbol {y}} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}, \zeta)) \leq p - \frac {| S ^ {c} |}{1 + \sqrt {n} \alpha \zeta \kappa_ {\max } / \lambda} \leq p - \frac {p - \| \nabla \mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} (\eta_ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f} , \zeta)) \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{1 + \alpha_ {\max } \zeta \kappa_ {\max } / \lambda}.
$$

Rearranging and taking expectations,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\mathbb {E} \left[ \| \boldsymbol {\delta} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \right]}{n} \geq \frac {\mathrm {d f} _ {\alpha} \left(\tau^ {2} , \zeta\right)}{n} - \frac {\alpha_ {\max} \zeta \kappa_ {\max}}{\lambda} \left(\frac {p}{n} - \frac {\mathrm {d f} _ {\alpha} \left(\tau^ {2} , \zeta\right)}{n}\right) \tag {55} \\ \geq \frac {\mathrm {d f} _ {\alpha} (\tau^ {2} , \zeta)}{n} - \frac {\alpha_ {\operatorname* {m a x}} \zeta \kappa_ {\operatorname* {m a x}}}{\lambda} \frac {p}{n}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Squaring Eq. (54), chaining it with the previous display, and multiplying by  $\lambda^2 (n / p) / \zeta^2$  gives the result of the lemma in the case  $\alpha >0$ .

Now consider the case  $\alpha = 0$ . In this case,  $\mathsf{M}_0(\pmb {\theta}) = \| \pmb {\theta}\| _1$ , Note that  $\delta \in \partial \| \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^{f}\|_{1}$  then implies that  $\| \pmb {\delta}\| ^2\geq \| \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^f\| _0^2$ . Moreover,  $\nabla \widehat{y}_{\alpha}(\pmb {y}^{f},\zeta)$  exists almost surely with respect to  $\pmb{g}$  by [12, Proposition 5.3], and is equal to (see, for example, [12, Table 1]),

$$
\nabla \widehat {\boldsymbol {y}} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}, \zeta) = (\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {\cdot , S} (\boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {S, S}) ^ {- 1} (\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {\cdot , S},
$$

where  $S = \{j\in [p]\mid \widehat{\theta}_j^f\neq 0\}$ . Thus,  $\operatorname {div}\widehat{\pmb{y}}_{\alpha}(\pmb {y}^{f},\zeta) = \| \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^{f}\|_{0}$  almost surely. Taking expectations, we get Eq. (55), and the result follows as before.

As a consequence, one arrives at the following result.

COROLLARY 15. Under Assumption (A1) and if  $\alpha \leq \alpha_{\mathrm{max}} / \sqrt{n}$ , then  $\mathrm{df}_{\alpha}(\tau_{\alpha}^{*2},\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}) / n$  is uniformly bounded away from one. Namely

$$
\frac {\mathrm {d f} _ {\alpha} \left(\tau_ {\alpha} ^ {* 2} , \zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}\right)}{n} = 1 - \zeta^ {*} \leq 1 - \zeta_ {\min } .
$$

with  $\zeta_{\mathrm{min}}$  depending uniquely on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  and  $\alpha_{\mathrm{max}}$ .

A.5. Continuity of fixed point solutions in smoothing parameter.

LEMMA A.4. If Assumption (A1) holds, then there exist constants  $\alpha_{\mathrm{max}}$ ,  $L_{\tau}$ , and  $L_{\zeta}$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\alpha \leq \alpha_{\mathrm{max}} / \sqrt{n}$ ,

$$
\left| \tau_ {0} ^ {*} - \tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*} \right| \leq L _ {\tau} \sqrt {\sqrt {n} \alpha}, \quad \left| \zeta_ {0} ^ {*} - \zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*} \right| \leq L _ {\zeta} \sqrt {\sqrt {n} \alpha}.
$$

PROOF OF LEMMA A.4. The function

$$
f: L ^ {2} \to \mathbb {R}, \quad \boldsymbol {v} \mapsto \sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} - \frac {\langle \boldsymbol {g} , \boldsymbol {v} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}}}{\sqrt {n}},
$$

is  $(1 + \sqrt{p / n})\leq (1 + \Delta_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1 / 2})$  -Lipschitz. Evaluated at the minimizer  $\pmb{v}_0^*$  of  $\mathcal{E}_0$  defined in (49),  $f(\pmb{v}_0^*)$  is equal to  $\tau_0^*\zeta_0^*\geq \sigma_{\mathrm{min}}\zeta_{\mathrm{min}}$  by the proof of Lemma A.2 in Section A.4. Thus, for  $\| \pmb {v} - \pmb{v}_0^*\|_{L^2}\leq \sigma_{\mathrm{min}}\zeta_{\mathrm{min}} / (2(1 + \Delta_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1 / 2}))$  , it is guaranteed that

$$
f (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq \frac {\sigma_ {\operatorname* {m i n}} \zeta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}{2}.
$$

Let  $r \coloneqq \min \left\{\frac{\sigma_{\min} \zeta_{\min}}{2(1 + \Delta_{\min}^{-1/2})}, \frac{\sigma_{\min}}{2}\right\}$ . By differentiation along affine paths, the function

$$
\frac {1}{2} f (\boldsymbol {v}) _ {+} ^ {2} \text {i s} \frac {\sigma_ {\min } ^ {2} \inf  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in B} f (\boldsymbol {v}) _ {+}}{(\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in B} \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} + \sigma_ {\min } ^ {2}) ^ {3 / 2}} \text {s t r o n g l y c o n v e x o n} \boldsymbol {v} \in B \text {f o r a n y} B \subset L ^ {2}.
$$

Thus,  $\mathcal{E}_0$  is  $a\coloneqq \frac{\sigma^2\sigma_{\min}\zeta_{\min} / 2}{(R^2 + \sigma^2)^{3 / 2}}$  -strongly convex on  $\| \pmb {v} - \pmb {v}_0^*\|_{L^2}\leq r$  , where  $R = \tau_{\mathrm{max}} + r$

By Eq. (44), for any  $\pmb{v} \in L^2$  and  $\alpha \geq 0$ ,  $\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{M}_0(\pmb{\theta}^* + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\pmb{v})] \geq \mathbb{E}[\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}(\pmb{\theta}^* + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\pmb{v})] \geq \mathbb{E}[\mathsf{M}_0(\pmb{\theta}^* + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\pmb{v})] - p\alpha/2$ . Thus,  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}(\pmb{v}_0^*) \leq \mathcal{E}_0(\pmb{v}_0^*)$  and for  $\| \pmb{v} - \pmb{v}_0^*\|_{L^2} \leq r$ ,  $\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}(\pmb{v}) \geq \mathcal{E}_0(\pmb{v}) - p\lambda\alpha/(2\sqrt{n}) \geq \mathcal{E}_0(\pmb{v}_0^*) + a\|\pmb{v} - \pmb{v}_0^*\|_{L^2}^2/2 - p\lambda\alpha/(2\sqrt{n})$ . Thus, if  $\sqrt{\frac{p\lambda_{\max}\alpha}{a\sqrt{n}}} \leq r$ , we have  $\| \pmb{v}_\alpha^* - \pmb{v}_0^*\|_{L^2} \leq \sqrt{\frac{p\lambda_{\max}\alpha}{a\sqrt{n}}}$ . Since, by the proof of Lemma A.2,  $\tau_{\alpha}^{*} = \sqrt{\sigma^2 + \| \pmb{v}_\alpha^*\|_{L^2}^2}$  and  $\zeta_{\alpha}^{*} = (1 - \langle \pmb{g}, \pmb{v}_\alpha^* \rangle_{L^2}/\sqrt{n})$ , we conclude

$$
| \tau_ {0} ^ {*} - \tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*} | \leq \sqrt {\frac {p \lambda_ {\max} \alpha}{a \sqrt {n}}}, | \zeta_ {0} ^ {*} - \zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*} | \leq \sqrt {\frac {p}{n}} \sqrt {\frac {p \lambda_ {\max} \alpha}{a \sqrt {n}}} \mathrm {f o r} \alpha \leq \frac {r ^ {2} a \sqrt {n}}{p \lambda_ {\max}}.
$$

Using that  $n / p \geq \Delta_{\mathrm{min}}$ , we may set  $\alpha_{\mathrm{max}} = r^2 a \Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / \lambda_{\mathrm{max}}$ ,  $L_{\tau} = \sqrt{\lambda_{\mathrm{max}} / (a \Delta_{\mathrm{min}})}$ , and  $L_{\zeta} = \sqrt{\lambda_{\mathrm{max}} / (a \Delta_{\mathrm{min}}^2)}$ .

A.6. The fixed point solutions as a saddle point. A crucial role in our analysis is played by the max-min problem

$$
\max  _ {\beta > 0} \min  _ {\tau \geq \sigma} \psi_ {\alpha} (\tau , \beta), \tag {56}
$$

$$
\psi_ {\alpha} (\tau , \beta) := - \frac {1}{2} \beta^ {2} - \frac {p - n}{2 n} \tau \beta + \frac {\sigma^ {2} \beta}{2 \tau} + \mathbb {E} \min _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \left\{\frac {\beta}{2 \tau} \left\| \boldsymbol {\theta} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} - \frac {\tau}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {g} \right\| _ {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} (\mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}) \right\},
$$

where the expectation is taken over  $g \sim \mathcal{N}(0, \mathbf{I}_p)$ . We establish that Eqs. (48a) and (48b) are first-order conditions for the solution to this max-min problem, and in the non-smoothed  $(\alpha = 0)$  case, Eqs. (9a) and (9b) are first-order conditions for the solution to this max-min problem.

LEMMA A.5. Let  $\tau_{\alpha}^{*}$ ,  $\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}$  be the unique solution to Eqs. (48a) and (48b), and let  $\beta_{\alpha}^{*} = \tau_{\alpha}^{*}\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}$ . Then  $(\tau_{\alpha}^{*},\beta_{\alpha}^{*})$  is a saddle point for the max-min value in Eq. (56). Namely, for all  $\beta >0$ ,  $\tau \geq \sigma$ ,

$$
\psi_ {\alpha} \left(\tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*}, \beta\right) \leq \psi_ {\alpha} \left(\tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*}, \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}\right) \leq \psi_ {\alpha} \left(\tau , \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}\right), \tag {57}
$$

$$
\psi_ {\alpha} \left(\tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*}, \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}\right) = \max  _ {\beta > 0} \min  _ {\tau \geq \sigma} \psi_ {\alpha} (\tau , \beta) = \min  _ {\tau \geq \sigma} \max  _ {\beta > 0} \psi_ {\alpha} (\tau , \beta). \tag {58}
$$

PROOF OF LEMMA A.5. Let us define function

$$
\Xi_ {\alpha} (\tau , \beta) := - \frac {1}{2} \beta^ {2} - \frac {p - n}{2 n} \tau \beta + \frac {\sigma^ {2} \beta}{2 \tau} + \min _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \left\{\frac {\beta}{2 \tau} \left\| \boldsymbol {\theta} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} - \frac {\tau}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {g} \right\| _ {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} (\mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}) \right\},
$$

so that  $\psi_{\alpha}(\tau, \beta) = \mathbb{E}_{\pmb{g}} \Xi_{\alpha}(\tau, \beta)$ . It is easily seen that  $\Xi_{\alpha}$  is convex-concave in  $(\tau, \beta)$  for  $\tau, \beta > 0$  because prior to the minimization over  $\pmb{\theta}$  it is jointly convex in  $(\tau, \pmb{\theta})$  and concave in  $\beta$ . By the envelope theorem [55, Theorem 1],

$$
\frac {\partial \Xi_ {\alpha}}{\partial \beta} = - \beta - \frac {p - n}{2 n} \tau + \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{2 \tau} + \frac {1}{2 \tau} \left\| \eta_ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \tau \boldsymbol {g} / \sqrt {n}, \beta / \tau) - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} - \frac {\tau}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {g} \right\| _ {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {2},
$$

$$
\frac {\partial \Xi_ {\alpha}}{\partial \tau} = - \frac {p - n}{2 n} \beta - \frac {\sigma^ {2} \beta}{2 \tau^ {2}} - \frac {\beta}{2 \tau^ {2}} \| \eta_ {\alpha} (\pmb {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \pmb {\theta} ^ {*} + \tau \pmb {g} / \sqrt {n}, \beta / \tau) - \pmb {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {\pmb {\Sigma}} ^ {2} + \frac {\beta \| \pmb {g} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 n}.
$$

Taking expectations with respect to  $\pmb{g}$ , exchanging expectations and derivatives by dominated convergence, and expanding the square in the first line, we conclude

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\partial \psi_ {\alpha} (\tau , \beta)}{\partial \beta} = - \beta + \frac {\tau}{2} + \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{2 \tau} + \frac {1}{2 \tau} \mathsf {R} _ {\alpha} (\tau^ {2}, \beta / \tau) - \tau \frac {\mathsf {d f} _ {\alpha} (\tau^ {2} , \beta / \tau)}{n} \\ = \tau \left(- \frac {\beta}{\tau} + 1 - \frac {\mathrm {d f} _ {\alpha} (\tau^ {2} , \beta / \tau)}{n}\right) + \frac {1}{2 \tau} \left(- \tau^ {2} + \sigma^ {2} + \mathsf {R} _ {\alpha} (\tau^ {2}, \beta / \tau)\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\frac {\partial \psi_ {\alpha} (\tau , \beta)}{\partial \tau} = \frac {\beta}{2} - \frac {\sigma^ {2} \beta}{2 \tau^ {2}} - \frac {\beta}{2 \tau^ {2}} \mathsf {R} _ {\alpha} (\tau^ {2}, \beta / \tau) = \frac {\beta}{2 \tau^ {2}} (\tau^ {2} - \sigma^ {2} - \mathsf {R} _ {\alpha} (\tau^ {2}, \beta / \tau)).
$$

Thus, if  $(\tau_{\alpha}^{*},\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}) = (\tau_{\alpha}^{*},\beta_{\alpha}^{*} / \tau_{\alpha}^{*})$  solves Eqs. (48a) and (48b), the derivatives in the preceding display are 0. Because  $\psi_{\alpha}(\tau ,\beta)$  is convex-concave in  $(\tau ,\beta)$ , we conclude that, for any  $\tau ,\beta >0$ , Eq. (57) holds. Thus,  $(\tau_{\alpha}^{*},\beta_{\alpha}^{*})$  is a saddle-point of  $\psi_{\alpha}$  (see, e.g., [66, pg. 380]). By [66, Lemma 36.2], the max-min value of (56) is achieved at  $(\tau_{\alpha}^{*},\beta_{\alpha}^{*})$ , and the maximization and minimization may be exchanged as in Eq. (58).

# B. Proofs of main results.

B.1. Control of  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso estimate and proof of Theorem 5 for a fixed  $\lambda$ . The following theorem controls the behavior of the  $\alpha$ -smoothed lasso.

THEOREM B.1. If Assumption (A1) holds and  $\alpha \leq \alpha_{\max} / \sqrt{n}$ , then there exist constants  $C, c, c', \gamma > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  and  $\alpha_{\max}$  such that for any 1-Lipschitz function  $\phi: \mathbb{R}^p \to \mathbb{R}$ , we have for all  $\epsilon < c'$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\exists \boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}, \left| \phi (\boldsymbol {\theta}) - \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}\right) \right] \right| > \epsilon a n d \mathcal {R} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) \leq \min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {R} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) + \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}}.
$$

Theorem 5 for a fixed  $\lambda$  is an immediate corollary of Theorem B.1 (apply Theorem 5 with  $\alpha = 0$ ). Uniformity over  $\lambda$  is achieved in the next section (Section B.2), completing the proof of Theorem 5.

Define the error vectors of the  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso in the random-design model,

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {\alpha} := \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}, \quad \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} := \Sigma^ {1 / 2} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}\right), \tag {59}
$$

where  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha}$  is defined by (45). The error vector  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}_{\alpha}$  is the minimizer of the reparameterized objective

$$
\mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) := \frac {1}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} - \sigma \boldsymbol {z} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right)
$$

(60)

$$
= \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {n}} \left\{\frac {1}{n} \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} - \sigma \boldsymbol {z}\right) - \frac {1}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \right\} =: \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {n}} C _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {u}).
$$

We also define the error vector of the  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso in the fixed-design model

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} := \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\eta_ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*} \boldsymbol {g}, \zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}\right) - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}\right). \tag {61}
$$

We control the behavior of  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso error  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}_{\alpha}$  in the random-design model using Gordon's minimax theorem [74, 56]. Define Gordon's objective by

(62)

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) := \frac {1}{2} \left(\sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) _ {+} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \\ = \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {n}} \left\{- \frac {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} \boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}}{n} + \sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} \frac {\boldsymbol {h} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {u}}{n} - \frac {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 n} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \right\} \\ =: \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {n}} L _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {u}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\pmb{g} \sim \mathsf{N}(\mathbf{0}_p, \mathbf{I}_p)$  and  $h \sim \mathsf{N}(\mathbf{0}_n, \mathbf{I}_n)$  are all independent. Gordon's lemma compares the (possibly constrained) minimization of  $\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}(\pmb{v})$  with the corresponding minimization of  $\mathcal{L}_{\alpha}(\pmb{v})$ .

LEMMA B.2 (Gordon's lemma). The following hold.

(a) Let  $D\subset \mathbb{R}^p$  be a closed set. For all  $t\in \mathbb{R}$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in D} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \leq t\right) \leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in D} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \leq t\right).
$$

(b) Let  $D\subset \mathbb{R}^p$  be a closed, convex set. For all  $t\in \mathbb{R}$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in D} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq t\right) \leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in D} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq t\right).
$$

We prove Lemma B.2 later in this section.

PROOF OF THEOREM B.1. For any set  $D$ , define  $D_{\epsilon} \coloneqq \{\pmb{x} \in \mathbb{R}^p \mid \inf_{\pmb{x}' \in D} \| \pmb{x} - \pmb{x}' \|_2 \geq \epsilon\}$ . Denote  $L_{\alpha}^{*} \coloneqq \psi_{\alpha}(\tau_{\alpha}^{*}, \beta_{\alpha}^{*})$  where  $\tau_{\alpha}^{*}, \beta_{\alpha}^{*}$  are as in Lemma A.5. To control  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}_{\alpha}$  using Gordon's lemma, we show that with high probability the minimal value of  $\mathcal{L}_{\alpha}$  is close to  $L_{\alpha}^{*}$ , and that if  $D$  contains  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}_{\alpha}^{f}$  with high probability, the objective  $\mathcal{L}_{\alpha}$  is uniformly sub-optimal on  $D_{\epsilon}$  with high probability. We need the following lemma.

LEMMA B.3. There exist constants  $C, c, c', \gamma > 0$ , depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  and  $\alpha_{\max}$  such that for  $\epsilon \in (0, c')$ , we have

$$
\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {B} _ {2} ^ {c} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}; \epsilon / 2\right)} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) > L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + 2 \gamma \epsilon^ {2}, \quad | \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) - L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} | \leq \gamma \epsilon^ {2}, \tag {63}
$$

with probability at least  $1 - \frac{C}{\epsilon^2} \exp(-cn\epsilon^4)$ .

We prove Lemma B.3 at the end of this section.

With  $C, c, c', \gamma > 0$  as in Lemma B.3, we have for  $\epsilon < c'$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in D _ {\epsilon / 2}} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \leq \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) + \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in D _ {\epsilon / 2}} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \leq \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) + \gamma \epsilon^ {2} \text {a n d} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \leq L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) > L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in D _ {\epsilon / 2}} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \leq L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + 2 \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) > L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right) \\ \leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in D _ {\epsilon / 2}} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \leq L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + 2 \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right) + 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) > L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} \notin D\right) + 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathsf {B} _ {2} ^ {c} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}; \epsilon / 2\right)} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \leq L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + 2 \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right) + 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) > L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right)
$$

(64)

$$
\leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} \notin D\right) + \frac {4 C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}},
$$

where the third-to-last inequality holds by Gordon's Lemma (Lemma B.2); the second to last inequality holds because either  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}_{\alpha}^{f}\notin D$  or  $D_{\epsilon /2}\subset \mathsf{B}_2^c (\widehat{\pmb{v}}_\alpha^f;\epsilon /2)$ ; and the last inequality holds by Lemma B.3.

Define  $\tilde{\phi}(\pmb{v}) \coloneqq \kappa_{\min}^{1/2}\phi\left(\pmb{\theta}^* + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\pmb{v}\right)$  (recall that  $\pmb{\theta}^*$  is deterministic), with  $\phi$  as in the statement of Theorem B.1. Define the set

$$
D := \left\{\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p} \Big | \Big | \tilde {\phi} (\boldsymbol {v}) - \mathbb {E} \big [ \tilde {\phi} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}) \big ] \Big | \leq \frac {\epsilon}{2} \right\}.
$$

By Eq. (61) and recalling that  $\beta_{\alpha}^{*} = \zeta_{\alpha}^{*}\tau_{\alpha}^{*}$ , we have

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} = \arg \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \left\{\frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{2 \tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*}} \left\| \boldsymbol {v} - \frac {\tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {1} \right\}. \tag {65}
$$

Thus,  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}_{\alpha}^{f}$  as a function of  $\tau_{\alpha}^{*}\pmb {g} / \sqrt{n}$  is a proximal operator, whence  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}_{\alpha}^{f}$  is a  $\tau_{\alpha}^{*} / \sqrt{n}$ -Lipschitz function of  $\pmb{g}$  [62, pg. 131]. Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions [17, Theorem 5.6] guarantees that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} \notin D\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {n \epsilon^ {2}}{8 \tau_ {\alpha} ^ {* 2}}\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {n \epsilon^ {2}}{8 \tau_ {\max} ^ {2} \delta}\right).
$$

Combined with Eq. (64) and appropriately adjusting constants, for  $\epsilon < c'$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in D _ {\epsilon / 2}} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \leq \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {C} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) + \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}}.
$$

Because  $\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}$  is a reparameterization of the  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso objective, the preceding display is equivalent to

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\exists \boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}, \left| \phi (\boldsymbol {\theta}) - \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}\right) \right] \right| > \kappa_ {\min } ^ {- 1 / 2} \epsilon \text {a n d} \mathcal {R} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) \leq \underset {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} {\min } \mathcal {R} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) + \gamma \epsilon^ {2}\right) \leq \frac {4 C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}}.
$$

Theorem B.1 follows by a change of variables.

PROOF OF LEMMA B.2. Because  $\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}(\pmb{\theta}^{*} + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\pmb{v}) \to \infty$  as  $\| \pmb{v} \|_2 \to \infty$ ,

$$
\min_{\boldsymbol {v}\in D}\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v}) = \lim_{R\to \infty}\min_{\substack{\boldsymbol {v}\in D\\ \| \boldsymbol {v}\|_{2}\leq R}}\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v}).
$$

Note that  $\arg \max_{\boldsymbol{u} \in \mathbb{R}^n} C_\alpha(\boldsymbol{v}, \boldsymbol{u}) = X \Sigma^{-1/2} \boldsymbol{v} - \sigma z$  has  $\ell_2$ -norm no larger than  $\|X \Sigma^{-1/2}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \| \boldsymbol{v}\|_2 + \sigma \| \boldsymbol{z}\|_2$ . In particular, for any realization of  $X, z$ , we have for  $R$  sufficiently large that  $\| \boldsymbol{v}\|_2 \leq R$  implies  $\| \arg \max_{\boldsymbol{u} \in \mathbb{R}^n} C_\alpha(\boldsymbol{v}, \boldsymbol{u})\|_2 \leq R^2$ . In particular, for any realization of  $X, z$

$$
\min_{\substack{\boldsymbol {v}\in D\\ \| \boldsymbol {v}\|_{2}\leq R}}\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v}) = \min_{\substack{\boldsymbol {v}\in D\\ \| \boldsymbol {v}\|_{2}\leq R}}\max_{\| \boldsymbol {u}\|_{2}\leq R^{2}}C_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {u})\quad \text{for} R\text{sufficiently large},
$$

where "sufficiently large" can depend on  $X, z$ . Thus, almost surely

$$
\min_{\boldsymbol {v}\in D}\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v}) = \lim_{R\to \infty}\min_{\substack{\boldsymbol {v}\in D\\ \| \boldsymbol {v}\|_{2}\leq R}}\max_{\| \boldsymbol {u}\|_{2}\leq R^{2}}C_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {u}).
$$

An equivalent argument shows that almost surely

$$
\min_{\boldsymbol {v}\in D}\mathcal{L}_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v}) = \lim_{R\to \infty}\min_{\substack{\boldsymbol {v}\in D\\ \| \boldsymbol {v}\|_{2}\leq R}}\max_{\| \boldsymbol {u}\|_{2}\leq R^{2}}L_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {u}).
$$

Because  $\sqrt{n} X\pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2}$  has iid standard Gaussian entries, by Gordon's min-max lemma (see, e.g., [56, Corollary G.1]), for any finite  $R$  and closed  $D$

$$
\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\substack{\boldsymbol {v}\in D\\ \| \boldsymbol {v}\|_{2}\leq R}}\max_{\| \boldsymbol {u}\|_{2}\leq R^{2}}C_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {u}) <   t\right)\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\substack{\boldsymbol {v}\in D\\ \| \boldsymbol {v}\|_{2}\leq R}}\max_{\| \boldsymbol {u}\|_{2}\leq R^{2}}L_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {u}) <   t\right),
$$

and if  $D$  is also convex

$$
\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\substack{\boldsymbol {v}\in D\\ \| \boldsymbol {v}\|_{2}\leq R}}\max_{\| \boldsymbol {u}\|_{2}\leq R^{2}}C_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {u}) > t\right)\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\substack{\boldsymbol {v}\in D\\ \| \boldsymbol {v}\|_{2}\leq R}}\max_{\| \boldsymbol {u}\|_{2}\leq R^{2}}L_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {u}) > t\right).
$$

Although [56, Corollary G.1]) states Gordon's lemma with weak inequalities inside the probabilities, strict inequalities follow by applying [56, Corollary G.1]) with  $t' \uparrow$  and  $t' \downarrow t$  in the previous two displays respectively. Taking  $R \to \infty$ , we conclude that the previous two displays hold without norm bounds on for  $R$  sufficiently large  $v$  and  $u$ . The strict inequalities can be made weak by applying the result with  $t' \downarrow t$  and  $t' \uparrow t$  respectively.

Proof of Lemma B.3. The proof follows almost exactly the proof of Theorem B.1 in the supplementary material of [56]. For convenience and completeness, we walk the reader through the main steps, but omit some steps which can be taken verbatim from [56].

Recall by Lemma A.5 that the max-min value of (56) is achieved at  $\tau_{\alpha}^{*}$ ,  $\beta_{\alpha}^{*}$ . We have  $\beta_{\min} \leq \beta_{\alpha}^{*} \leq \beta_{\max}$ , where  $\beta_{\min} := \sigma_{\min} \zeta_{\min}$  and  $\beta_{\max} := \tau_{\max}$ . Let  $t = \min (\beta_{\min} / 16, \sigma_{\min})$ . Define events

$$
\mathcal {A} _ {1} := \left\{\| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} \leq 2 \sqrt {p} + 2 \sqrt {n}, \left(1 - \frac {\beta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}{8 \tau_ {\operatorname* {m a x}}}\right) \leq \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \leq 2 \right\},
$$

$$
\mathcal {A} _ {2} := \left\{\left| \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \mathbb {E} \left[ \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \right] \right| \leq t ^ {2}, \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}}{\sqrt {n}} \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}}{\sqrt {n}} \right] + t \right\}.
$$

There exist  $r, a > 0$ , depending only on  $\beta_{\mathrm{min}}, \beta_{\mathrm{max}}, \sigma_{\mathrm{min}}, \tau_{\mathrm{max}}$  such that on the event  $\mathcal{A}_1 \cap \mathcal{A}_2$  the objective  $\mathcal{L}_{\alpha}$  is  $a$ -strongly convex on  $\mathsf{B}_2(\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}_\alpha^f; r)$ . This follows verbatim from the proof of Theorem B.1 in the supplementary material of [56] up to the fifth display on pg. 20, except for one small change: because we bound  $\|g\|_2$  by  $2\sqrt{p} + 2\sqrt{n}$  rather than  $2\sqrt{p}$  as they do, the function  $\boldsymbol{v} \mapsto \sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_2^2 + \sigma^2} \| \boldsymbol{h}\|_2 / \sqrt{n} - \boldsymbol{g}^\top \boldsymbol{v} / \sqrt{n}$  is  $2\sqrt{p/n} + 4$ -Lipschitz rather than  $2\sqrt{p/n} + 2$ -Lipschitz. This impacts the proof only by requiring adjusted constants. (We make this change to achieve a probability that decays exponentially in  $n$  when  $n/p \to \infty$ . Note that [56] use a different but completely equivalent normalization to ours).

Let  $R = \tau_{\mathrm{max}} + r$ ,  $\gamma = a / (96\Delta_{\mathrm{min}})$ ,  $c' = \sqrt{ar^2 / (24\gamma)}$ , and  $\epsilon \in (0, c')$ . Define events

$$
\mathcal {A} _ {3} := \left\{\min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} - \gamma \epsilon^ {2} \right\},
$$

$$
\mathcal {A} _ {4} := \left\{\mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}) \leq L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + \gamma \epsilon^ {2} \right\}.
$$

On event  $\mathcal{A}_3\cap \mathcal{A}_4$

$$
\mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}\right) \leq \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) + 2 \gamma \epsilon^ {2} <   \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) + 3 \gamma \epsilon^ {2}.
$$

Because  $3\gamma \epsilon^2 < ar^2 /8$ , the previous display corresponds to (B.7) of the supplementary material of [56]. Thus, by Lemma B.1 of the supplementary material of [56], we have that on  $\bigcap_{i = 1}^{4}\mathcal{A}_{i}$ ,

$$
\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathsf {B} _ {2} ^ {c} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}; \epsilon / 2)} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathsf {B} _ {2} ^ {c} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}; \sqrt {2 4 \gamma \epsilon^ {2} / a})} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) + 3 \gamma \epsilon^ {2}.
$$

and

$$
\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) = \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}).
$$

We conclude that on  $\bigcap_{i=1}^{4} \mathcal{A}_i$ ,

$$
\min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathsf {B} _ {2} ^ {c} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}; \epsilon / 2)} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) + 3 \gamma \epsilon^ {2} = \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) + 3 \gamma \epsilon^ {2} \geq L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + 2 \gamma \epsilon^ {2},
$$

and

$$
L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} + \gamma \epsilon^ {2} \geq \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}) \geq \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) = \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq \sqrt {n} R} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq L _ {\alpha} ^ {*} - \gamma \epsilon^ {2}.
$$

Lemma B.3 follows as soon as we show there exists  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  and  $\alpha_{\mathrm{max}}$  such that for  $\epsilon < c'$  we have  $\mathbb{P}(\cap_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{A}_i) \geq 1 - \frac{C}{\epsilon^2}\exp(-cn\epsilon^4)$ .

Now to complete the proof of Lemma B.3, it is only left for us to control the probability of each  $\mathcal{A}_i$  respectively.

Event  $\mathcal{A}_1$  occurs with high probability depending on  $\beta_{\mathrm{min}}, \tau_{\mathrm{max}}, \delta$ . Because  $\pmb{g} \mapsto \| \pmb{g} \|_2$  and  $\pmb{h} \mapsto \| \pmb{h} \|_2$  are Lipschitz functions of standard Gaussian random vectors, there exist  $C, c$  depending only on  $\beta_{\mathrm{min}}, \tau_{\mathrm{max}}, \delta$  such that

$$
\mathbb {P} (\mathcal {A} _ {1}) \geq 1 - C \exp (- c n).
$$

Event  $\mathcal{A}_2$  occurs with high probability depending on  $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}},\beta_{\mathrm{min}},\tau_{\mathrm{max}}$ . The function  $g\mapsto \| \widehat{v}_{\alpha}^{f}\|_{2}$  is  $n^{-1 / 2}\tau_{\mathrm{max}}$ -Lipschitz because  $\widehat{v}_{\alpha}^{f}$  is a proximal operator applied to  $\tau_{\alpha}^{*}g / \sqrt{n}$  by Eq. (65) [62, pg. 131]. By Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions,  $n^{-1 / 2}\| \widehat{v}_{\alpha}^{f}\|_{2}$  is  $\tau_{\mathrm{max}}^2 /n$ -sub-Gaussian. By the fixed point equations (48a), we bound its expectation  $\mathbb{E}[\| \widehat{v}_{\alpha}^{f}\|_{2}]\leq \mathbb{E}[\| \widehat{v}_{\alpha}^{f}\|^{2}]^{1 / 2}\leq \tau_{\mathrm{max}}$ . Combining its sub-Gaussianity and bounded expectation, we conclude by Proposition G.5 of [56] that

$$
\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \text {i s} (C / n, C / n) \text {- s u b - G a m m a f o r s o m e} C \text {d e p e n d i n g o n l y o n} \tau_ {\max }
$$

Write

$$
\tau_ {\max} \pmb {g} ^ {\top} \widehat {\pmb {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} / \sqrt {n} = (\| \widehat {\pmb {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} - \tau_ {\max} \pmb {g} / \sqrt {n} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \| \widehat {\pmb {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \tau_ {\max} ^ {2} \| \pmb {g} \| _ {2} ^ {2} / n) / 2.
$$

Because  $\pmb{g} \mapsto \widehat{\pmb{v}}_{\alpha}^{f} - \tau_{\max}\pmb{g} / \sqrt{n}$  is  $2\tau_{\max} / \sqrt{n}$ -Lipschitz, the first term is  $(C / n, C / n)$ -sub-Gamma for some  $C$  depending only on  $\tau_{\max}$ . We conclude

$$
\tau_ {\max } \boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} / \sqrt {n} \text {i s} (C / n, C / n) \text {- s u b - G a m m a f o r s o m e} C \text {d e p e n d i n g o n l y o n} \tau_ {\max }.
$$

By standard bounds on the tails of sub-Gamma random variables, we deduce that there exist  $C, c > 0$  depending only on  $\tau_{\max}$ , such that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left| \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \mathbb {E} \left[ \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \right] \right| > \epsilon\right) \leq C \exp \left(- c n \left(\epsilon^ {2} \vee \epsilon\right)\right),
$$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left| \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}}{\sqrt {n}} - \mathbb {E} \left[ \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}}{\sqrt {n}} \right] \right| > \epsilon\right) \leq C \exp \left(- c n \left(\epsilon^ {2} \vee \epsilon\right)\right). \tag {66}
$$

Because  $t$  depends only on  $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}, \beta_{\mathrm{min}}$ , there exists  $C, c > 0$  depending only on  $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}, \beta_{\mathrm{min}}, \tau_{\mathrm{max}}$  such that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {2}\right) \geq 1 - C \exp (- c n).
$$

Event  $\mathcal{A}_3$  occurs with high probability depending on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ . Our control on the probability of  $\mathcal{A}_3$  closely follows the proof of Proposition B.2 in the supplementary material of [56]. Consider for any  $\epsilon > 0$  the event

$$
\mathcal {A} _ {3} ^ {(1)} := \left\{\left| \frac {\| h \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} - 1 \right| \leq \epsilon \right\}. \tag {67}
$$

By Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions,  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_3^{(1)})\geq Ce^{-cn\epsilon^2}$  for all  $\epsilon \geq 0$

By maximizing over over  $\pmb{u}$  for which  $\| \pmb {u}\| /\sqrt{n} = \beta$  in Eq. (62), we compute

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) = \max _ {\beta \geq 0} \left(\sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) \beta - \frac {1}{2} \beta^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} (\mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}) \\ =: \max  _ {\beta \geq 0} \ell_ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}, \beta). \\ \end{array}
$$

Consider the slightly modified objective

$$
\ell_ {\alpha} ^ {0} (\pmb {v}, \beta) := \left(\sqrt {\| \pmb {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} - \frac {\pmb {g} ^ {\top} \pmb {v}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) \beta - \frac {1}{2} \beta^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} (\mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\pmb {\theta} ^ {*} + \pmb {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \pmb {v}) - \| \pmb {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}).
$$

On the event (67), for every  $\| \pmb{v}\| _2\leq R$  and  $\beta \in [0,\beta_{\mathrm{max}}]$

$$
\left| \ell_ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}, \beta) - \ell_ {\alpha} ^ {0} (\boldsymbol {v}, \beta) \right| \leq \beta_ {\max } \left(R ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \epsilon .
$$

Thus, on the event (67),

$$
\min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) = \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \max  _ {\beta \geq 0} \ell_ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}, \beta) \geq \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \ell_ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}, \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*})
$$

$$
\geq \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \ell_ {\alpha} ^ {0} (\boldsymbol {v}, \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}) - \beta_ {\max } \left(R ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \epsilon . \tag {68}
$$

For  $\| \pmb{v}\| _2\leq R$

$$
\sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} = \min _ {\tau \in [ \sigma , \sqrt {\sigma^ {2} + R ^ {2}} ]} \left\{\frac {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}}{2 \tau} + \frac {\tau}{2} \right\}.
$$

Thus, we obtain that

$$
\ell_ {\alpha} ^ {0} (\pmb {v}, \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}) = \min _ {\tau \in [ \sigma , \sqrt {\sigma^ {2} + R ^ {2}} ]} \left\{\left(\frac {\| \pmb {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}}{2 \tau} + \frac {\tau}{2}\right) \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*} - \frac {\pmb {g} ^ {\top} \pmb {v}}{\sqrt {n}} \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*} - \frac {1}{2} \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {* 2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} (\mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\pmb {\theta} ^ {*} + \pmb {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \pmb {v}) - \| \pmb {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}) \right\},
$$

which further implies that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \ell_ {\alpha} ^ {0} (\boldsymbol {v}, \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}) \\ = \min  _ {\tau \in [ \sigma , \sqrt {\sigma^ {2} + R ^ {2}} ]} \left\{\frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{2} \left(\frac {\sigma^ {2}}{\tau} + \tau\right) - \frac {1}{2} \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {* 2} + \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \left\{\frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{2 \tau} \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*} \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}}{\sqrt {n}} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \right\} \right\} \\ =: \min  _ {\tau \in [ \sigma , \sqrt {\sigma^ {2} + R ^ {2}} ]} F (\tau , \boldsymbol {g}). \\ \end{array}
$$

We claim that  $F(\tau, \pmb{g})$  concentrates around its expectation. In order to see this, first note that for every  $\tau \in [\sigma, \sqrt{\sigma^2 + R^2}]$ , the function

$$
\boldsymbol {g} \mapsto \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \left\{\frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{2 \tau} \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*} \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}}{\sqrt {n}} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \right\}
$$

is  $\beta_{\mathrm{max}}R / \sqrt{n}$ -Lipschitz, whence  $\pmb {g}\mapsto F(\tau ,\pmb {g})$  is as well. By Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions [17, Theorem 5.6],

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left| F (\tau , \boldsymbol {g}) - \mathbb {E} [ F (\tau , \boldsymbol {g}) ] \right| > \epsilon\right) \leq 2 e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {2}},
$$

for  $c = 1 / (2\beta_{\mathrm{max}}^2 R^2)$ . Because  $\tau \geq \sigma_{\mathrm{min}} > 0$ , for all  $\pmb{g}$  the function  $\tau \mapsto F(\tau, \pmb{g})$  is  $(\beta_{\mathrm{max}} + \beta_{\mathrm{max}}R^2 / (2\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}^2))$ -Lipschitz on  $[\sigma, \sqrt{\sigma^2 + R^2}]$ , so that by an  $\epsilon$ -net argument, we conclude that for  $C, c$  depending only on  $R, \beta_{\mathrm{max}}, \sigma_{\mathrm{min}}$  that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {3} ^ {(2)}\right) := \mathbb {P} \left(\sup  _ {\tau \in [ \sigma , \sqrt {\sigma^ {2} + R ^ {2}} ]} | F (\tau , \boldsymbol {g}) - \mathbb {E} [ F (\tau , \boldsymbol {g}) ] | \leq \epsilon\right) \geq 1 - \frac {C}{\epsilon} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {2}}.
$$

On  $\mathcal{A}_3^{(2)}$

$$
\min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \ell_ {\alpha} ^ {0} (\boldsymbol {v}, \beta_ {\max }) = \min  _ {\tau \in [ \sigma , \sqrt {\sigma^ {2} + R ^ {2}} ]} F (\tau , \boldsymbol {g}) \geq \min  _ {\tau \in [ \sigma , \sqrt {\sigma^ {2} + R ^ {2}} ]} \mathbb {E} [ F (\tau , \boldsymbol {g}) ] - \epsilon . \tag {69}
$$

We compute

$$
\begin{array}{l} F (\tau , \boldsymbol {g}) = \frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{2} \left(\frac {\sigma^ {2}}{\tau} + \tau\right) - \frac {1}{2} \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {* 2} + \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \left\{\frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{2 \tau} \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*} \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}}{\sqrt {n}} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \right\} \\ = \frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{2} \left(\frac {\sigma^ {2}}{\tau} + \tau\right) - \frac {1}{2} \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {* 2} - \frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*} \tau \| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 n} + \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \left\{\frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{2 \tau} \left\| \boldsymbol {v} - \frac {\tau}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \right\} \\ \geq \frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{2} \left(\frac {\sigma^ {2}}{\tau} + \tau\right) - \frac {1}{2} \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {* 2} - \frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*} \tau \| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 n} + \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \left\{\frac {\beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{2 \tau} \left\| \boldsymbol {v} - \frac {\tau}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\mathrm {M} _ {\alpha} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \right\}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Taking expectations, we have for any  $\tau \geq 0$

$$
\mathbb {E} [ F (\tau , \boldsymbol {g}) ] = \psi_ {\alpha} (\tau , \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}), \tag {70}
$$

where  $\psi_{\alpha}$  is defined in (56).

Combining Eqs. (68), (69), and (70), we conclude that on  $\mathcal{A}_3^{(1)} \cap \mathcal{A}_3^{(2)}$

$$
\min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \mathcal {L} _ {\alpha} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq \psi_ {\alpha} \left(\tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*}, \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}\right) - K \epsilon ,
$$

with  $K = \beta_{\mathrm{max}}\sqrt{R^2 + \sigma^2} +1$ . By a change of variables and applying the probability bounds on  $\mathcal{A}_3^{(1)}$  and  $\mathcal{A}_3^{(2)}$  establishes

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {3}\right) \geq \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {3} ^ {(1)} \cap \mathcal {A} _ {3} ^ {(2)}\right) \geq 1 - \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} \exp \left(- c n \epsilon^ {4}\right),
$$

for some  $C, c$  depending only on  $R$ ,  $\sigma_{\max}$ ,  $\beta_{\max}$ ,  $\sigma_{\min}$ , and  $\gamma$ , and hence only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ .

Event  $\mathcal{A}_4$  occurs with high probability depending on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ . There exist  $C, c > 0$  depending only on  $\kappa_{\min}, \Delta_{\min}, \tau_{\max}$  such that for  $\epsilon > 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\frac {\mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\pmb {\theta} ^ {*} + \pmb {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\pmb {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f})}{\sqrt {n}} - \mathbb {E} \left[ \frac {\mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\pmb {\theta} ^ {*} + \pmb {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\pmb {v}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f})}{\sqrt {n}} \right] > \epsilon\right) \leq C \exp (- c n \epsilon^ {2}),
$$

because  $g \mapsto \mathsf{M}_{\alpha}(\pmb{\theta}^{*} + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\widehat{v}_{\alpha}^{f}) / \sqrt{n}$  is  $\kappa_{\min}^{-1/2}\sqrt{p/n}\tau_{\max}/\sqrt{n}$ -Lipschitz. For any  $x_0 \geq 0$ , note that  $x \mapsto x_{+}^{2}$  is locally Lipschitz in any ball around  $x_0$  with Lipschitz constant and ball radius depending only on an upper bound on  $|x_0|$ . Thus, considering  $x_0 = \beta_{\alpha}^{*}$ , there exists  $L, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\beta_{\max}$  such that for  $\epsilon < c'$ , if  $\mathcal{A}_2$  and  $\mathcal{A}_3^{(1)}$  occur, then

$$
\left| \left(\sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) _ {+} ^ {2} - \beta_ {\alpha} ^ {* 2} \right| \leq L \epsilon .
$$

Using the probability bounds on  $\mathcal{A}_2$  and  $\mathcal{A}_3^{(1)}$  and absorbing  $L$  into constants, we may find  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\gamma, \sigma_{\min}, \kappa_{\min}, \Delta_{\min}, \beta_{\min}, \tau_{\max}$  such that for  $\epsilon < c'$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} (\mathcal {A} _ {4}) \geq 1 - C \exp (- c n \epsilon^ {4}).
$$

Lemma B.3 is established now follows by combining the probability bounds on  $\mathcal{A}_i$  for  $1 \leq i \leq 4$ .

B.2. Uniform control over  $\lambda$ : proof of Theorem 5. In this section, we complete the proof of Theorem 5 by showing that Theorem B.1 holds uniformly over  $\lambda$ , at a very small cost in the rate of concentration.

To make the dependence of the Lasso objective on  $\lambda$  explicit, we write  $\mathcal{R}^{\lambda}(\pmb{\theta})$  for Eq. (1). As before,  $\mathcal{C}^{\lambda}(\pmb{v})$  is a re-parametrization of  $\mathcal{R}^{\lambda}(\pmb{\theta})$ , namely  $\mathcal{C}^{\lambda}(\pmb{v}):= \mathcal{R}^{\lambda}(\pmb{\theta}^{*} + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\pmb{v}) - \lambda \| \pmb{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}/\sqrt{n}$ . We also write  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^{\lambda}$  for the minimizer of  $\mathcal{R}^{\lambda}(\pmb{\theta})$  and  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{\lambda}$  for the minimizer of  $\mathcal{C}^{\lambda}(\pmb{v})$  (in particular  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^{\lambda} = \pmb{\theta}^{*} + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{\lambda}$ ). Finally, in order to expose the full dependency of  $\eta$  on the regularization parameter  $\lambda$ , we redefine

$$
\eta (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f}, \zeta / \lambda) := \arg \min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \left\{\frac {\zeta / \lambda}{2} \| \boldsymbol {y} ^ {f} - \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {1} \right\}.
$$

Throughout this section we will use this definition instead of Eq. (6). We denote the Lasso error vector in the fixed-design model at regularization  $\lambda$  by

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} := \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} (\eta (\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \tau^ {*} \boldsymbol {g} / \sqrt {n}, \zeta^ {*} / \lambda) - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}),
$$

where implicitly  $\tau^{*},\zeta^{*}$  depend on  $\lambda$  via the fixed-point Eqs. (9a) and (9b). For simplicity, we write  $\tilde{\phi} (\pmb {v}) = \phi \bigl (\pmb {\theta}^{*} + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1 / 2}\pmb {v}\bigr)$ . For  $\lambda \in [\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]$ , let

$$
D _ {\epsilon} ^ {\lambda} := \left\{\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p} \left| \left| \tilde {\phi} (\boldsymbol {v}) - \mathbb {E} \left[ \tilde {\phi} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda}\right) \right] \right| > \epsilon \right. \right\}.
$$

Define  $\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}: L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{p};\mathbb{R}^{p}) \to \mathbb{R}$  as  $\mathcal{E}(\boldsymbol{v}) = \mathcal{E}_0(\boldsymbol{v})$ , where  $\mathcal{E}_0$  is as in the proof of Lemma A.4, and we make dependence on  $\lambda$  explicit in the notation. In particular,

$$
\mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda} (\boldsymbol {v}) := \frac {1}{2} \left(\sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} - \frac {\langle \boldsymbol {g} , \boldsymbol {v} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) _ {+} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} (\boldsymbol {g}) \| _ {1} - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\}.
$$

We emphasize that the argument  $\pmb{v}$  is not a vector but a function  $\pmb{v}: \mathbb{R}^p \to \mathbb{R}^p$ . Recall, by the proof of Lemma A.2, that  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{f,\lambda}$ , viewed as a function of  $\pmb{g}$  and thus a member of  $L^2(\mathbb{R}^p; \mathbb{R}^p)$ , is the unique minimizer of  $\mathcal{E}^\lambda$ .

The proof of Theorem 5 relies on two lemmas. The first quantifies the sensitivity of the Lasso problem (1) to the regularization parameter  $\lambda$ . The second quantifies the continuity of the minimizer of the objective function  $\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}$  in the regularization parameter  $\lambda$ .

LEMMA B.4. Under Assumption (A1), there exist constants  $K, C_0, c_0 > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\forall \lambda , \lambda^ {\prime} \in \left[ \lambda_ {\min }, \lambda_ {\max } \right], \mathcal {C} ^ {\lambda^ {\prime}} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda}) \leq \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {C} ^ {\lambda^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {v}) + K | \lambda - \lambda^ {\prime} |\right) \geq 1 - C _ {0} e ^ {- c _ {0} n}.
$$

LEMMA B.5. Under Assumption (A1), there exists constants  $K, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for all  $\lambda, \lambda' \in [\lambda_{\min}, \lambda_{\max}]$  with  $|\lambda - \lambda'| < c'$  we have

$$
f o r a l l \lambda , \lambda^ {\prime} \in \left[ \lambda_ {\min }, \lambda_ {\max } \right], \quad \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda^ {\prime}} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}} \leq K | \lambda^ {\prime} - \lambda | ^ {1 / 2},
$$

where in the previous display we view  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{f,\lambda}$ ,  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{f,\lambda'}$  as functions of the same random vector  $\pmb{g}$  and thus as members of  $L^2 (\mathbb{R}^p;\mathbb{R}^p)$ .

The characterization of the Lasso solution involves only the distribution of  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{f,\lambda}$ . The preceding lemma implicitly constructs a coupling between these distributions defined for different values of  $\lambda$  by using the same source of randomness  $\pmb{g}$  in defining  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{f,\lambda}$  and  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{f,\lambda'}$ . We prove Lemma B.4 and B.5 in Sections B.8.3 and B.8.4 respectively.

To achieve a uniform control over  $\lambda \in [\lambda_{\min}, \lambda_{\max}]$ , we invoke an  $\epsilon$ -net argument. Consider  $\epsilon < c'$ , where  $c'$  is as in Theorem B.1. Let  $C_0, c_0$  be as in Lemma B.4 and Lemma and let  $K_1, K_2$  be the  $K$ 's which appear in Lemma B.4 and Lemma B.5, respectively. Set  $\epsilon' = \min \left\{\gamma \epsilon^2 / K_1, \epsilon \kappa_{\min}^{1/2} / K_2\right\}$ . Define  $\lambda_i = \lambda_{\min} + i\epsilon'$  for  $i = 1, \ldots, k$ ,  $k := \lfloor \frac{\lambda_{\min} - \lambda_{\max}}{\epsilon'} \rfloor$  and  $\lambda_{k+1} = \lambda_{\max}$ .

By a union bound over  $\lambda_{i}$ , Theorem B.1 implies that, for  $C, c, c', \gamma > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ , with probability at least  $1 - \frac{C(k + 1)}{\epsilon^2} \exp(-cn\epsilon^4)$ ,

$$
\forall \boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}, \forall \lambda_ {i}, \quad \mathcal {C} _ {\lambda_ {i}} (\boldsymbol {v}) \leq \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {C} _ {\lambda_ {i}} (\boldsymbol {v}) + \gamma \epsilon^ {2} \Rightarrow \boldsymbol {v} \in \left(D _ {\epsilon} ^ {\lambda_ {i}}\right) ^ {c}. \tag {71}
$$

Further, Lemma B.4 implies that with probability at least  $1 - C_0e^{-c_0n}$ , the following occurs: for all  $\lambda \in [\lambda_{\min}, \lambda_{\max}]$

$$
\mathcal {C} _ {\lambda_ {i}} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda}) \leq \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {C} _ {\lambda_ {i}} (\boldsymbol {v}) + K | \lambda - \lambda_ {i} | \leq \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \mathcal {C} _ {\lambda_ {i}} (\boldsymbol {v}) + \gamma \epsilon^ {2},
$$

where  $i = i(\lambda)$  is chosen such that  $\lambda \in [\lambda_i, \lambda_{i+1}]$  and the inequality holds by the choice of  $\epsilon'$ . Combining with inequality (71), we conclude that

$$
\text {f o r a l l} \lambda , \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \in \left(D _ {\epsilon} ^ {\lambda_ {i}}\right) ^ {c} \text {w h e r e} i = i (\lambda) \text {i s s u c h t h a t} \lambda \in \left[ \lambda_ {i}, \lambda_ {i + 1} \right].
$$

Because  $\phi$  is 1-Lipschitz,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathbb {E} \tilde {\phi} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda}\right) - \mathbb {E} \tilde {\phi} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda_ {i}}\right) \right| \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f} (\lambda_ {i}) \| _ {2}\right) \right] \leq \kappa_ {\min } ^ {- 1 / 2} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda_ {i}} \| _ {L ^ {2}} \\ \leq K \kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {- 1 / 2} | \lambda_ {i} - \lambda | ^ {1 / 2} \leq \epsilon , \\ \end{array}
$$

where the third-to-last inequality holds by Jensen's inequality, and the second-to-last inequality holds by Lemma B.5, and the last inequality holds by the choice of  $\epsilon'$ . Note we have compared the two expectations on the left-hand side by constructing a coupling between the distribution of  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{f,\lambda}$  defined for different values of  $\lambda$ ; see comment following Lemma B.5. By the triangle inequality, if  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{\lambda} \in (D_{\epsilon}^{\lambda_i})^c$ , then  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{\lambda} \in (D_{2\epsilon}^{\lambda})^c$ . Thus, we conclude that with  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$

$$
