\mathbb {P} \left(\exists \lambda \in [ \lambda_ {\min }, \lambda_ {\max } ], \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \in D _ {2 \epsilon} ^ {\lambda}\right) \geq 1 - \frac {C (k + 1)}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}}.
$$

For  $\epsilon < c'$ , we have  $(k + 1) \leq C / \epsilon^2$  for some  $C$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ . Absorbing constants appropriately, the proof of Theorem 5 is complete.

B.3. Control of Lasso residual: proof of Theorem 7. Like the proof of Theorem 5, the proof of Theorem 7 uses Gordon's lemma. Specifically, denote

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {u}} := \boldsymbol {X} \widehat {\boldsymbol {w}} - \sigma \boldsymbol {z} = \boldsymbol {X} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} - \boldsymbol {y},
$$

where  $\widehat{\boldsymbol{w}}\coloneqq \widehat{\boldsymbol{w}}_0 = \widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}} -\boldsymbol{\theta}^*$  as defined in Eq. (59). Then  $\widehat{\pmb{u}}$  is the unique maximizer of

$$
\boldsymbol {u} \mapsto \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \left\{\frac {1}{n} \langle \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {w} - \sigma \boldsymbol {z}, \boldsymbol {u} \rangle - \frac {1}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} + \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \right\},
$$

where the function on the right hand side (before minimizing over  $\mathbf{w}$ ) is defined as  $C_0(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{u})\eqqcolon C(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{u})$  in expression (60) with re-parametrization  $\pmb {v}\coloneqq \pmb{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\pmb{w}$ . Compared with the analysis in Theorem 5 which focuses on the behavior of  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}$ , the focus of this section is the behavior of  $\widehat{\pmb{u}}$ .

Study of the corresponding Gordon's problem. Recall Gordon's optimization problem defined in expression (62) with  $\alpha = 0$  and  $\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}(\pmb {\theta}) = \| \pmb {\theta}\| _1$ . For every  $(\pmb {v},\pmb {u})$ , we have (defining  $L(\pmb {v},\pmb {u}) = L_0(\pmb {v},\pmb {u})$ , cf. Eq (62)):

$$
L (\pmb {v}, \pmb {u}) := - \frac {\| \pmb {u} \| _ {2} \pmb {g} ^ {\top} \pmb {v}}{n} + \sqrt {\| \pmb {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} \frac {\pmb {h} ^ {\top} \pmb {u}}{n} - \frac {\| \pmb {u} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 n} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} (\| \pmb {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \pmb {v} + \pmb {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} - \| \pmb {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}).
$$

Denote  $U(\pmb{u}) = \min_{\pmb{v} \in \mathbb{R}^p} L(\pmb{v}, \pmb{u})$  and  $\widetilde{U}(\pmb{u}) = L(\widehat{\pmb{v}}^f, \pmb{u})$  where  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^f$  is defined in expression (61) with  $\alpha = 0$ , namely

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f} := \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \left[ \eta \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \frac {\tau^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {g}, \frac {\beta^ {*}}{\tau^ {*}}\right) - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \right].
$$

By definition,  $U(\boldsymbol{u}) \leq \widetilde{U}(\boldsymbol{u})$ . From direct calculations, the maximizer of  $\widetilde{U}(\boldsymbol{u})$  is

$$
\frac {\pmb {u}}{\sqrt {n}} = \left(\sqrt {\| \widehat {\pmb {v}} ^ {f} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} \frac {\| \pmb {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} - \frac {\pmb {g} ^ {\top} \widehat {\pmb {v}} ^ {f}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) _ {+} \frac {\pmb {h}}{\| \pmb {h} \| _ {2}}.
$$

Let us define quantity  $\widetilde{\pmb{u}} := \tau^{*}\zeta^{*}\pmb{h}$ . By the concentration of  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^f$  (given by inequality (66)) and the definition of the  $(\tau^{*},\zeta^{*})$  in (9a) and (9b),  $\widetilde{\pmb{u}}$  is  $\epsilon$ -close to the maximizer of  $\widetilde{U}(\pmb{u})$  (in the sense that  $\| \widetilde{\pmb{u}} - \pmb{u}^{*} \|_{2} / \sqrt{n} \leq \epsilon$ ). In particular, Lemma D.1 [56] holds verbatim here.

Define the set

$$
D _ {\epsilon} := \left\{\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p} \mid \left| \phi \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {u}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) - \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi \left(\frac {\tau^ {*} \zeta^ {*} \boldsymbol {h}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) \right] \right| > \epsilon \right\}.
$$

The probability  $\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\pmb{u}}\in D_{\epsilon})$  can be controlled as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {u}} \in D _ {\epsilon}) = \mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in D _ {\epsilon}} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w}} C (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {u}) \geq \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w}} C (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {u})\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} (\max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in D _ {\epsilon}} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v}} C (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {u}) \geq L ^ {*} - \epsilon^ {2}) + \mathbb {P} (\max  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v}} C (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {u}) \leq L ^ {*} - \epsilon) \\ \leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in D _ {\epsilon}} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v}} L (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {u}) \geq L ^ {*} - \epsilon^ {2}\right) + 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {u}} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v}} L (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {u}) \leq L ^ {*} - \epsilon^ {2}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality follows by Gordon's lemma (Lemma B.2). The second term in the last expression is upper bounded  $\frac{C}{\epsilon^2} e^{-cn\epsilon^4}$  using same argument as in Theorem 5, more concretely, in Lemma B.3. The first term is upper bounded as

$$
2\mathbb{P}(\max_{\boldsymbol {u}\in D_{\epsilon}}\min_{\boldsymbol {v}}L(\boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {u})\geq L^{*} - \epsilon^{2}) = 2\mathbb{P}(\max_{\boldsymbol {u}\in D_{\epsilon}}U(\boldsymbol {u})\geq L^{*} - \epsilon^{2})\leq 2\mathbb{P}(\max_{\boldsymbol {u}\in D_{\epsilon}}\widetilde{U} (\boldsymbol {u})\geq L^{*} - \epsilon^{2}).
$$

We control the right-hand side following verbatim from the proof of Theorem D.1 and Lemma D.1 of [56]. Putting the details above together yields  $\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\pmb{u}}\in D_{\epsilon})\leq \frac{C}{\epsilon^2}\exp (-cn\epsilon^4)$ .

B.4. Control of the subgradient. The proof of Theorem 8 is based on controlling the vector

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {t}} = \frac {1}{\sqrt {n} \lambda} \boldsymbol {X} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}}), \tag {72}
$$

which is a subgradient of the  $\ell_1$ -norm at  $\widehat{\theta}$ . Since controlling this subgradient may be of independent interest, we state our result formally below. Similarly, we prove that  $\widehat{t}$  behaves approximately like the corresponding subgradient in the fixed-design model

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {t}} ^ {f} := \frac {\sqrt {n} \zeta^ {*}}{\lambda} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f} - \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {f}\right), \tag {73}
$$

where  $\pmb{y}^{f} = \pmb{\Sigma}^{1/2}\pmb{\theta}^{*} + \frac{\tau^{*}}{\sqrt{n}}\pmb{g}$ ,  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^{f} = \eta(\pmb{y}^{f}, \zeta^{*})$ , and  $\pmb{g} \sim \mathsf{N}(\mathbf{0}, \mathbf{I}_{p})$ . The quality of the approximation is controlled uniformly over models and estimators satisfying Assumption (A1).

For any measurable set  $D \subset \mathbb{R}^p$ , define its  $\epsilon$ -enlargement  $D_{\epsilon} := \{\pmb{x} \in \mathbb{R}^p \mid \inf_{\pmb{x}' \in D} \| \pmb{x} - \pmb{x}' \|_2 \geq \epsilon\}$ . The following result makes the connection between  $\hat{\pmb{t}}$  and  $\hat{\pmb{t}}^f$  precise.

LEMMA 16. Under Assumption (A1), there exist constants  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for any measurable set  $D \subset \mathbb{R}^p$  and for all  $\epsilon < c'$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {t}} \in D _ {\sqrt {n} \epsilon}\right) \leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {t}} ^ {f} \notin D\right) + \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}}. \tag {74}
$$

Consequently, there exist (possibly new) constants  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for any 1-Lipschitz function  $\phi: \mathbb{R}^p \to \mathbb{R}$  and for  $\epsilon < c'$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left| \phi \left(\frac {\widehat {\boldsymbol {t}}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) - \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi \left(\frac {\widehat {\boldsymbol {t}} ^ {f}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) \right] \right| \geq \epsilon\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}}. \tag {75}
$$

The proof of Lemma 16 relies on concentration results established in Lemma B.3. To begin with, define for  $\| t \|_{\infty} \leq 1$

$$
\mathcal {V} (\boldsymbol {t}) := \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \left\{\frac {1}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {w} - \sigma \boldsymbol {z} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {t} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {w}\right) - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\} =: \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} V (\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {t}).
$$

Define, for  $g\sim \mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_p)$ $h\sim \mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_n)$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t}) := \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \left\{\frac {1}{2} \left(\sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) _ {+} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {t} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\} \\ =: \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} T (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {t}). \\ \end{array}
$$

We may compare the maximization of  $\mathcal{V}(\pmb{t})$  with the maximization of  $\mathcal{T}(t)$  using Gordon's lemma.

LEMMA B.6. Let  $D\subset \{\pmb {t}\in \mathbb{R}^p\mid \| \pmb {t}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}$  be a closed set.

(a) For all  $t\in \mathbb{R}$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \mathcal {V} (\boldsymbol {t}) \geq t\right) \leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t}) \geq t\right). \tag {76}
$$

(b) If  $D$  is also convex, then for any  $t \in \mathbb{R}$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \mathcal {V} (\boldsymbol {t}) \leq t\right) \leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t}) \leq t\right). \tag {77}
$$

We prove Lemma B.6 at the end of this section. The maximization of  $\mathcal{T}(t)$  can be controlled because  $\mathcal{T}(t)$  is strongly-concave with high probability. We first establish this strong concavity.

LEMMA B.7. Under Assumption (A1), the objective  $\mathcal{T}(\pmb{t})$  is  $c_0 / n$ -strongly concave on the event

$$
\left\{\frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{n} \leq 2, \frac {\| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{p} \leq 2 \right\},
$$

where  $c_0 > 0$  is a constant depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ .

We prove Lemma B.7 at the end of this section. We are ready to prove Lemma 16.

Consider  $\alpha = 0$ , and let  $\pmb{v}^{*}\in \mathbb{R}^{p}$  be a minimizer of  $\mathcal{L}(\pmb {v})\coloneqq \mathcal{L}_{\alpha = 0}(\pmb {v})$  defined in (62). Let

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {t} ^ {*} := - \frac {\sqrt {n}}{\lambda} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \nabla \left(\boldsymbol {v} \mapsto \frac {1}{2} \left(\sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) _ {+} ^ {2}\right) \Bigg | _ {\boldsymbol {v} = \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*}} \\ = - \frac {\sqrt {n}}{\lambda} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) _ {+} \left(\frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2} / \sqrt {n}}{\sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}}} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} - \frac {\boldsymbol {g}}{\sqrt {n}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

By the KKT conditions,  $\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}} \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2} \pmb{t}^* \in \frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}} \partial (\pmb{v} \mapsto \| \pmb{\theta}^* + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2} \pmb{v} \|_1)$  at  $\pmb{v} = \pmb{v}^*$ . With this definition,  $\mathbf{0}_p$  is in the subdifferential with respect to  $\pmb{v}$  of  $T(\pmb{v}, \pmb{t})$  at  $(\pmb{v}^*, \pmb{t}^*)$ . Moreover,  $t_j^* = 1$  whenever  $(\pmb{\theta}^* + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2} \pmb{v}^*)_j > 0$  and  $t_j^* = -1$  whenever  $(\pmb{\theta}^* + \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2} \pmb{v}^*)_j < 0$ , whence  $\pmb{t}^* \in \operatorname{argmax}_{\| \pmb{t} \|_\infty \leq 1} T(\pmb{v}^*, \pmb{t})$ . Because  $T$  is convex-concave, we have

$$
T (\boldsymbol {v} ^ {*}, \boldsymbol {t}) \leq T (\boldsymbol {v} ^ {*}, \boldsymbol {t} ^ {*}) \leq T (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {t} ^ {*}),
$$

for all  $\pmb{v} \in \mathbb{R}^p$ ,  $\| \pmb{t} \|_{\infty} \leq 1$ . Thus,  $(\pmb{v}^{*}, \pmb{t}^{*})$  is a saddle-point and by [66, pg. 380]

$$
\max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {t} \| _ {\infty} \leq 1} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} T (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {t}) = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {t} \| _ {\infty} \leq 1} T (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {t}),
$$

and

$$
\boldsymbol {t} ^ {*} \in \arg \max _ {\| \boldsymbol {t} \| _ {\infty} \leq 1} \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t}).
$$

Fix  $\epsilon > 0$ . Define the events

$$
\mathcal {A} _ {1} := \left\{\widehat {\boldsymbol {t}} ^ {f} \in D \right\}, \quad \mathcal {A} _ {2} := \left\{\frac {\| \boldsymbol {t} ^ {*} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {t}} ^ {f} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \leq \frac {\epsilon}{2} \right\},
$$

$$
\mathcal {A} _ {3} := \left\{\frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{n} \leq 2, \frac {\| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{p} \leq 2 \right\}, \quad \mathcal {A} _ {4} := \left\{| \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t} ^ {*}) - L ^ {*} | \leq \frac {c _ {0}}{1 6} \epsilon^ {2} \right\}.
$$

We claim that on the event  $\bigcap_{a = 1}^{4}\mathcal{A}_{a}$

$$
\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D _ {\epsilon}} \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t}) \leq L ^ {*} - \frac {c _ {0}}{1 6} \epsilon^ {2}. \tag {78}
$$

Indeed, because  $\mathcal{A}_1$  occurs,  $t\in D_{\sqrt{n}\epsilon}$  implies  $\frac{\|t - \widehat{t}^f\|_2}{\sqrt{n}}\geq \epsilon$ . Because  $\mathcal{A}_2$  occurs, also  $\frac{\|t - t^{*}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\geq \frac{\epsilon}{2}$ . Because  $\mathcal{A}_3$  occurs,  $\mathcal{T}(t)$  is  $\frac{c_0}{n}$ -strongly concave by Lemma B.7, whence because  $t^{*}$  maximizes  $\mathcal{T}$

$$
\mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t}) \leq \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t} ^ {*}) - \frac {1}{2} \frac {c _ {0} \epsilon^ {2}}{4}.
$$

Because  $\mathcal{A}_4$  occurs, we conclude Eq. (78).

By Gordon's lemma for the subgradient (Lemma B.6) and because  $D_{\sqrt{p} \epsilon}$  is closed,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D _ {\sqrt {p} \epsilon}} \mathcal {V} (\boldsymbol {t}) \geq L ^ {*} - \frac {c _ {0}}{1 6} \epsilon^ {2}\right) \leq 2 \left(1 - \mathbb {P} \left(\bigcap_ {a = 1} ^ {4} \mathcal {A} _ {a}\right)\right) \leq 2 \sum_ {a = 1} ^ {4} \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {a} ^ {c}\right). \tag {79}
$$

We control the probabilities in the sum one at a time.

Event  $\mathcal{A}_2$  occurs with high probability depending on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ . By Lemma B.3, there exists  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\epsilon \in (0, c')$  we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\| \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f} \| _ {2} > \frac {\epsilon}{2}\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}}.
$$

Indeed, the event in the preceding display occurs when the two conditions in Eq. (63) are met. Also,  $\| \widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^f\| _2^2 +\sigma^2$ $g^{\top}\widehat{v}^{f} / \sqrt{n}$ , and  $\| \pmb {h}\|_{2} / \sqrt{n}$  concentrate on  $\tau^{*2}$ ,  $\tau^{*}(1 - \zeta^{*})$ , and 1 at sub-Gamma or sub-Gaussian rates depending only on  $\tau_{\mathrm{max}}$  (see, e.g., Eq. (66) in the proof of Lemma B.3). Combining this with the previous display and updating constants appropriately, we conclude there exists  $C,c,c^{\prime} > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\epsilon \in (0,c^{\prime})$  we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \left\| \pmb {t} ^ {*} - \frac {1}{\lambda} \pmb {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} (\tau^ {*} - \tau^ {*} (1 - \zeta^ {*})) \left(\frac {\widehat {\pmb {v}} ^ {f}}{\tau^ {*}} - \pmb {g}\right) \right\| _ {2} > \frac {\epsilon}{2}\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}}.
$$

By the definition of  $\widehat{t}^f$  (Eq. (73)) and of  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}$  (Eq. (59)), the preceding display is equivalent to

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {2} ^ {c}\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}}.
$$

Event  $\mathcal{A}_3$  occurs with high probability depending on  $\delta$ . By Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions,  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_3) \leq Ce^{-cn}$  for some  $C, c$  depending only on  $\delta$ .

Event  $\mathcal{A}_4$  occurs with high probability depending on  $\delta$ . Observe that  $\mathcal{T}(\pmb{t}^*) = \mathcal{L}(\pmb{v}^*)$ . Then, by Lemma B.3 there exist constants  $C, c, c' > 0$ , depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\epsilon \in (0, c')$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {4} ^ {c}\right) = \mathbb {P} \left(| \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t} ^ {*}) - L ^ {*} | > \epsilon\right) = \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {t} \| _ {\infty} \leq 1} \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t}) - L ^ {*} \right| > \epsilon\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}}. \tag {80}
$$

Combining the established probability bounds on  $\mathcal{A}_i$ ,  $i = 2,3,4$ , Eq. (79) implies that for all  $\epsilon < c'$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D _ {\sqrt {n} \epsilon}} \mathcal {V} (\boldsymbol {t}) \geq L ^ {*} - \frac {3}{2} \gamma \epsilon\right) \leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {t}} ^ {f} \notin D\right) + \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}} \text {a n d} \mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {t} \| _ {\infty} \leq 1} \mathcal {V} (\boldsymbol {t}) <   L ^ {*} - \gamma \epsilon\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}},
$$

where the second probability bound holds by Eq. (80). Thus,  $\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{\pmb{t}}\in D_{\sqrt{n}\epsilon}\right)\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{\pmb{t}}^f\notin D\right) + \frac{C}{\epsilon^2} e^{-cn\epsilon^4}$ . Using the definition of  $D_{\sqrt{n}\epsilon}$  and a change of variables (which absorbs certain constants into  $c$ ), we conclude that Eq. (74) holds.

To complete the proof of Lemma 16, we prove Eq. (75). Define

$$
D = \left\{\boldsymbol {t} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p} \left| \left| \phi \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {t}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) - \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi \left(\frac {\widehat {\boldsymbol {t}} ^ {f}}{\sqrt {n}}\right) \right] \right| \leq \epsilon \right. \right\}.
$$

By Eq. (73),  $\widehat{\pmb{t}}^f$  is  $\frac{\tau_{\mathrm{max}}\zeta_{\mathrm{max}}\kappa_{\mathrm{max}}^{1 / 2}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}}$ -Lipschitz in  $\pmb{g}$ , whence

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {t}} ^ {f} \notin D\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {3 \gamma n}{c _ {0} \tau_ {\max} \zeta_ {\max}} \epsilon^ {2}\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}},
$$

where the last inequality holds for  $\epsilon < c'$  with  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ . Eq. (75) is then a special case of Eq. (74). The proof of Lemma 16 is complete.

PROOF OF LEMMA B.6. Fix  $R > 0$ . The function  $t \mapsto \min_{\| \boldsymbol{w} \|_2 \leq R} V(\boldsymbol{w}, t)$  is concave and continuous and is defined on a compact set  $D$ . Moreover,  $\min_{\| \boldsymbol{w} \|_2 \leq R} V(\boldsymbol{w}, t)$  is non-increasing in  $R$ . Because the maximum of a non-increasing limit of continuous functions defined on a compact set is equal to the limit of the maxima of these functions,

$$
\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \mathcal {V} (\boldsymbol {t}) = \max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \lim  _ {R \to \infty} \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \leq R} V (\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {t}) = \lim  _ {R \to \infty} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \leq R} V (\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {t}).
$$

We may write

$$
V (\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {t}) := \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} \leq R ^ {\prime}} \check {V} (\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {t}, \boldsymbol {u}),
$$

for any  $R^{\prime} > \| X\|_{\mathrm{op}}\| \pmb {w}\|_{2} + \sigma \| \pmb {z}\|_{2}$  , where

$$
\check {V} (\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {t}, \boldsymbol {u}) = \frac {1}{n} \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {w} - \sigma \boldsymbol {z}) - \frac {1}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {w}) - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}.
$$

Because almost surely  $R^2 > \| \pmb{X} \|_{\mathrm{op}} R + \sigma \| \pmb{z} \|_2$  for sufficiently large  $R$ , we conclude

$$
\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \mathcal {V} (\boldsymbol {t}) = \lim  _ {R \to \infty} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \leq R} \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} \leq R ^ {2}} \tilde {V} (\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {t}, \boldsymbol {u})
$$

$$
= \lim  _ {R \rightarrow \infty} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} \leq R ^ {2}} \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \leq R} \check {V} (\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {t}, \boldsymbol {u}), \tag {81}
$$

almost surely, where we may exchange minimization and maximization because they are taken over compact sets and  $\tilde{V}$  is convex-concave and continuous.

Similarly,

$$
\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t}) = \lim  _ {R \to \infty} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} T (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {t}).
$$

We may write

$$
T(\boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {t}) = \max_{\| \boldsymbol {u}\|_{2}\leq R^{\prime}}\check{T} (\boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {t},\boldsymbol {u}),
$$

for any  $R' > \sqrt{n}$ $\left(\sqrt{\|\pmb{v}\|_2^2 + \sigma^2}\frac{\|\pmb{h}\|_2}{\sqrt{n}} +\frac{\|\pmb{g}\|_2\|\pmb{v}\|_2}{\sqrt{n}}\right)$ , where

$$
\breve {T} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {t}, \boldsymbol {u}) := - \frac {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}}{\sqrt {n}} + \sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} \cdot \frac {\boldsymbol {h} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {u}}{n} - \frac {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 n} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {t} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v}\right) - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}.
$$

Because almost surely  $R^2 > \sqrt{n} \left( \sqrt{\frac{R^2}{+}\sigma^2} \frac{\|\pmb{h}\|_2}{\sqrt{n}} + \frac{\|\pmb{g}\|R}{\sqrt{n}} \right)$  for sufficiently large  $R$ , we conclude

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t}) = \lim  _ {R \to \infty} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} \leq R ^ {2}} \check {T} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {t}, \boldsymbol {u}) \\ = \lim  _ {R \rightarrow \infty} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} \leq R ^ {2}} \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq R} \tilde {T} (\boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {t}, \boldsymbol {u}), \tag {82} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second equality holds by the following argument. For fixed  $t, u$ , the function  $\check{T}(\boldsymbol{v}, t, \boldsymbol{u})$  depends on  $\boldsymbol{v}$  only through  $g^{\top} \boldsymbol{v}$ ,  $t^{\top} \Sigma^{-1/2} \boldsymbol{v}$ , and  $\| \boldsymbol{v} \|_2$ . Moreover,  $\check{T}(\boldsymbol{v}, t, \boldsymbol{u})$  is convex in the triple  $(\boldsymbol{g}^{\top} \boldsymbol{v}, t^{\top} \Sigma^{-1/2} \boldsymbol{v}, \| \boldsymbol{v} \|_2)$  and  $\{(g^{\top} \boldsymbol{v}, t^{\top} \Sigma^{-1/2} \boldsymbol{v}, \| \boldsymbol{v} \|_2) | \| \boldsymbol{v} \|_2 \leq R\}$  is a compact, convex set. Similarly, for fixed  $t, \boldsymbol{v}$ , the function  $\check{T}(\boldsymbol{v}, t, \boldsymbol{u})$  depends on  $\boldsymbol{u}$  only through  $h^{\top} \boldsymbol{u}$ ,  $\| \boldsymbol{u} \|_2$ . Moreover,  $\check{T}(\boldsymbol{v}, t, \boldsymbol{u})$  is convex in the pair  $(\boldsymbol{h}^{\top} \boldsymbol{u}, \| \boldsymbol{u} \|_2)$  and  $\{(h^{\top} \boldsymbol{u}, \| \boldsymbol{u} \|_2) | \| \boldsymbol{u} \|_2 \leq R^2\}$  is a compact, convex set. Thus, the exchange of minimization and maximization in the preceding display is justified.

By Gordon's Lemma (see [74, Theorem 3]), for any finite  $R > 0$  and any  $t \in \mathbb{R}$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} \leq R ^ {2}} \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \leq R} \check {V} (\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {t}, \boldsymbol {u}) > t\right) \leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| _ {2} \leq R ^ {2}} \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \leq R} \check {T} (\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {t}, \boldsymbol {u}) > t\right).
$$

Taking  $R\to \infty$  and using Eqs. (81) and (82), we conclude

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \mathcal {V} (\boldsymbol {t}) > t\right) \leq 2 \mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {\boldsymbol {t} \in D} \mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t}) > t\right).
$$

The strict inequalities become weak by considering  $t' > t$  in place of  $t$  and taking  $t' \to t$ . We conclude Eq. (76). Eq. (77) follows similarly.

PROOF OF LEMMA B.7. Define

$$
f (\boldsymbol {v}) := \sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}} \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} - \frac {\boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v}}{\sqrt {n}}.
$$

The gradient and Hessian of  $f(\pmb{v})$  are

$$
\nabla f (\boldsymbol {v}) = \left(\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {v} - \frac {\boldsymbol {g}}{\sqrt {n}},
$$

$$
\nabla^ {2} f (\boldsymbol {v}) = \left(\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(\mathbf {I} _ {p} - \left(\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {v} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top}\right) \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \preceq \left(\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbf {I} _ {p}.
$$

We bound

$$
\| \nabla f (\boldsymbol {v}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq \frac {2 \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{n} + \frac {2 \| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{n},
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} | f (\boldsymbol {v}) | \| \nabla^ {2} f (\boldsymbol {v}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} + \frac {\| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \\ \leq \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2}}{n}. \\ \end{array}
$$

The Hessian of  $\frac{1}{2} (f(\pmb {x}))_+^2$  is  $[\nabla f(\pmb {x})\nabla f(\pmb {x})^\top +f(\pmb {x})\nabla^2 f(\pmb {x})]\mathbf{1}_{f(\pmb {x})\geq 0}$ , whence on the event appearing in the statement of the lemma,

$$
\| \nabla^ {2} \cdot (f (\boldsymbol {v}) _ {+} ^ {2} / 2) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {2 \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{n} + \frac {2 \| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{n} + \frac {\| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {h} \| _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {g} \| _ {2}}{n} \leq \frac {1}{c _ {0}},
$$

where  $c_0 = (4 + 4 / \Delta_{\mathrm{min}} + 2(1 + \Delta_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1 / 2}))^{-1}$ . That is,  $\pmb{v} \mapsto \frac{1}{2} f(\pmb{v})_+^2$  is  $1 / c_0$ -strongly smooth. Note that

$$
\mathcal {T} (\boldsymbol {t}) = - \tilde {f} ^ {*} (- \boldsymbol {t}) - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1},
$$

where  $\tilde{f}(\tilde{\pmb{v}}) \coloneqq f(\pmb{\Sigma}^{1/2}(\sqrt{n}\tilde{\pmb{v}}/\lambda - \pmb{\theta}^*))^2/2$ , and  $\tilde{f}^*$  is the Fenchel-Legendre conjugate of  $\tilde{f}$ . Because  $f(\pmb{v})_{+}^{2}/2$  is  $1/c_0$ -strongly smooth,  $\tilde{f}$  is  $\frac{n\kappa_{\max}}{c_0\lambda_{\min}^2}$ -strongly smooth. By the duality of strong smoothness and strong convexity [46, Theorem 6], we conclude that  $\mathcal{T}(\pmb{v})$  is  $\frac{c_0\lambda_{\min}^2}{n\kappa_{\max}}$ -strongly concave.

B.5. Control of the Lasso sparsity: proof of Theorem 8. For notational convenience, let us write

$$
\bar {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} := \frac {1}{\kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}}} \boldsymbol {\Sigma}, \quad \bar {\boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {*} := \sqrt {\frac {1}{\kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}}} \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {*}}, \quad \bar {\boldsymbol {\lambda}} := \frac {1}{\kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}}} \boldsymbol {\lambda},
$$

so that by Eqs. (6)

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {f} = \arg \min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \left\{\frac {\zeta^ {*}}{2} \left\| \frac {\tau^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {\theta}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {1} \right\}
$$

(83)

The KKT conditions of this optimization problem are

$$
\bar {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\frac {\bar {\tau} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} + \bar {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {f})\right) \in \frac {\bar {\lambda}}{\sqrt {n} \zeta^ {*}} \partial \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {f} \| _ {1},
$$

whence

(84)  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^f = \eta_{\mathrm{soft}}\left(\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^f +\bar{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\left(\frac{\bar{\tau}^*}{\sqrt{n}}\pmb {g} + \bar{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}(\pmb {\theta}^* -\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^f)\right);\frac{\bar{\lambda}}{\sqrt{n}\zeta^*}\right) = : \eta_{\mathrm{soft}}\left(\breve{\pmb{y}}^f;\frac{\bar{\lambda}}{\sqrt{n}\zeta^*}\right),$

and by Eq. (73)

(85)  $\widehat{\pmb{t}}^f = \frac{\sqrt{n}\zeta^*}{\bar{\lambda}}\left(\breve{\pmb{y}}^f - \eta_{\mathrm{soft}}\left(\breve{\pmb{y}}^f;\frac{\bar{\lambda}}{\sqrt{n}\zeta^*}\right)\right),$

where  $\eta_{\mathrm{soft}}(\cdot ,\alpha)$  applies  $x\mapsto \operatorname {sign}(x)(|x| - \alpha)_+$  coordinates-wise. This representation is useful because the marginals of  $\check{y}^f$  have bounded density, which will allow us to control the expected number of coordinates of  $\widehat{\pmb{t}}^{f}$  which are close to 1.

LEMMA B.8 (Anti-concentration of  $\breve{y}^f$ ). For each  $j$ , the coordinate  $\breve{y}_j^f$  has marginal density with respect to Lebesgue measure bounded above by  $\frac{\sqrt{n}\kappa_{\min}^{1/2}\kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}}{\sqrt{2}\pi\sigma_{\min}}$ .

PROOF OF LEMMA B.8. We compute

(86)

$$
\breve {\boldsymbol {y}} ^ {f} = \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \bar {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\frac {\bar {\tau} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} + (\mathbf {I} _ {p} - \bar {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {- 1}) \bar {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {f})\right) =: \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \bar {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\frac {\bar {\tau} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} + f (\bar {\tau} ^ {*} \boldsymbol {g} / \sqrt {n})\right).
$$

By definition, all eigenvalues of  $\bar{\Sigma}$  are bounded below and above by 1 and  $\kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}$ , respectively, so that all eigenvalues of  $(\mathbf{I}_p - \bar{\Sigma}^{-1})$  are between 0 and  $1 - \kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}^{-1}$ . Because  $\bar{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^f$  is 1-Lipschitz in  $\bar{\tau}^*g/\sqrt{n}$  (by Eq. (83), using [62, pg. 131]), the function  $f$  is  $(1 - \kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}^{-1})$ -Lipschitz.

Let  $\bar{\sigma}_i$  be the  $i^{\mathrm{th}}$  row of  $\bar{\Sigma}^{1/2}$ . Let  $\mathsf{P}_i^\perp$  be the projection operator onto the orthogonal complement of the span of  $\bar{\sigma}_i$ . Then

$$
\breve {y} _ {i} ^ {f} = \theta_ {i} ^ {*} + \bar {\tau} ^ {*} \bar {\pmb {\sigma}} _ {i} ^ {\top} \pmb {g} / \sqrt {n} + \bar {\pmb {\sigma}} _ {i} ^ {\top} f \Big (\bar {\tau} ^ {*} (\bar {\pmb {\sigma}} _ {i} ^ {\top} \pmb {g} / \sqrt {n}) \bar {\pmb {\sigma}} _ {i} / \| \bar {\pmb {\sigma}} _ {i} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \bar {\tau} ^ {*} \mathsf {P} _ {i} ^ {\bot} \pmb {g} / \sqrt {n} \Big).
$$

Consider the function

$$
h (x) := \bar {\tau} ^ {*} x + \bar {\sigma} _ {i} ^ {\top} f \left(\bar {\tau} ^ {*} x \bar {\sigma} _ {i} / \| \bar {\sigma} _ {i} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \bar {\tau} ^ {*} \mathrm {P} _ {i} ^ {\perp} g / \sqrt {n}\right).
$$

Since  $f$  is  $(1 - \kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}^{-1})$ -Lipschitz, for any  $x_1 < x_2$ ,  $x_1, x_2 \in \mathbb{R}$ , we have

$$
h (x _ {2}) - h (x _ {1}) \geq \bar {\tau} ^ {*} \kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}} ^ {- 1} (x _ {2} - x _ {1}). \tag {87}
$$

Because  $\bar{\sigma}_i^\top g / \sqrt{n} \sim \mathsf{N}(0, \| \bar{\sigma}_i\|_2^2 / n)$ , its density is upper bounded by  $\sqrt{n / (2\pi\|\bar{\sigma}_i\|_2^2)}$ . Further, it is independent of  $\mathsf{P}_i^\perp g$ . Thus, the lower bound (87) implies that  $\check{y}_i^f$  has density  $q(y)$  upper bounded by

$$
\sup _ {y} q (y) \leq \frac {\sqrt {n}}{\sqrt {2 \pi} \| \bar {\sigma} _ {i} \| _ {2}} \cdot \frac {1}{\inf _ {y} h ^ {\prime} (y)} \leq \frac {\sqrt {n} \kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {1 / 2} \kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}}}{\sqrt {2 \pi} \sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}}},
$$

where we have used that  $\| \bar{\sigma}_i\| _2$  is no smaller than the minimal singular value of  $\bar{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}$  which is no smaller than 1 by construction, and that  $\bar{\tau}^{*} = \tau^{*} / \kappa_{\mathrm{min}}^{1 / 2}\geq \sigma_{\mathrm{min}} / \kappa_{\mathrm{min}}^{1 / 2}$ .

We are now ready to complete the proof of Theorem 8. We prove high-probability upper and lower bounds on the sparsity separately. The arguments are almost identical, but establishing the upper bound involves analyzing the subgradient  $\widehat{\pmb{t}}$  and establishing the lower bound involves analyzing  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}$ .

Upper bound on sparsity via the subgradient: The lasso sparsity is upper bounded in terms of the lasso subgradient:

$$
\frac {\left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\| _ {0}}{n} \leq \frac {\left| \left\{j \in [ p ] : \left| \widehat {t} _ {j} \right| = 1 \right\} \right|}{n}. \tag {88}
$$

We prove a high-probability upper bound on the right-hand side. Given any  $\Delta \leq 1$ , define  $T(\check{\mathbf{y}}, \Delta) \coloneqq \{j \in [p] \mid |\check{y}_j| \geq \bar{\lambda}(1 + \Delta) / (\sqrt{n}\zeta^*)\}$ . We will control quantity  $T(\check{\mathbf{y}}^f, -\Delta)$  for  $\Delta \leq 1$ . Consider the function

$$
\phi^ {\mathrm {u b}} (\breve {\boldsymbol {y}}, \Delta) := \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} \phi_ {1} ^ {\mathrm {u b}} (\breve {y} _ {j}, \Delta) \text {w h e r e} \phi_ {1} ^ {\mathrm {u b}} (\breve {y}, \Delta) := \min  (1, \sqrt {n} \zeta^ {*} | \breve {y} | / (\bar {\lambda} \Delta) - 1 / \Delta + 2) _ {+}.
$$

The function  $\phi_1^{\mathrm{ub}}$  regarding the first argument equals to 0 on  $[- \bar{\lambda} (1 - 2\Delta) / (\sqrt{n}\zeta^{*}), \bar{\lambda} (1 - 2\Delta) / (\sqrt{n}\zeta^{*})]$ , 1 on  $[- \bar{\lambda} (1 - \Delta) / (\sqrt{n}\zeta^{*}), \bar{\lambda} (1 - \Delta) / (\sqrt{n}\zeta^{*})]^c$ , and linearly interpolates between the function values on these sets everywhere else. Unlike  $\breve{\boldsymbol{y}} \mapsto T(\breve{\boldsymbol{y}}, \Delta)$ , the function  $\phi^{\mathrm{ub}}(\breve{\boldsymbol{y}}, \Delta)$  is  $\frac{\sqrt{p}\zeta^{*}}{\lambda\Delta\sqrt{n}} \leq \frac{\zeta^{*}}{\bar{\lambda}\Delta\sqrt{\Delta_{\min}}}.$  Lipschitz in  $\breve{\boldsymbol{y}}$ . For all  $\breve{\boldsymbol{y}}$ , by definition we have

$$
\frac {| T (\breve {\boldsymbol {y}} , - \Delta) |}{n} \leq \phi^ {\mathrm {u b}} (\breve {\boldsymbol {y}}, \Delta).
$$

(The preceding display justifies the superscript ub, which stands for "upper bound".) Moreover, by Eq. (84)

$$
\phi^ {\mathbf {u b}} (\breve {\boldsymbol {y}} ^ {f}, \Delta) \leq \frac {\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {f} \| _ {0}}{n} + \frac {| \{j \in [ p ] | 1 - \sqrt {n} \zeta^ {*} | \breve {y} _ {j} ^ {f} | / \bar {\lambda} \in [ 0 , 2 \Delta ] \} |}{n},
$$

whence

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \phi^ {\mathbf {u b}} \left(\check {\boldsymbol {y}} ^ {f}, \Delta\right) \right] \leq 1 - \zeta^ {*} + \frac {4 \bar {\lambda} \Delta}{(n / p) \zeta^ {*}} \frac {\kappa_ {\min } ^ {1 / 2} \kappa_ {\operatorname* {c o n d}}}{\sqrt {2 \pi} \sigma_ {\min }} \leq 1 - \zeta^ {*} + \frac {4 \lambda_ {\max } \kappa_ {\operatorname* {c o n d}} \Delta}{\Delta_ {\min } \sigma_ {\min } \zeta_ {\min } \sqrt {2 \pi \kappa_ {\min }}}
$$

where we have applied Lemma B.8. By the definition of  $\check{y}^f$  in Eq. (86) and recalling that  $\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{\theta}^f$  is  $\tau^*/\sqrt{n}$ -Lipschitz in  $\pmb{g}$ , we have that  $\pmb{g} \mapsto \check{y}^f$  is  $\kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}^{1/2}\bar{\tau}^*/\sqrt{n} + \kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}^{1/2}\tau^*/(\sqrt{n}\kappa_{\mathrm{min}}^{1/2}) = 2\kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}^{1/2}\tau_{\max} / (\sqrt{n}\kappa_{\mathrm{min}}^{1/2})$ -Lipschitz in  $\pmb{g}$ . By Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\frac {| T (\breve {\boldsymbol {y}} ^ {f} , - \Delta) |}{n} \geq 1 - \zeta^ {*} + \frac {4 \lambda_ {\max} \kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}} \Delta}{\Delta_ {\min} \sigma_ {\min} \zeta_ {\min} \sqrt {2 \pi \kappa_ {\min}}} + \epsilon\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\phi^ {\mathrm {u b}} (\breve {\boldsymbol {y}} ^ {f}, \Delta) \geq \mathbb {E} [ \phi^ {\mathrm {u b}} (\breve {\boldsymbol {y}} ^ {f}, \Delta) ] + \epsilon\right) \\ \leq \exp \left(- \frac {n \Delta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}} \bar {\lambda} ^ {2}}{2 \zeta^ {* 2}} \Delta^ {2} \cdot \frac {\kappa_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}{4 \kappa_ {\operatorname* {c o n d}} \tau_ {\operatorname* {m a x}} ^ {2}} \epsilon^ {2}\right) \\ \leq \exp \left(- \frac {n \Delta_ {\mathrm {m i n}} \lambda_ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {2}}{8 \kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}} \zeta_ {\mathrm {m a x}} ^ {2} \kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}} \tau_ {\mathrm {m a x}} ^ {2}} \Delta^ {2} \epsilon^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Plugging in  $\epsilon = \Delta$  and absorbing constants appropriately, we conclude there exists  $c_{1}, c_{2} > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\Delta \geq 0$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\frac {\left| T \left(\breve {\boldsymbol {y}} ^ {f} , - \Delta\right) \right|}{n} \geq 1 - \zeta^ {*} + c _ {1} \Delta\right) \leq \exp \left(- c _ {2} n \Delta^ {4}\right).
$$

By Eq. (85), if  $\frac{|T(\check{\mathbf{y}}^f, - \Delta)|}{n} < 1 - \zeta^* + c_1\Delta$ , then for all  $\pmb{t} \in \mathbb{R}^p$  with  $|\{j \in [p] | |t_j| \geq 1\}| / n \geq 1 - \zeta^* + 2c_1\Delta$ ,

$$
\frac {\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {t}} ^ {f} - \boldsymbol {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{n} \geq c _ {1} \Delta^ {3},
$$

because there are at least  $c_{1}\Delta n$  coordinates where  $\widehat{t}^f$  and  $\pmb{t}$  differ by at least  $\Delta$ . Absorbing constants and taking  $D = \{\pmb {t}\in \mathbb{R}^p\mid |\{j\in [p]\mid 1 - |t_j|\leq \Delta \} | / n\leq 1 - \zeta^* +c_1\Delta \}$  in Lemma 16, there exists  $C,c,c^{\prime} > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  and  $\delta$  such that for  $\Delta < c^{\prime}$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\frac {| \{j \in [ p ] | | \widehat {t _ {j}} | \geq 1 \} |}{n} \geq 1 - \zeta^ {*} + 2 \Delta\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- c n \Delta^ {4}\right) + \frac {C}{\Delta^ {3}} \exp \left(- c n \Delta^ {6}\right).
$$

We may absorb the first term into the second at the cost of changing the constants  $C, c, c'$  because the bound applies only to  $\Delta < c'$ . By Eq. (88),  $\mathbb{P}(\|\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}\|_0 / n > 1 - \zeta^* + \Delta) \leq \frac{C}{\Delta^3} e^{-cn\Delta^6}$ . A high probability upper bound on the sparsity of the lasso solution has been established.

# Lower bound on sparsity via the lasso estimate: Define

$$
\phi^ {\mathrm {l b}} (\check {\mathbf {y}}, \Delta) := \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} \phi_ {1} ^ {\mathrm {l b}} (\check {y} _ {j}, \Delta) \text {w h e r e} \phi_ {1} ^ {\mathrm {l b}} (\check {y}, \Delta) := \min  (1, \sqrt {n} \zeta^ {*} | \check {y} | / (\bar {\lambda} \Delta) - 1 / \Delta - 1) _ {+}.
$$

The function  $\phi_{1}^{\mathrm{lb}}$  is 0 on  $[- \bar{\lambda}(1 + \Delta) / \zeta^{*}, \bar{\lambda}(1 + \Delta) / \zeta^{*}]$ , 1 on  $[- \bar{\lambda}(1 + 2\Delta) / (\sqrt{n}\zeta^{*}), \bar{\lambda}(1 + 2\Delta) / (\sqrt{n}\zeta^{*})]^{c}$ , and linearly interpolates between the function values on these sets everywhere else. The function  $\phi_{1}^{\mathrm{lb}}$  is a  $\frac{\sqrt{p}\zeta^{*}}{\lambda\Delta\sqrt{n}} \leq \frac{\zeta^{*}}{\lambda\Delta\sqrt{\Delta_{\min}}}$ -Lipschitz lower bound for  $|T(\breve{\boldsymbol{y}}, \Delta)| / n$ :

$$
\frac {| T (\breve {\boldsymbol {y}} , \Delta) |}{n} \geq \phi^ {\mathrm {l b}} (\breve {\boldsymbol {y}}, \Delta).
$$

Moreover, by Eq. (84)

$$
\phi^ {\mathrm {l b}} (\check {\boldsymbol {y}} ^ {f}, \Delta) \geq \frac {\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {f} \| _ {0}}{n} - \frac {| \left\{j \in [ p ] \mid \sqrt {n} \zeta^ {*} | \check {y} _ {j} ^ {f} | / \bar {\lambda} - 1 \in [ 0 , 2 \Delta ] \right\} |}{n},
$$

whence

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \phi^ {\mathrm {l b}} (\check {\boldsymbol {y}} ^ {f}, \Delta) ] \geq 1 - \zeta^ {*} - \frac {4 \bar {\lambda} \Delta}{(n / p) \zeta^ {*}} \frac {\kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {1 / 2} \kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}}}{\sqrt {2 \pi} \sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}}} \leq 1 - \zeta^ {*} - \frac {4 \lambda_ {\mathrm {m a x}} \kappa_ {\mathrm {c o n d}} \Delta}{\Delta_ {\mathrm {m i n}} \sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} \zeta_ {\mathrm {m i n}} \sqrt {2 \pi \kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}}}},
$$

where we have applied Lemma B.8. Following the same argument used to establish the upper bound, we conclude there exists  $c_{1}, c_{2} > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\Delta \geq 0$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\frac {\left| T \left(\check {\boldsymbol {y}} ^ {f} , \Delta\right) \right|}{n} \leq 1 - \zeta^ {*} - c _ {1} \Delta\right) \leq \exp \left(- c _ {2} n \Delta^ {4}\right). \tag {89}
$$

By Eq. (84), if  $\frac{|T(\check{\mathbf{y}}^f, \Delta)|}{n} > 1 - \zeta^* - c_1\Delta$ , then  $\frac{|\{j \in [p]||\widehat{\theta}_j^f| \geq \bar{\lambda}\Delta / (\sqrt{n}\zeta^*)\}|}{n} > 1 - \zeta^* - c_1\Delta$ . Then for all  $\boldsymbol{\theta} \in \mathbb{R}^p$  with  $\| \boldsymbol{\theta} \|_0 / n \leq 1 - \zeta^* - 2c_1\Delta$ ,

$$
\left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {f} - \boldsymbol {\theta} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \geq c _ {1} \Delta^ {3},
$$

because there are at least  $c_{1}\Delta n$  coordinates where  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^f$  and  $\pmb{\theta}$  differ by at least  $\bar{\lambda}\Delta /(\sqrt{n}\zeta^{*})$ . In particular, taking  $D:=\{\pmb{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\mid\frac{|\{j\in[p]\|\widehat{\theta}_{j}^{f}\|\bar{\lambda}\Delta/(\sqrt{n}\zeta^{*})\}|}{n}>1-\zeta^{*}-c_{1}\Delta\}$  and  $D_{\epsilon}:=\{\pmb{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\mid\inf _{\pmb{x}^{\prime}\in D}\|\pmb{x}-\pmb{x}^{\prime}\|_{2}\geq\epsilon\}$ , we have that  $\{\pmb{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\mid\|\pmb{\theta}\|_{0}/n\leq 1-\zeta^{*}-2c_{1}\Delta\}\subset D_{\epsilon/2}$  for  $\epsilon/2=\sqrt{c_{1}\delta\Delta^{3}}$ . Equation (89) says  $\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^f\notin D)\leq e^{-c_2n\Delta^4}$ . Thus, by the proof of Theorem B.1 in Appendix B.1—in particular, Eq. (64)—we conclude there exists  $C,c,c^{\prime}>0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\Delta<c'$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\frac {\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {0}}{n} \leq 1 - \zeta^ {*} - \Delta\right) \leq \exp \left(- c n \Delta^ {4}\right) + \frac {C}{\Delta^ {3}} \exp \left(- c n \Delta^ {6}\right).
$$

We may absorb the first term into the second at the cost of changing the constants  $C, c, c'$  because the bound applies only to  $\Delta < c'$ . (In applying Eq. (64), recall  $\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^f = \boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2} (\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^f - \boldsymbol{\theta}^*)$ , with the definition of  $D$  modified according to this change of variables). A high probability lower bound on the sparsity of the lasso solution has been established.

Theorem 8 follows by putting together the upper and lower bounds.

B.6. Proof of Theorem 9: estimate of effective noise level. This result is an immediate consequence of the uniform and simultaneous and uniform concentration of  $\widehat{\tau}(\lambda)$  and of  $\|\widehat{\pmb{\theta}} - \pmb{\theta}^{*}\|_{\Sigma}$ . The simultaneous and uniform concentration of  $\|\widehat{\pmb{\theta}} - \pmb{\theta}^{*}\|_{\Sigma}$  was established in Theorem 5, with a stronger rate than that which appears in Theorem 9. The uniform and simultaneous concentration of  $\widehat{\tau}(\lambda)$  follows from the uniform and simultaneous concentration of the Lasso residual and Lasso sparsity. To establish these, we use the same argument as used in Theorem 5 to establish simultaneous concentration: we use the point-wise concentration of the residual and sparsity, as established in Theorems 7 and 8, as well as the fact that these quantities are Lipschitz in  $\lambda$ , as we now explain.

Regarding the residual  $\| y - X\widehat{\theta}\| _2 / \sqrt{n}$ , the required Lipschitz property is established using the same property for  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}$ , which is established in proving Theorem combined with a high-probability operator norm bound  $\mathbb{P}(\| X\|_2/\sqrt{n}\geq C)\leq e^{-cp}$  for appropriately chosen and  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ -dependent  $C,c > 0$ .

Consider now the Lasso sparsity. In the proof of Theorem B.5, we showed that with the probability given in Theorem 8, the subgradient  $\widehat{\pmb{t}}$  was (Euclidean) distance at least  $\sqrt{n}\epsilon^{3/2}$  from the subgradient of any Lasso solution with sparsity  $\|\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}\|_0/n > 1 - \zeta^* + c'\epsilon$ . Moreover, because  $\widehat{\pmb{t}} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{n\lambda}}\pmb{X}^\top(\pmb{y} - \pmb{X}\widehat{\pmb{\theta}})$  and  $\widehat{\pmb{t}}^f = (\sqrt{n}\zeta^*/\lambda)\pmb{\Sigma}^{1/2}(\pmb{y}^f - \pmb{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^f)$ , we have that  $\widehat{\pmb{t}}/\sqrt{n}$  and  $\widehat{\pmb{t}}^f/\sqrt{n}$  satisfies the same continuity properties in  $\lambda$  which are established for  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}$  and  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^f$  in the proof of Theorem 5 (see, in particular, Lemmas B.4 and B.5 and the following

paragraphs). Because we must control the location of the subgradient to within a region of radius  $\epsilon^{3/2}$ , to get the upper bound on the Lasso sparsity uniformly and simultaneously over  $[\lambda_{\min}, \lambda_{\max}]$ , we can consider a mesh of size  $C / (\epsilon^{3/2})^2$ , which incurs an extra factor for  $1/\epsilon^3$  in front of the probability bound in Theorem 8. An equivalent argument applies to the lower bound on the Lasso sparsity: in the proof of Theorem B.5, we showed that with the probability given in the theorem, the Lasso estimate  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}$  was (Euclidean) distance at least  $\epsilon^{3/2}$  from any vector with sparsity  $\| \widehat{\pmb{\theta}} \|_0 / n < 1 - \zeta^* - c' \epsilon$ . Thus, to get a lower bound on the Lasso sparsity uniformly and simultaneously over  $[\lambda_{\min}, \lambda_{\max}]$ , we incur the extra factor of  $1/\epsilon^3$  in front of the probability bound in Theorem 8.

Combining these results gives Theorem 9.

B.7. Control of the debiased Lasso: proofs of Theorem 10 and Corollary 11. We control the debiased Lasso by approximating it with the debiased  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso, which turns out to be easier to study due to the Lipschitz differentiability of the  $\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}$  (cf. (43)). Define the debiased  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso

$$
\widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {\mathrm {d}} := \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {\alpha} + \frac {\pmb {\Sigma} ^ {- 1} \pmb {X} ^ {\top} (\pmb {y} - \pmb {X} \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {\alpha})}{n \zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}.
$$

This definition is analogous to (4) except that  $1 - \|\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}\|_0 / n$  is replaced by the constant  $\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}$ . It is not feasible to calculate  $\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}$  exactly without knowing  $\pmb{\theta}^{*}$ , whence  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{d}}$  cannot be computed either. Rather,  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{d}}$  is a theoretical tool. Define

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f, \mathrm {d}} := \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {y} ^ {f} - \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f}) = \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \frac {\tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {g}
$$

To establish Theorem 10, we first characterize the behavior of  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{d}}$  and second show that  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^{\mathrm{d}}$  is close to  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{d}}$  with high-probability. The next lemma characterizes  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{d}}$ .

LEMMA B.9 (Characterization of the debiased  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso). Let  $\alpha > 0$ . Under assumptions (A1) and  $A2_{\alpha}$ , there exist constants  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for any 1-Lipschitz  $\phi: \mathbb{R}^p \to \mathbb{R}$ , we have for all  $\epsilon < c'$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left| \phi \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {\mathrm {d}}\right) - \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f, \mathrm {d}}\right) \right] \right| > \left(1 + \frac {\lambda_ {\max}}{\kappa_ {\min} ^ {1 / 2} \zeta_ {\min} \sqrt {n} \alpha}\right) \epsilon\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {2}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {4}}.
$$

We leave the proof of Lemma B.9 at the end of this section.

The following lemma will allow us to show that  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}^{\mathrm{d}}$  and  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{d}}$  are close with high probability.

LEMMA B.10 (Closeness of the Lasso and  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso). There exists  $C_1, C, c, \alpha_{\max} > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq C _ {1} \sqrt {n} \alpha , f o r a l l \alpha \leq \alpha_ {\max } / \sqrt {n}\right) \geq 1 - C e ^ {- c n}.
$$

We prove Lemma B.10 at the end of this section. Equipped with these two lemma, we are now ready to prove Theorem 10.

B.7.1. Proof of Theorem 10: characterization of the debiased Lasso. For any  $\alpha > 0$ , direct calculations give (setting  $\zeta^{*} = \zeta_{0}^{*}$ )

(90)

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {\mathrm {d}} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {\mathrm {d}} \| _ {2} \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {X} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}}) \left(\frac {1}{n - \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {0}} - \frac {1}{n \zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}\right) \right\| _ {2} + \left\| (\mathbf {I} _ {p} - \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {X} / (n \zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*})) (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha}) \right\| _ {2} \\ \leq \kappa_ {\min } ^ {- 1 / 2} \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {X} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\sqrt {n}} \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} \hat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\left| \frac {1}{1 - \left\| \hat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\| _ {0} / n} - \frac {1}{\zeta^ {*}} \right| + \left| \frac {1}{\zeta^ {*}} - \frac {1}{\zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}} \right|\right) \\ + \left(1 + \frac {\kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {- 1 / 2} (\| \pmb {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \pmb {X} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} / \sqrt {n}) (\| \pmb {X} \pmb {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} / \sqrt {n}) \| \pmb {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}\right) \| \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} - \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {\alpha} \| _ {2} \\ =: T _ {1} + T _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Bounding  $T_{1}$ . We start with bounding the  $T_{1}$  term in Eq. (90). By [77, Corollary 5.35] and Theorem 7, there exist  $C_1,C,c > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that with probability at least  $1 - Ce^{-cn}$

$$
\kappa_ {\min } ^ {1 / 2} \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {X} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\sqrt {n}} \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \right\| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \leq C _ {1}. \tag {91}
$$

Let  $L_{\tau}$  and  $L_{\zeta}$  be as in Lemma A.4, and let  $\alpha_{\mathrm{max}}$  be the minimum of the corresponding quantities in Lemma A.4 and Lemma B.10. Let  $\alpha_{\mathrm{max}}' = \min \{\alpha_{\mathrm{max}}, \sigma_{\mathrm{min}}^2 / (4L_{\tau}^2), \zeta_{\mathrm{min}}^2 / (4L_{\zeta}^2)\}$ . By Lemma A.4, for all  $\alpha < \alpha_{\mathrm{max}}'$ , one has

$$
\sigma_ {\min } / 2 \leq \tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*} \leq \tau_ {\max } + \sigma_ {\min } / 2, \quad \zeta_ {\min } / 2 \leq \zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*} \leq 1.
$$

For  $\alpha \leq \alpha_{\mathrm{max}}^{\prime}$ , by Lemma A.4

$$
\left| 1 / \zeta^ {*} - 1 / \zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*} \right| \leq \left(4 / \zeta_ {\min } ^ {2}\right) L _ {\zeta} \sqrt {\sqrt {n} \alpha}. \tag {92}
$$

By Theorem 8, there exists  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\alpha < c'/\sqrt{n}$  with probability  $1 - \frac{C}{(\sqrt{n}\alpha)^{3/2}} e^{-cn(\sqrt{n}\alpha)^3}$

$$
\left| 1 / \left(1 - \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {0} / n\right) - 1 / \zeta^ {*} \right| \leq \left(4 / \zeta_ {\min } ^ {2}\right) \sqrt {\sqrt {n} \alpha}. \tag {93}
$$

Combining the Eqs. (91), (92), and (93), we conclude there exists  $C_1, C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\alpha < c'$ , with probability  $1 - \frac{C}{(\sqrt{n\alpha})^{3/2}} e^{-cn(\sqrt{n}\alpha)^3}$  the first term on the right-hand side of Eq. (90) is bounded by  $C_1\sqrt{\sqrt{n}\alpha}$ .

Bounding  $T_{2}$ . We now bound the  $T_{2}$  term of Eq. (90). Because  $\zeta_{\alpha}^{*} \geq \zeta_{\min} / 2$ , by [77, Corollary 5.35] there exist  $C_2, C, c > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that with probability at least  $1 - Ce^{-cn}$

$$
\left(1 + \frac {\kappa_ {\operatorname* {m i n}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {X} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}\right) \leq C _ {2}.
$$

Combining this bound with Lemma B.10, absorbing parameters into constants, and absorbing smaller terms into larger ones, we conclude there exists  $C_1, C, c > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\alpha < \alpha_{\mathrm{max}}'$ , with probability  $1 - Ce^{-cn}$  the second term on the right-hand side of Eq. (90) is bounded by  $C_1\sqrt{\sqrt{n}\alpha}$ .

Combining the high-probability upper bounds on the terms on the right-hand side of Eq. (90), we conclude there exists  $C_1, C, c, \alpha_{\max} > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that

for  $\alpha <  \alpha_{\mathrm{max}} / \sqrt{n}$  (94)

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(| \phi (\widehat {\pmb {\theta}} ^ {\mathrm {d}}) - \phi (\widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {\mathrm {d}}) | > C _ {1} \sqrt {\sqrt {n} \alpha}\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\| \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} ^ {\mathrm {d}} - \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {\mathrm {d}} \| _ {2} > C _ {1} \sqrt {\sqrt {n} \alpha}\right) \leq \frac {C}{(\sqrt {n} \alpha) ^ {3 / 2}} e ^ {- c n (\sqrt {n} \alpha) ^ {3}}.
$$

Further, for  $\alpha <  \alpha_{\mathrm{max}} / \sqrt{n}$  , by Lemma A.4,

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \frac {\tau^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {g}\right) \right] - \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \frac {\tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {g}\right) \right] \right| \leq C _ {1} \sqrt {\sqrt {n} \alpha}. \tag {95}
$$

Taking  $\epsilon = (\sqrt{n}\alpha)^3$  in Lemma B.9 (and assuming  $(\sqrt{n}\alpha)^{3} < c^{\prime}$  for  $c^\prime$  in that lemma),

$$
\left. \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \phi \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {\mathrm {d}}\right) - \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \frac {\tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {g}\right) \right] \right| > C _ {1} (\sqrt {n} \alpha) ^ {2}\right) \leq \frac {C}{(\sqrt {n} \alpha) ^ {6}} e ^ {- c n (\sqrt {n} \alpha) ^ {1 2}}. \right. \tag {96}
$$

Combining Eqs. (94), (95), and (96) and appropriately adjusting constants, we conclude there exists  $C, C', c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\epsilon < c'$

$$
\left. \right. \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \phi \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {\mathrm {d}}\right) - \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \frac {\tau^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {g}\right)\right]\right| > C _ {1} \epsilon\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon^ {3}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon^ {6}}.
$$

We complete the proof of Theorem 10.

B.7.2. Proof of Lemma B.9: characterization of the debiased  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso. By the KKT conditions for the optimization defining the  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso (cf. (45)),  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{d}} = \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha} + \frac{\lambda\Sigma^{-1}\nabla\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}(\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha})}{\sqrt{n}\zeta_{\alpha}^{*}}$ . Since  $\pmb {\theta}\mapsto \nabla \mathsf{M}_{\alpha}(\pmb {\theta})$  is  $1 / \alpha$ -Lipschitz,  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{d}}$  is a  $\left(1 + \frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\kappa_{\min}\zeta_{\min}\sqrt{n}\alpha}\right)$ -Lipschitz function of  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha}$ . Moreover, by the KKT conditions for the optimization defining the  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso in the fixed design model (Eq. (46)),

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} + \frac {\lambda \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1} \nabla \mathsf {M} _ {\alpha} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f})}{\sqrt {n} \zeta_ {\alpha} ^ {*}} = \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\frac {\tau_ {\alpha} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {n}} \boldsymbol {g} - \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*})\right) = \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} ^ {f, \mathrm {d}}.
$$

Because  $\mathcal{R}_{\alpha}(\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\alpha}) \leq \min_{\pmb{\theta} \in \mathbb{R}^{p}} \mathcal{R}_{\alpha}(\pmb{\theta}) + \gamma \epsilon^{2}$  for any  $\gamma, \epsilon > 0$ , Theorem B.1 and the previous display implies the result.

B.7.3. Proof of Lemma B.10: closeness of the Lasso and the  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso. The proof of Lemma B.10 relies on showing that with high-probability, the Lasso objective is strongly convex locally around its minimizer. We then show that because the value of the  $\alpha$ -smoothed Lasso objective is close to that of the Lasso objective pointwise, the minimizers of the two objectives must also be close.

LEMMA B.11 (Local strong convexity of Lasso objective). Assume  $n\zeta^{*} / 8 \geq 1$ . Then there exists  $C, c, c', c_{1} > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that with probability at least  $1 - Ce^{-cn}$  the following occurs: for all  $\pmb{\theta} \in \mathbb{R}^p$  with  $\| \pmb{\theta} - \widehat{\pmb{\theta}} \|_2 \leq c'$ ,

$$
\mathcal {R} (\pmb {\theta}) - \mathcal {R} (\widehat {\pmb {\theta}}) \geq c _ {1} \| \pmb {\theta} - \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} \| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

We prove Lemma B.11 below. By Eq. (44),  $\mathcal{R}(\pmb{\theta}) \geq \mathcal{R}_{\alpha}(\pmb{\theta}) \geq \mathcal{R}(\pmb{\theta}) - \frac{\lambda p\alpha}{2\sqrt{n}}$  for all  $\pmb{\theta} \in \mathbb{R}^p$ . On the event of Lemma B.11, for  $\| \pmb{\theta} - \widehat{\pmb{\theta}} \|_2 \leq c'$

$$
\mathcal {R} _ {\alpha} (\pmb {\theta}) \geq \mathcal {R} (\pmb {\theta}) - \frac {\lambda p \alpha}{2 \sqrt {n}} \geq \mathcal {R} (\widehat {\pmb {\theta}}) + c _ {1} \| \pmb {\theta} - \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \frac {\lambda p \alpha}{2 \sqrt {n}} \geq \mathcal {R} _ {\alpha} (\widehat {\pmb {\theta}}) + \frac {c _ {1}}{p} \| \pmb {\theta} - \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \frac {\lambda p \alpha}{2 \sqrt {n}}.
$$

Since  $\widehat{\theta}_{\alpha}$  minimizes  $\mathcal{R}_{\alpha}(\pmb {\theta})$  , we conclude that for  $\alpha \leq \alpha_{\mathrm{max}} / \sqrt{n}$

$$
\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\alpha} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {2} \leq \sqrt {\lambda_ {\max } \sqrt {n} \alpha / \left(2 c _ {1} \Delta_ {\min }\right)},
$$

provided that also  $\alpha$  is small enough for the right-hand side to be less than  $c'$ . The proof of Lemma B.10 is complete.

B.7.4. Proof of Lemma B.11: local strong convexity of the Lasso objective. We make the observation that with high probability, the Lasso subgradient  $\widehat{\pmb{t}} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{n\lambda}}\pmb{X}^{\top}(\pmb{y} - \pmb{X}\widehat{\pmb{\theta}})$  (cf. (72)), cannot have too many coordinates with magnitude close to 1, even off of the Lasso support. The next lemma makes this precise.

LEMMA B.1. There exists  $C, c, \Delta > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\frac {\left| \left\{j \in [ p ] \mid \left| \widehat {t _ {j}} \right| \geq 1 - \Delta / 2 \right\} \right|}{n} \geq 1 - \zeta^ {*} / 2\right) \leq C e ^ {- c n}. \tag {97}
$$

PROOF OF LEMMA B.1. The proof is as for Theorem 8 with the following minor changes. We apply Eq. (89) with  $\Delta = \zeta^{*} / (4c_{1})$  with  $c_{1}$  as in that equation. By Eq. (85) and with this choice of  $\Delta$ , if  $\frac{|T(\check{y}^f,\Delta)|}{n} < 1 - 3\zeta^{*} / 4$ , then for all  $t\in \mathbb{R}^p$  with  $|\{j\in [p]\mid |t_j|\geq 1-$ $\Delta /2\} | / n\geq 1 - \zeta^{*} / 2$

$$
\frac {\| \widehat {\pmb {t}} ^ {f} - \pmb {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{n} \geq \frac {\Delta^ {2} \zeta^ {*}}{1 6} = \frac {\zeta^ {* 4}}{2 5 6 c _ {1} ^ {2}},
$$

because there are at least  $\zeta^{*}n / 4$  coordinates where  $\widehat{t^f}$  and  $t$  differ by at least  $\Delta /2$ . Absorbing constants and taking  $D = \{\pmb {t}\in \mathbb{R}^p\mid |\{j\in [p]\mid 1 - |t_j|\leq \Delta \} | / n\leq 1 - 3\zeta^{*} / 4\}$  in Lemma 16, there exists  $C,c > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that Eq. (97) holds.

We are now ready to prove Lemma B.11. Define the minimum singular value of  $X$  over a set  $S \subset [p]$  by

$$
\kappa_ {-} (\boldsymbol {X}, S) = \inf  \left\{\| \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} / \sqrt {n} \mid \operatorname {s u p p} (\boldsymbol {w}) \subset S, \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} = 1 \right\},
$$

and the  $s$  sparse singular value by

$$
\kappa_ {-} (\boldsymbol {X}, s) = \min  _ {| S | \leq s} \kappa_ {-} (\boldsymbol {X}, S).
$$

Consider the event

$$
\mathcal {A} := \Bigl \{\kappa_ {-} \bigl (\boldsymbol {X}, n (1 - \zeta^ {*} / 4) \bigr) \geq \kappa_ {\min} ^ {\prime} \Bigr \} \cap \Bigl \{\frac {\| \boldsymbol {X} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\sqrt {n}} \leq C \Bigr \} \cap \Bigl \{\frac {| \{j \in [ p ] | | \widehat {t _ {j}} | \geq 1 - \Delta / 2 \} |}{n} \leq 1 - \zeta^ {*} / 2 \Bigr \}.
$$

(Note that we need not assume that  $(1 - \zeta^{*} / 2)n\leq p$  or  $(1 - \zeta^{*} / 4)n\leq p$  for these definitions or events to make sense).

We aim to show that there exist  $\kappa_{\mathrm{min}}^{\prime}, \Delta, C, c > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that

$$
\mathbb {P} (\mathcal {A}) \geq 1 - C e ^ {- c n}. \tag {98}
$$

The second event in the definition of  $\mathcal{A}$  is controlled by [77, Corollary 5.35] and the third event by Lemma B.1. Now it is sufficient to consider the first event in the definition of  $\mathcal{A}$ .

Case  $n / p > 1 / (1 - \zeta^{*} / 8)$ . Because  $1 / (1 - \zeta^{*} / 8) \geq 1 / (1 - \zeta_{\min} / 8) > 1$ , we have  $\mathbb{P}(\kappa_{-}\bigl (\pmb {X},n(1 - \zeta^{*} / 4)\bigr) \geq \kappa_{\min}^{\prime}) \geq 1 - Ce^{-cn}$  because  $\kappa_{-}(\pmb {X},n(1 - \zeta^{*} / 4)) \geq \kappa_{-}(\pmb {X},p)$  is the minimum singular value of  $X / \sqrt{n}$ , whence we invoke [77, Corollary 5.35].

Case  $n / p \leq 1 / (1 - \zeta^* / 8)$ . Let  $k = \lfloor n(1 - \zeta^* / 4) \rfloor$  and note that  $k < p$  because  $n / \leq p / (1 - \zeta^* / 8)$ . Because  $\kappa_{-}(\mathbf{X}, S') \geq \kappa_{-}(\mathbf{X}, S)$  when  $S' \subset S$ , we have that  $\kappa_{-}(\mathbf{X}, n(1 - \zeta^* / 4)) = \min_{|S| = k} \kappa_{-}(\mathbf{x}, S)$ . By a union bound, for any  $t > 0$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\kappa_ {-} \left(\boldsymbol {X}, n \left(1 - \zeta^ {*} / 4\right)\right) \leq t\right) \leq \sum_ {| S | = k} \mathbb {P} \left(\kappa_ {-} \left(\boldsymbol {X} _ {S}\right) \leq t\right). \tag {99}
$$

The matrix  $\mathbf{X}_S = \widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_S \mathbf{\Sigma}_{S,S}^{1/2}$  where  $\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_S$  has entries distributed i.i.d.  $\mathsf{N}(0,1)$ . Thus, one has

$$
\kappa_ {-} (\pmb {X} _ {S}) \geq \kappa_ {-} (\widetilde {\pmb {X}} _ {S}) \kappa_ {-} (\pmb {\Sigma} _ {S, S} ^ {1 / 2}) \geq \kappa_ {-} (\widetilde {\pmb {X}} _ {S}) \kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {1 / 2}.
$$

Invoking the fact that  $\widetilde{\pmb{X}}_S$  has the same distribution for all  $|S| = k$ , expression (99) implies

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\kappa_ {-} (\boldsymbol {X}, n (1 - \zeta^ {*} / 4)) \leq t\right) \leq \binom {p} {k} \mathbb {P} (\kappa_ {-} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {X}} _ {S}) \leq t / \kappa_ {\min } ^ {1 / 2}),
$$

where the  $S$  appearing on the right-hand side can be any  $S$  with cardinality  $k$ . By Lemma 2.9 of [16],

$$
\mathbb {P} (\kappa_ {-} (\widetilde {\pmb {X}} _ {S}) \leq t / \kappa_ {\min} ^ {1 / 2}) \leq C (n, t / \kappa_ {\min} ^ {1 / 2}) \exp \left(n \psi (k / n, t / \kappa_ {\min} ^ {1 / 2})\right),
$$

where  $C(a, b)$  is a universal polynomial in  $a, b$  and  $\psi(a, b) := \frac{1}{2}[(1 - a)\log b + 1 - a + a\log a - b]$ . (Lemma 2.9 of [16] states a bound on the density of  $\kappa_{-}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_S)$ , but a deviation bound incurs only a factor  $t / \kappa_{\min}^{1/2}$  which we may absorb into the polynomial term). Note also that  $\binom{p}{k} \leq C'(p)\exp(nH(k/p)/(n/p))$ , where  $C'$  is a universal polynomial. We conclude that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\kappa_ {-} (\pmb {X}, n (1 - \zeta^ {*} / 4)) \leq t\right) \leq C (n, p, t / \kappa_ {\min} ^ {1 / 2}) \exp \left(n (H (k / p) / (n / p) + \psi (k / n, t / \kappa_ {\min} ^ {1 / 2}))\right).
$$

Note that  $\psi(a, b) \leq \frac{\zeta^*}{8} \log b$  for all  $a = k/n \leq 1 - \zeta^*/4$  and  $b \in (0,1)$ . Thus, there exists  $c, \kappa_{\min}' > 0$ , depending only on  $\Delta_{\min}, \kappa_{\min}, \zeta_{\min}$ , such that  $H(k/p)/(n/p) + \psi(k/n, \kappa_{\min}'/\kappa_{\min}^{1/2}) < -2c$ . Because  $C(n,p, \kappa_{\min}'/\kappa_{\min}^{1/2})e^{-cn}$  is upper bounded by a constant  $C$  depending only on  $\Delta_{\min}, \kappa_{\min}, c$ , we conclude there exists  $C, c, \kappa_{\min}' > 0$  depending only on  $\Delta_{\min}, \kappa_{\min}^{1/2}, \zeta_{\min}$  such that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\kappa_ {-} (\boldsymbol {X}, n (1 - \zeta^ {*} / 4)) \leq \kappa_ {\min} ^ {\prime}\right) \leq C e ^ {- c n}.
$$

This conclude the proof of the high-probability bound Eq. (98).

The remainder of the argument takes place on the high-probability event  $\mathcal{A}$ . Consider any  $\pmb{\theta} \in \mathbb{R}^p$ . Let  $\{j \in [p] \mid |\widehat{t_j}| \geq 1 - \Delta/2\}$ . We first construct  $S_+ \supset S(\Delta/2)$  such that

$$
(i) \left| S _ {+} \right| \leq n \left(1 - \zeta^ {*} / 4\right) \quad \text {a n d} \quad (i i) \| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {S _ {+} ^ {c}} \| _ {2} \leq \frac {2 \sqrt {2}}{\sqrt {n \zeta^ {*}}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {S (\Delta / 2) ^ {c}} \| _ {1}, \tag {100}
$$

where we adopt the convention that  $\| \pmb{\theta}_{\emptyset}\| _1 = \| \pmb{\theta}_{\emptyset}\| _2 = 0$ . We establish this by considering two cases.

Case 1:  $p \leq n(1 - \zeta^{*} / 4)$ . In this case, let  $S_{+} = [p]$ . Then Eq. (100) holds trivially.

Case 2:  $p > n(1 - \zeta^{*} / 4)$ . Let  $S_{1},\ldots ,S_{k}$  be a partition of  $[p]\setminus S(\Delta /2)$  satisfying the following properties: first,  $|S_i|\geq n\zeta^* /8$  for  $i = 1,\dots ,k - 1$ ; second,  $|S_{1}|\geq \dots \geq |S_{k}|$ ; third,  $|S(\Delta /2)\cup S_1|\leq n(1 - \zeta^* /4)$ ; and fourth,  $|\theta_j|\geq |\theta_{j'}|$  if  $j\in S_i$  and  $j^{\prime}\in S_{i^{\prime}}$  for  $i\leq i^{\prime}$ . This is possible because  $|S(\Delta /2)|\leq n(1 - \zeta^{*} / 2)$  and, because  $n\zeta^{*} / 8\geq 1$ , there exists an integer between  $n(1 - \zeta^{*} / 4)$  an  $n(1 - \zeta^{*} / 8)$ . In this case, let  $S_{+} = S(\Delta /2)\cup S_{1}$ . Condition (i) holds by construction. To verify condition (ii), observe

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \pmb {\theta} _ {S _ {+} ^ {c}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} = \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {k} \| \pmb {\theta} _ {S _ {i}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {i = 2} ^ {k} | S _ {i} | \left(\frac {\| \pmb {\theta} _ {S _ {i - 1}} \| _ {1}}{| S _ {i - 1} |}\right) ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{\min _ {i = 1 , \ldots , k - 1} \{| S _ {i - 1} | \}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {k - 1} \| \pmb {\theta} _ {S _ {i}} \| _ {1} ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {8}{n \zeta^ {*}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {S (\Delta / 2) ^ {c}} \| _ {1} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds because  $|\theta_j| \leq \|\pmb{\theta}_{S_{i-1}}\|_1 / |S_{i-1}|$  for  $j \in S_i$ , the second inequality holds because  $|S_i| \leq |S_{i-1}|$ , and the third inequality holds because  $|S_i| \geq n\zeta^* / 8$  for  $i \leq k-1$ . Thus, Eq. (100) holds in this case as well.

We lower bound the growth of the Lasso objective by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {R} (\pmb {\theta}) - \mathcal {R} (\widehat {\pmb {\theta}}) = \frac {1}{2 n} \| \pmb {X} (\pmb {\theta} - \widehat {\pmb {\theta}}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{n} \langle \pmb {X} ^ {\top} (\pmb {y} - \pmb {X} \widehat {\pmb {\theta}}), \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} - \pmb {\theta} \rangle + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\| \pmb {\theta} \| _ {1} - \| \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} \| _ {1}\right) \\ = \frac {1}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {X} (\boldsymbol {\theta} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\langle \widehat {\boldsymbol {t}}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} - \boldsymbol {\theta} \rangle + \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {1} - \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {1}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

We first make the observation that

$$
\langle \widehat {t}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} - \boldsymbol {\theta} \rangle + \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {1} - \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {1} \geq \frac {\Delta}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {S (\Delta / 2) ^ {c}} \| _ {1}.
$$

Because  $\widehat{\pmb{t}}\in \partial \| \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}\| _1$  and  $|t_j|\leq 1 - \Delta /2$  on  $S(\Delta /2)^c$  so that  $t_j(\widehat{\theta}_j - \theta_j) + |\theta_j| - |\widehat{\theta}_j|\geq 0$  for all  $j$ , and is no smaller than  $\Delta |\theta_j| / 2$  for  $j\in S(\Delta /2)^c$ . Thus, it is guaranteed that

$$
\mathcal {R} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) - \mathcal {R} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}}) \geq \frac {\lambda \Delta}{2 \sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {S (\Delta / 2) ^ {\circ}} \| _ {1} + \frac {1}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {X} (\boldsymbol {\theta} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}}) \| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

Now choose  $S_{+} \subset [p]$  satisfying Eq. (100). Condition (ii) of Eq. (100) implies

$$
\mathcal {R} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) - \mathcal {R} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}}) \geq c _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {S _ {\pm} ^ {c}} \| _ {2}, \tag {101}
$$

where  $c_{1} > 0$  depends on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ . Next we prove that there exists  $c' > 0$  such that for  $\| \pmb{\theta}_{S_{+}} - \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{S_{+}} \|_{2} < c'$ ,

$$
\mathcal {R} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) - \mathcal {R} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}}) \geq c ^ {\prime} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {S _ {+}} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {S _ {+}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \text {h o l d s t r u e o n e v e n t} \mathcal {A}. \tag {102}
$$

In order to see this, if  $\| X_{S_+}(\pmb{\theta}_{S_+} - \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{S_+})\|_2 / 2 \geq \| X_{S_+^c}\pmb{\theta}_{S_+^c}\|_2$ , then

$$
\mathcal {R} (\pmb {\theta}) - \mathcal {R} (\widehat {\pmb {\theta}}) \geq \frac {1}{8 n} \| \pmb {X} _ {S _ {+}} (\pmb {\theta} _ {S _ {+}} - \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {S _ {+}}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \geq \frac {\kappa_ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {\prime 2}}{8} \| \pmb {\theta} _ {S _ {+}} - \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {S _ {+}} \| _ {2} ^ {2},
$$

as a consequence of Eq. (98). Otherwise, if  $\| X_{S_+}(\pmb{\theta}_{S_+} - \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{S_+})\|_2 / 2 < \| X_{S_+^c}\pmb{\theta}_{S_+^c}\|_2$ , then  $\|\pmb{\theta}_{S_+} - \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{S_+}\|_2 \leq \kappa_{\min}^{'-1/2} \|\pmb{X}_{S_+}(\pmb{\theta}_{S_+} - \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{S_+})\|_2 / \sqrt{n} \leq 2\kappa_{\min}^{'-1/2} \|\pmb{X}_{S_+^c}\pmb{\theta}_{S_+^c}\|_2 / \sqrt{n} \leq C \|\pmb{\theta}_{S_+^c}\|_2$ . Thus

$$
\mathcal {R} (\boldsymbol {\theta}) - \mathcal {R} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}}) \geq c _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {S _ {+} ^ {c}} \| _ {2} \geq c _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {S _ {+}} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {S _ {+}} \| _ {2},
$$

where the value of  $c_{1}$  changes between the last inequalities. Combining the previous two displays, we have established inequality (102), where again the value of  $c'$  has changed from the previous displays.

Combined with Eq. (101), we conclude there exists  $c_{1}, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\| \pmb{\theta} - \widehat{\pmb{\theta}} \|_2 \leq c'$ ,

$$
\mathcal {R} (\pmb {\theta}) - \mathcal {R} (\widehat {\pmb {\theta}}) \geq c ^ {\prime} \| \pmb {\theta} - \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} \| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

The proof is completed.

B.7.5. Proof of Corollary 11. To start, let us define

$$
\phi_ {1} (x, \Delta) := \min  \left(1, x / \Delta - z _ {1 - q / 2} / \Delta + 1\right) _ {+}.
$$

The function  $\phi_1(x)$  equals to 0 for  $x \leq z_{1 - q / 2} - \Delta$  and 1 for  $x \geq z_{1 - q / 2}$ , and linearly interpolates between these two regions elsewhere. Therefore, the false-coverage proportion  $\mathsf{FCP} := \frac{1}{p} \sum_{j=1}^{p} \mathbf{1}_{\theta_j^* \notin \mathsf{Cl}_j}$  can be controlled as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathsf {F C P} = \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} \mathbf {1} \left\{| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {d}} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {*} | > \frac {\sum_ {j \mid - j} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| \boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {2}}{n (1 - \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {0} / n)} z _ {1 - q / 2} \right\} \\ \leq \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} \phi_ {1} \left(\frac {\Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} (1 - \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {0} / n) \sqrt {n} | \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {d}} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {*} |}{\| \boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {2} / \sqrt {n}}, \Delta\right) \\ \leq \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} \phi_ {1} \left(\Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} \sqrt {n} | \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {d}} - \theta_ {j} ^ {*} | / \tau^ {*}, \Delta\right) + \frac {1}{\Delta} \left| \frac {1 - \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {0} / n}{\| \boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} \| _ {2} / \sqrt {n}} - \frac {1}{\tau^ {*}} \right| \left(\frac {1}{p} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} \sqrt {n} \Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} | \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {d}} - \theta_ {j} ^ {*} |\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

We bound the terms on the right-hand side respectively.

- By Theorems 7 and Theorem 8, there exist  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\epsilon < c'$ , we have  $\left|\frac{1 - \|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|_0 / n}{\|\boldsymbol{y} - \boldsymbol{X}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|_2 / \sqrt{n}} - \frac{1}{\tau^*}\right| < \epsilon$  with probability at least  $1 - \frac{C}{\epsilon^3} e^{-cn\epsilon^6}$ .  
- Because  $\Sigma_{j| - j}^{1 / 2}\leq \kappa_{\max}^{1 / 2}$  for all  $j$ , the quantity  $\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j = 1}^{p}\sqrt{n\Sigma_{j| - j}^{1 / 2}} |\widehat{\theta}_j^{\mathrm{d}} - \theta_j^* |$  is  $\sqrt{\kappa_{\max}n / p}$ -Lipschitz in  $\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{d}}$ . Moreover, when  $\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{d}}$  is replaced by  $\theta^{*} + \tau^{*}\Sigma^{-1 / 2}g / \sqrt{n}$ , this quantity has expectation bounded by a constant depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ . By Theorem 10, there exist  $C,C^{\prime},c > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that  $\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j = 1}^{p}\sqrt{n\Sigma_{j| - j}^{1 / 2}} |\widehat{\theta}_j^{\mathrm{d}} - \theta_j^* | < C^{\prime}\sqrt{n / p}$  with probability at least  $1 - Ce^{-cn}$ .  
- The quantity  $\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\phi_1\left(\Sigma_{j|-j}^{1/2}\sqrt{n}|\widehat{\theta}_j^{\mathrm{d}} - \theta_j^*|/\tau^*, \Delta\right)$  is  $\frac{L\sqrt{n}}{\Delta\sqrt{p}}$ -Lipschitz in  $\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{d}}$ , where  $L$  is a constant depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ . By Theorem 10, we conclude there exists  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\epsilon < c'$ , we have

$$
\frac {1}{p} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} \phi_ {1} \left(\Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} \sqrt {n} | \widehat {\theta_ {j} ^ {\mathrm {d}}} - \theta_ {j} ^ {*} | / \tau^ {*}, \Delta\right) <   \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} \mathbb {E} \left[ \phi_ {1} \left(\Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} | (\tau^ {*} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {g}) _ {j} | / \tau^ {*}, \Delta\right) \right] + \epsilon \sqrt {n / p} / \Delta ,
$$

with probability at least  $1 - \frac{C}{\epsilon^3} e^{-cn\epsilon^6}$ .
