- Using the fact that the standard Gaussian density is upper bounded by  $(2\pi)^{-1/2}$ , we obtain the bound  $\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_1\left(\Sigma_{j| - j}^{1/2} \left|\left(\tau^*\pmb{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\pmb{g}\right)_j\right| / \tau^*, \Delta\right)\right] \leq q + \frac{2\Delta}{\sqrt{2\pi}}$ .

Combining the previous bounds, we conclude there exist  $C, C', c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for all  $\epsilon < c'$ , we have  $\mathsf{FCP} \leq q + C' (\Delta + \epsilon \sqrt{n/p / \Delta})$  with probability at least  $1 - \frac{C}{\epsilon^3} e^{-cn\epsilon^6}$ . Optimizing over  $\Delta$ , we conclude there exists  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  and  $\Delta_{\mathrm{max}}$  such that for all  $\epsilon < c'$ , we have  $\mathsf{FCP} \leq q + \epsilon$  with probability at least  $1 - \frac{C}{\epsilon^6} e^{-cn\epsilon^{12}}$ .

The lower bound holds similarly.

B.8. More details on confidence interval for a single coordinate. Because they may be of independent interest, we first describe in detail the construction of the exact tests outlined in the discussion in Section 4.6 and state some results about the quantities involved in the construction (Lemma B.12 and Theorem B.13 below). The proof of Theorem 12 uses a special case of Lemma B.12, whereas Theorem B.13 is independent of any future development, and is stated only for general interest.

B.8.1. Description of exact test. We begin with the following lemma.

LEMMA B.12. We have the following.

(a) (Exact conditional normality of  $\xi_{j}$  when  $\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}} = \theta_j^*$ ). If  $\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}} = \theta_j^*$ , then

$$
\sqrt {n} \left(\xi_ {j} - \theta_ {j} ^ {*}\right) / \hat {\tau} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {j} \sim \mathrm {N} \left(0, \Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {- 1}\right). \tag {103}
$$

(b) (Approximate normality of  $\xi_{j}$  in general). Assume  $p\geq 2$  . Let  $\delta_{\mathrm{loo}} = n / (p - 1)$  . Assume Assumption (A1) is satisfied with  $\Sigma_{-j, - j}$  in place of  $\boldsymbol{\Sigma}$  and  $\theta_{-j}^{*}$  in place of  $\pmb{\theta}^{*}$

Then there exist constants  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that the following occurs. There exist random variables  $r_j, R_j, Z_j$  such that

$$
\sqrt {n} (\xi_ {j} - \theta_ {j} ^ {*}) / \hat {\tau} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {j} = r _ {j} Z _ {j} + R _ {j},
$$

and for all  $|\theta_j^* - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}| < c'$

$$
Z _ {j} \sim \mathsf {N} (0, \Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {- 1}), \qquad \mathbb {P} \left(| r _ {j} - 1 | > C | \theta_ {j} ^ {*} |\right) \leq e ^ {- c n}, \qquad \mathbb {P} \left(| R _ {j} | > \epsilon\right) \leq \frac {C \theta_ {j} ^ {* 2}}{\epsilon^ {2}}.
$$

Lemma B.12(a) implies that the test which rejects when  $|\xi_j| \geq \sum_{j| - j}^{-1/2} \widehat{\tau}_{\mathrm{loo}}^j z_{1 - \alpha/2}$  is an exact level- $\alpha$  test of the null  $\theta_j^* = \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}$ . Lemma B.12(b) states that under the alternative  $\xi_j$  is approximately normal with mean  $\theta_j^* - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}$  and standard deviation  $\sum_{j| - j}^{-1/2} \widehat{\tau}_{\mathrm{loo}}^j / \sqrt{n}$ . (The latter quantity is random but concentrates). Thus, Lemma B.12(b) permits a power analysis of the exact test.

The next theorem is included because it may be of independent interest. No future development depend upon this theorem, and it can safely be skipped.

THEOREM B.13 (Insensitiveity of fixed point parameter to  $\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}$ ). Let  $\tau_{\mathrm{loo}}^{*}(\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}},\zeta_{\mathrm{loo}}^{*}(\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}})$  be the solution to the fixed point equations (9a) and (9b) in the leave-one-out model for the Lasso at regularization  $\lambda$ .

There exists  $c', L > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $|\theta_j^* - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}| \leq c'$ , we have  $|\tau_{\mathrm{loo}}^*(\theta_j^*) - \tau_{\mathrm{loo}}^*(\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}})| \leq L \sqrt{|\theta_j^* - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}|}$ .

Theorem B.13 says that the noise variance is effectively constant for all  $\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}} - \theta_{j}^{*} = o(1)$ .

B.8.2. Proof of Lemma B.12, Theorem 12, and Theorem B.13.

Proof of Lemma B.12(a). When  $\theta_j^* = \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}$ , the data  $(\pmb{y}_{\mathrm{init}},\pmb{X}_{-j})$  is independent of  $\check{x}_j^\perp$ . Because  $\widehat{\theta}_{\mathrm{loo}}$  is  $\sigma (\pmb{y}_{\mathrm{init}},\pmb{X}_{-j})$ -measurable,

$$
\sqrt {n} (\xi_ {j} - \theta_ {j} ^ {*}) / \widehat {\tau} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {j} = \frac {(\breve {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp}) ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {y} _ {\mathrm {i n i t}} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}})}{\Sigma_ {j | - j} \| \boldsymbol {y} _ {\mathrm {i n i t}} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {2}}.
$$

Because  $\check{x}_j^\perp \sim \mathsf{N}(0,\Sigma_{j| - j}\mathbf{I}_p)$  and is independent of  $\boldsymbol{y}_{\mathrm{init}} - \boldsymbol{X}_{-j}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loo}}$ , conditionally on  $\boldsymbol{y}_{\mathrm{init}},\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}$  the quantity is distributed  $\mathsf{N}(0,\Sigma_{j| - j}^{-1})$ . Thus, it is distributed  $\mathsf{N}(0,\Sigma_{j| - j}^{-1})$  unconditionally as well. We have established (103).

Proof of Lemma B.12(b). We may without loss of generality consider the case  $\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}} = 0$ . Indeed, the joint distribution of  $(\pmb{y}_{\mathrm{init}},\pmb{X}_{-j},\check{\pmb{x}}_{j}^{\perp},\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loo}})$  under  $\theta_j^*$  is equal to the joint distribution of  $(\pmb{y}_{\mathrm{init}},\pmb{X}_{-j},\check{\pmb{x}}_{j}^{\perp},\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loo}})$  if the  $j^{\mathrm{th}}$  coordinate of the original model were instead  $\theta_j^* -\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}$ , and the leave-one-out model and Lasso are taken with  $\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}} = 0$ . Under this transformation, the conditions of the Theorem are still met, possibly with  $M^{\prime}$  replaced by  $2M^{\prime}$ .

Thus, consider the case  $\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}} = 0$ . In this case,  $\pmb{y}_{\mathrm{init}} = \pmb{y}$ , and we will write the latter in place of the former in what follows. Define the quantity

$$
\tilde {\xi} _ {j} := \left(\check {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp}\right) ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}}) - \theta_ {j} ^ {*} \Sigma_ {j | - j} (n - \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {0}).
$$

Direct calculations give

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\xi} _ {j} = \left(\check {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp}\right) ^ {\top} \left(\sigma \boldsymbol {z} + \check {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp} \theta_ {j} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}}\right) - \theta_ {j} ^ {*} \Sigma_ {j | - j} (n - \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {0}) \\ = \underbrace {(\check {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp}) ^ {\top} (\sigma \boldsymbol {z} + \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {\prime})} _ {\Delta_ {1}} + \underbrace {(\check {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp}) ^ {\top} (\check {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp} \theta_ {j} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {\prime} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}})) - \theta_ {j} ^ {*} \Sigma_ {j | - j} (n - \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {0})} _ {\Delta_ {2}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {\prime} := \arg \min  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p}} \left\{\frac {1}{2 n} \| \sigma \boldsymbol {z} + \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} \| _ {1} \right\}.
$$

In particular,  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loo}}^{\prime}$  is  $\sigma (z,X_{-j})$  -measurable, so is independent of  $\check{x}_j^\perp$  , whence

$$
\Delta_ {1} \mid z, \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \sim \mathsf {N} \Big (0, \Sigma_ {j | - j} \| \sigma \boldsymbol {z} + \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {\prime} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \Big).
$$

The estimate  $\widehat{\theta}_{\mathrm{loo}}$  is a function of  $z$ ,  $X_{-j}$ , and  $\check{x}_j^\perp$ . We make this explicit by writing  $\widehat{\theta}_{\mathrm{loo}}(z, X_{-j}, \check{x}_j^\perp)$ . Following this notation,  $\widehat{\theta}_{\mathrm{loo}}'$  defined above is equal to  $\widehat{\theta}_{\mathrm{loo}}(z, X_{-j}, 0)$ .

Next consider the term  $\Delta_2$ . First define

$$
F (\pmb {z}, \pmb {X} _ {- j}, \check {\pmb {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp}) := \check {\pmb {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp} \theta_ {j} ^ {*} + \pmb {X} _ {- j} (\widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} (\pmb {z}, \pmb {X} _ {- j}, \mathbf {0}) - \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} (\pmb {z}, \pmb {X} _ {- j}, \check {\pmb {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp})).
$$

Use  $\nabla_{\check{\mathbf{x}}_j^{\perp}}$  to denote the Jacobian with respect to  $\check{x}_j^\perp$ . Almost surely,  $\nabla_{\check{\mathbf{x}}_j^{\perp}}F(\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{X}_{-j},\check{\mathbf{x}}_j^{\perp}) = \theta_j^* (\mathbf{I}_n - \mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{-j\hat{\mathcal{S}}}})$ , where  $\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{-j\hat{\mathcal{S}}}}$  is the projector onto the span of  $\{\check{x}_k|k\in \widehat{S}\}$  and  $\widehat{S}$  is the support of  $\widehat{\theta}_{\mathrm{loo}}(\boldsymbol {z},\boldsymbol{X}_{-j},\check{\mathbf{x}}_j^{\perp})$  [80]. The function  $F$  is  $\theta_j^*$ -Lipschitz in  $\check{x}_j^{\perp}$  for fixed  $\boldsymbol {z},\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}$ . Therefore we conclude that  $\Delta_2 = (\check{x}_j^{\perp})^\top F(\boldsymbol {z},\boldsymbol{X}_{-j},\check{x}_j^{\perp}) - \Sigma_{j|\text{-} j}\operatorname {div}_{\check{x}_j^{\perp}}F(\boldsymbol {z},\boldsymbol{X}_{-j},\check{x}_j^{\perp})$ . Applying Stein's formula and the second-order Stein's formula [11, Eq. (2.1) and Theorem 2.1], we get

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \Delta_ {2} | \boldsymbol {z}, \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \right] = 0 \quad \text {a n d}
$$

$$
\mathrm {V a r} (\Delta_ {2} | \pmb {z}, \pmb {X} _ {- j}) = \Sigma_ {j | - j} \left(\mathbb {E} \Big [ \| F (\pmb {z}, \pmb {X} _ {- j}, \check {\pmb {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \Sigma_ {j | - j} \mathrm {T r} (\nabla_ {\check {\pmb {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp}} F (\pmb {z}, \pmb {X} _ {- j}, \check {\pmb {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp}) ^ {2}) \mid \pmb {z}, \pmb {X} _ {- j} \Big ]\right).
$$

Note that, almost surely  $\mathrm{Tr}(\nabla_{\check{\mathbf{x}}_j^\perp}F(\boldsymbol {z},\boldsymbol{X}_{-j},\check{\mathbf{x}}_j^\perp)^2) = \theta_j^{*2}(n - \| \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loo}}\| _0)$ . Further,  $\| F(z,\boldsymbol {X}_{-j},\check{\mathbf{x}}_j^\perp)\| _2^2\leq$ $\theta_j^{*2}\| \check{\mathbf{x}}_j^\perp \| _2^2$  because  $F$  is  $\theta_j^*$ -Lipschitz and  $F(\boldsymbol {z},\boldsymbol{X}_{-j},\mathbf{0}) = \mathbf{0}$ . Thus,

$\operatorname{Var}(\Delta_2|\boldsymbol{z}, \boldsymbol{X}_{-j}) \leq \Sigma_{j| - j}\mathbb{E}\left[\theta_j^{*2}\left(\|\check{\boldsymbol{x}}_j^\perp\|_2^2 + \Sigma_{j| - j}\left(n - \|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loo}}\|_0\right)\right)\Big|\boldsymbol{z}, \boldsymbol{X}_{-j}\right] \leq 2n\Sigma_{j| - j}^2\theta_j^{*2}$  almost surely.

Because the  $\mathbb{E}[\Delta_2|\pmb {z},\pmb{X}_{-j}] = 0$  almost surely, we have  $\mathrm{Var}(\Delta_2)\leq 2n\Sigma_{j| - j}^2\theta_j^{*2}$  as well. Next observe that  $\frac{\tilde{\xi}_j}{\Sigma_{j| - j}(n - \| \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\|_0)} = \xi_j - \theta_j^*$ . Thus,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {(n - \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {0}) (\xi_ {j} - \theta_ {j} ^ {*})}{\| \boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {2}} = \frac {\| \sigma \boldsymbol {z} + \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {\prime} \| _ {2}}{\| \boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {2}} \frac {\Delta_ {1}}{\Sigma_ {j | - j} \| \sigma \boldsymbol {z} + \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {\prime} \| _ {2}} \\ + \frac {\Delta_ {2}}{\Sigma_ {j | - j} \| \pmb {y} - \pmb {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {2}} \\ =: r _ {j} Z _ {j} + R _ {j}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $Z_{j} \coloneqq \frac{\Delta_{1}}{\Sigma_{j| - j}\|\sigma z + X_{-j}\pmb{\theta}_{\mathrm{loo}}^{*} - X_{-j}\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loo}}^{\prime}\|_{2}} \sim \mathsf{N}(0,\Sigma_{j| - j}^{-1})$  (and normality follows by the proof of Eq. (103)).

The singular values of  $\Sigma_{-j, - j}$  are bounded between the minimal and maximal singular values of  $\pmb{\Sigma}$ . Thus, the matrix  $\Sigma_{-j, - j}$  satisfies Assumption (A1) because  $\pmb{\Sigma}$  does. In particular, the triple  $\lambda$ ,  $\Sigma_{-j, - j}$ , and  $\sigma_{\mathrm{loo}}^2$  satisfy Assumption (A1) provided  $|\theta_j^* |\leq 1$  (or any constant). Because  $\pmb{\theta}_{-j}^{*}$  satisfies Assumption (A1)(d) for matrix  $\Sigma_{-j, - j}$ , and because  $\| \theta_j^*\Sigma_{-j, - j}^{-1}\Sigma_{-j,j}\| _1\leq |\theta_j^* ||\Sigma_{-j, - j}^{-1}\Sigma_{-j,j}\| _2\leq \sqrt{p} |\theta_j^* | \kappa_{\min}^{-1 / 2}\kappa_{\max}$ , Assumption (A1)(d) for the leave-one-out model if  $M$  is replaced by  $M + |\theta_j^* |\kappa_{\min}^{-1 / 2}\kappa_{\max} / \sqrt{\Delta_{\min}}$ . In particular, we may apply all of our results to this model when  $|\theta_j^* |\leq 1$ .

Because  $F$  is  $\theta_{j}^{*}$ -Lipschitz in  $\check{x}_{j}^{\perp}$ , we have  $||\boldsymbol{y} - \boldsymbol{X}_{-j}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loo}}||_2 - ||\sigma \boldsymbol{z} + \boldsymbol{X}_{-j}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\mathrm{loo}}^* - \boldsymbol{X}_{-j}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loo}}'\|_2| < \theta_{j}^{*}||\check{x}_{j}^{\perp}||_2$ . By Theorem 7 and since  $\theta_{j}^{*2}||\check{x}_{j}^{\perp}||_{2}^{2} \sim \Sigma_{j| - j}\theta_{j}^{*2}\chi_{n}^{2}$ , there exist  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  and  $M'$  such that for  $\epsilon < c'$ , it is guaranteed that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(| r _ {j} - 1 | > \epsilon\right) = \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \frac {\| \sigma \boldsymbol {z} + \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {\prime} \| _ {2}}{\| \boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {2}} - 1 \right| > \epsilon\right) \\ = \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \frac {\left\| \sigma z + X _ {- j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} - X _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {2} - \left\| \boldsymbol {y} - X _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \right\| _ {2}}{\| \boldsymbol {y} - X _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {2}} \right| > \epsilon\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\frac {| \theta_ {j} ^ {*} | \| \check {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp} \| _ {2}}{\| \boldsymbol {y} - \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {\prime} \| _ {2} - | \theta_ {j} ^ {*} | \| \check {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {j} ^ {\perp} \| _ {2}} > \epsilon\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Recall we have used that  $\| \pmb {y} - \pmb{X}_{-j}\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loo}}^{\prime}\|_{2} / \sqrt{n}$  concentrates on a quantity for which we have a lower bound by Theorem 7. Second, we have used that  $\mathbb{P}(|\theta_j^* ||\check{x}_j^\perp ||_2 / \sqrt{n} >2\Sigma_{j| - j}^{1 / 2}|\theta_j^*)\leq$ $C\exp (-cn)$ . Thus, choosing  $C,C^{\prime},c,c^{\prime} > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ , we have for  $|\theta_j^* | < c'$  and  $\epsilon = C|\theta_j^*$  that the previous display is buounded above by  $Ce^{-cn}$

Similarly, combining the concentration of  $\Sigma_{j| - j}\| \pmb {y} - \pmb{X}_{-j}\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{\mathrm{loo}}^{\prime}\|_{2} / \sqrt{n}$  on a quantity for which we have a lower bound, the high probability upper bound on  $|\theta_j^*\| \check{x}_j^\perp \| _2 / \sqrt{n}$ , and Chebyshev's inequality applied to  $\Delta_2$ , there exists  $C,c^{\prime} > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for  $\epsilon < c^{\prime}$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(| R _ {j} | > \epsilon\right) <   \frac {C \theta_ {j} ^ {* 2}}{\epsilon^ {2}}.
$$

The proof of the lemma is complete.

Proof of Theorem 12(a). The event  $\theta \notin \mathsf{Cl}_j^{\mathrm{loo}}$  is equivalent to

$$
\frac {\sum_ {j \mid - j} ^ {1 / 2} \sqrt {n} (\xi_ {j} - \theta)}{\widehat {\tau} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {j}} \notin [ - z _ {1 - \alpha / 2}, z _ {1 - \alpha / 2} ].
$$

With  $r_j, R_j$  defined as in Theorem B.12, this is equivalent to

$$
A := \Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} (r _ {j} Z _ {j} + R _ {j}) + \frac {\Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} \sqrt {n} (\theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \theta)}{\widehat {\tau} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {j}} \notin [ - z _ {1 - \alpha / 2}, z _ {1 - \alpha / 2} ].
$$

By Theorems 7 and 8 on concentration of the Lasso residual and sparsity and Theorem B.12 on the concentration of  $r_j$  and  $R_j$ , there exist  $C, c, c' > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that for all  $\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2 < c'$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left| A - \Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} Z _ {j} - \frac {\Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} \sqrt {n} (\theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \theta)}{\tau_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*}} \right| > \epsilon_ {1} + \sqrt {n} | \theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \theta | \epsilon_ {2}\right) \leq \frac {C}{\epsilon_ {2} ^ {3}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon_ {2} ^ {6}} + e ^ {- c n} + \frac {C (\theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \widehat {\theta} _ {j , \mathrm {i n i t}}) ^ {2}}{\epsilon_ {1} ^ {2}}.
$$

Thus, by direct calculation (where  $C$  may take different values between lines)

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(A \notin [ - z _ {1 - \alpha / 2}, z _ {1 + \alpha / 2} ]\right) \geq \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} Z _ {j} + \frac {\Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} \sqrt {n} \left(\theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \theta\right)}{\tau_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*}} \right| > z _ {1 - \alpha / 2} + \epsilon_ {1} + \sqrt {n} \left| \theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \theta \right| \epsilon_ {2}\right)
$$

$$
- \mathbb {P} \left(\left| A - \Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} Z _ {j} - \frac {\Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} \sqrt {n} \left(\theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \theta\right)}{\tau_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*}} \right| > \epsilon_ {1} + \sqrt {n} \left| \theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \theta \right| \epsilon_ {2}\right)
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \geq \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} Z _ {j} + \frac {\Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {1 / 2} \sqrt {n} (\theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \theta)}{\tau_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*}} \right| > z _ {1 - \alpha / 2} + \epsilon_ {1} + \sqrt {n} | \theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \theta | \epsilon_ {2}\right) \\ - C \left(\frac {1}{\epsilon_ {2} ^ {3}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon_ {2} ^ {6}} + e ^ {- c n} + \frac {(\theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \widehat {\theta} _ {j , \mathrm {i n i t}}) ^ {2}}{\epsilon_ {1} ^ {2}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \geq \mathbb {P} \left(| \theta_ {j} ^ {*} + \Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {- 1 / 2} \tau_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} G / \sqrt {n} - \theta | \geq \Sigma_ {j | - j} ^ {- 1 / 2} \tau_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} z _ {1 - \alpha / 2}\right) \\ - C \left(\epsilon_ {1} + \sqrt {n} | \theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \theta | \epsilon_ {2} + \frac {1}{\epsilon_ {2} ^ {3}} e ^ {- c n \epsilon_ {2} ^ {6}} + e ^ {- c n} + \frac {(\theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \widehat {\theta} _ {j , \mathrm {i n i t}}) ^ {2}}{\epsilon_ {1} ^ {2}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Taking  $\epsilon_{1} = |\theta_{j}^{*} - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}|^{2 / 3}$  and  $\epsilon_{2} = n^{-1 / 6 + \gamma}$ , we get one side of the inequality. The reverse inequality is obtained similarly.

Proof of Theorem 12(b). By definition, one has

$$
\frac {\widehat {\tau} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {j}}{\tau_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*}} = \frac {\| \pmb {y} - \pmb {X} _ {- j} \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {2} / \sqrt {n}}{(1 - \| \widehat {\pmb {\theta}} _ {\mathrm {l o o}} \| _ {0} / n) \tau_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*}}.
$$

As argued in the proof of Theorem B.12(b), the leave-one-out model obeys Assumption (A1) provided  $\widehat{\theta}_j - \theta_j^* \leq 1$  (or some constant). Then Equation (29) follows from Theorems 7 and 8 on the concentration results for the Lasso residual and the sparsity.

Proof of Theorem B.13. As argued in the proof of Theorem B.12(b), Assumption (A1) is satisfied by the leave-one-out model provided  $|\theta_j^* - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}| \leq 1$  (or any constant).

To emphasize the dependence of  $\tau_{\mathrm{loo}}^{*}$  on  $\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}$ , we write  $\tau_{\mathrm{loo}}^{*}(\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}})$ . Our goal is to bound  $|\tau_{\mathrm{loo}}^{*}(\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}) - \tau_{\mathrm{loo}}^{*}(\theta_{j}^{*})|$  To control the fixed point parameter  $\tau_{\mathrm{loo}}^{*}$ , we will study the functional objective  $\mathcal{E}_0$  of Eq. (49) for the leave-one-out model as we vary  $\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}$ . For simplicity of notation, we will drop the subscript on  $\mathcal{E}_0$ . As we vary  $\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}$ , the only parameters defining the leave-one-out model which change are the noise variance  $\sigma_{\mathrm{loo}}^2 = \sigma_{\mathrm{loo}}^2 (\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}})$  and  $\pmb{\theta}_{\mathrm{loo}}^{*} = \pmb{\theta}_{\mathrm{loo}}^{*}(\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}})$ . We write

$$
\mathscr {E} (\boldsymbol {v}; \widehat {\theta} _ {j, \text {i n i t}}) := \frac {1}{2} \Big (\sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} + \sigma_ {\text {l o o}} ^ {2}} - \frac {\langle \boldsymbol {g} , \boldsymbol {v} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}}}{\sqrt {n}} \Big) _ {+} ^ {2} + \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \Big \{\| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\text {l o o}} ^ {*} + (\boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {- j, - j}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {1} - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\text {l o o}} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \Big) \Big \}.
$$

Denote the unique minimizer of  $\mathcal{E}$  by  $\pmb{v}^{*} = \pmb{v}^{*}(\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}})$ . Existence and uniqueness is guaranteed by Lemma A.2. Also by the proof of Lemma A.2,

$$
\tau_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} = \sqrt {\sigma_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}}. \tag {104}
$$

Under Assumption (A1), the objective  $\mathcal{E}$  is  $L$ -Lipschitz in  $\sigma_{\mathrm{loo}}^2$  on  $\sigma_{\mathrm{loo}}^2 > \sigma_{\mathrm{min}}^2$  and is  $L$ -Lipschitz in  $\theta_{\mathrm{loo}}^*$  for some  $L$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ . Recall that  $\sigma_{\mathrm{loo}}^2 - \sigma^2 = \Sigma_{j| - j} (\theta_j^* - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}})^2 \leq C |\theta_j^* - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}|$  for  $|\theta_j^* - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}| < c'$  and  $\| \theta_{\mathrm{loo}}^*(\theta_j^*) - \theta_{\mathrm{loo}}^*(\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}})\|_2 \leq C |\theta_j^* - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}|$ , for appropriately chosen  $C, c'$ . By the proof of Lemma A.4, there exists  $r, a > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that  $\mathcal{E}(\cdot; \theta_j^*)$  is  $a$ -strongly convex in  $\pmb{v}$  on  $\| \pmb{v} - \pmb{v}^*(\theta_j^*) \|_2 \leq r$ . Thus, for  $\| \pmb{v} - \pmb{v}^*(\theta_j^*) \|_{L^2} \leq r$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {E} (\pmb {v}; \widehat {\theta} _ {j, \mathrm {i n i t}}) \geq \mathcal {E} (\pmb {v}; \theta_ {j} ^ {*}) - L | \theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \widehat {\theta} _ {j, \mathrm {i n i t}} | \\ \geq \mathcal {E} (\boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} (\theta_ {j} ^ {*}); \theta_ {j} ^ {*}) + a \| \boldsymbol {v} - \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} (\theta_ {j} ^ {*}) \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} - L | \theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \widehat {\theta} _ {j, \mathrm {i n i t}} | \\ \geq \mathcal {E} (\boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} (\theta_ {j} ^ {*}); \widehat {\theta} _ {j, \mathrm {i n i t}}) + a \| \boldsymbol {v} - \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} (\theta_ {j} ^ {*}) \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} - 2 L | \theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \widehat {\theta} _ {j, \mathrm {i n i t}} |. \\ \end{array}
$$

We conclude that if  $\sqrt{2L|\theta_j^* - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}| / a} \leq r$ , then  $\| \pmb{v}^{*}(\widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}) - \pmb{v}^{*}(\theta_{j}^{*})\|_{L^{2}} \leq \sqrt{2L|\theta_{j}^{*} - \widehat{\theta}_{j,\mathrm{init}}| / a}$ . By Eq. (104),

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \tau_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {j, \mathrm {i n i t}}) - \tau_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {*}) | = \left| \sqrt {\sigma_ {\mathrm {l o o}} ^ {2} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {j , \mathrm {i n i t}}) + \| \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {j , \mathrm {i n i t}}) \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}} - \sqrt {\sigma^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {v} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {*}) \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}} \right| \\ \leq L \sqrt {\left| \theta_ {j} ^ {*} - \omega \right|}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the  $L$  in the final line differs from the one in the preceding line and depends only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ .

The proof is complete.

# B.8.3. Proof of Lemma B.4.

LEMMA B.14. Assume  $n / p \geq \delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\mathrm{sign}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{*}), \boldsymbol{\Sigma}) + \Delta_{\min}$ . Then there exist finite constants  $a, c_0, c_1, C_0 > 0$  depending only on  $\Delta_{\min}, \kappa_{\min}, \kappa_{\max}$  such that if  $n \geq c_1$  the following happens with probability at least  $1 - C_0 e^{-c_0 n}$ . For any  $\boldsymbol{w} \in \mathbb{R}^p$ :

$$
\| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {1} - \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \leq 0 \Rightarrow \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \geq a \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2}.
$$

PROOF OF LEMMA B.14. The Gaussian width  $\mathcal{G}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\Sigma})$  is an upper bound on the standard notion of Gaussian width  $\mathcal{G}_d(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\Sigma})$  defined in Eq. (11). Thus,

$$
\sqrt {n / p - \Delta_ {\min }} \geq \mathcal {G} _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {\Sigma}) = \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \mathbb {E} \left[ \max  _ {\substack {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathcal {K} (\boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {\Sigma}) \\ \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} / p \leq 1}} \langle \boldsymbol {v}, \boldsymbol {g} \rangle \right]. \tag{105}
$$

The result then follows from standard results; see, for example, Corollary 3.3 of [23] and its proof. We repeat the proof here for convenience.

For simplicity of notation, we will denote  $\mathcal{K} = \mathcal{K}(\pmb{x},\pmb{\Sigma})$ . Let  $\pmb{x} = \mathrm{sign}(\pmb{\theta}^{*})$ . Note that

$$
\| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {1} - \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {1} = \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {S ^ {c}} \| _ {1} + \| (\bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {w}) _ {S} \| _ {1} - \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {S} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \geq \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {S ^ {c}} \| _ {1} + \sum_ {j \in \operatorname {s u p p} (\boldsymbol {x})} x _ {j} \boldsymbol {w} _ {j},
$$

whence  $\| \bar{\pmb{\theta}}^{*} + \pmb {w}\|_{1} - \| \bar{\pmb{\theta}}^{*}\|_{1}\leq 0$  implies  $\Sigma^{1 / 2}\pmb {w}\in \mathcal{K}$ . Thus, it suffices to show that with probability at least  $1 - C_0e^{-c_0n}$ , one has

$$
\boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {w} \in \mathcal {K} \Rightarrow \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \geq a \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2}.
$$

Define the minimum singular value over  $\kappa$  as

$$
\kappa_ {-} (\boldsymbol {X}, \mathcal {K}): = \inf  \left\{\frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \mid \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {w} \in \mathcal {K}, \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} = 1 \right\},
$$

and define  $\tilde{\kappa}_{-}(\pmb {X},\mathcal{K}):= \inf \left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}} ||\pmb {X}\pmb {w}||_{2}\mid \pmb{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\pmb {w}\in \mathcal{K},\| \pmb{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\pmb {w}\|_{2} = 1\right\}$ . Then, because  $\mathcal{K}$  is a cone (and so is scale invariant),

$$
\kappa_ {-} (\boldsymbol {X}, \mathcal {K}) \geq \tilde {\kappa} _ {-} (\boldsymbol {X}, \mathcal {K}) \cdot \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} = 1} \| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \geq \tilde {\kappa} _ {-} (\boldsymbol {X}, \mathcal {K}) \kappa_ {\min } ^ {1 / 2}.
$$

Thus, it suffices to show there exists  $a > 0$  depending on  $\Delta_{\min}, \kappa_{\min}, \kappa_{\max}$  such that with high-probability  $\kappa_{-}(\mathbf{X}, \mathcal{K}) \geq a$ .

By definition,

$$
-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\kappa}_{-}(\boldsymbol {X},\mathcal{K})] = \mathbb{E}\Big[\max_{\substack{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\boldsymbol {w}\in \mathcal{K}\\ \| \boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\boldsymbol {w}\|_{2} = 1}} - \frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\| \boldsymbol {X}\boldsymbol {w}\|_{2}\Big] = \mathbb{E}\Big[\max_{\substack{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\boldsymbol {w}\in \mathcal{K}\\ \| \boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\boldsymbol {w}\|_{2} = 1}}\min_{\| \boldsymbol {u}\|_{2} = 1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol {X}\boldsymbol {w}\Big].
$$

Recall that the rows of  $\mathbf{X}$  are distributed iid from  $\mathsf{N}(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{\Sigma})$ . By Gordon's lemma (Corollary G.1 of [56])

$$
\begin{array}{l} -\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\kappa}_{-}(\boldsymbol {X},\mathcal{K})]\leq \mathbb{E}\Big[\max_{\substack{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\boldsymbol {w}\in \mathcal{K}\\ \| \boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\boldsymbol {w}\|_{2} = 1}}\min_{\| \boldsymbol {u}\|_{2} = 1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\| \boldsymbol {\Sigma}\boldsymbol {w}\|_{2}\langle \boldsymbol {h},\boldsymbol {u}\rangle +\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\| \boldsymbol {u}\|_{2}\langle \boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\boldsymbol {w},\boldsymbol {g}\rangle \Big] \\ = \frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\max_{\substack{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\boldsymbol {w}\in \mathcal{K}\\ \| \boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\boldsymbol {w}\|_{2} = 1}} - \| \boldsymbol {h}\|_{2} + \langle \boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1 / 2}\boldsymbol {w},\boldsymbol {g}\rangle \Big]\leq -\sqrt{\frac{n}{n + 1}} +\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\mathcal{G}(\boldsymbol {x},\boldsymbol {\Sigma}) \\ \leq - \sqrt {\frac {n}{n + 1}} + \sqrt {\frac {p}{n}} \wedge \sqrt {1 - \Delta_ {\min} \frac {p}{n}} \leq - \sqrt {\frac {n}{n + 1}} + \frac {1}{2} \vee \left(1 - \frac {\Delta_ {\min}}{4}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second-to-last equality uses  $\mathbb{E}[\| \pmb {h}\| _2]\geq \frac{n}{\sqrt{n + 1}}$  and the definition of  $\mathcal{G}(\pmb {x},\pmb {\Sigma})$ , and the last inequality uses the upper bound on the Gaussian width (105) and considering cases  $p / n\leq 1 / 4$  and  $p / n\geq 1 / 4$ . For all  $n\geq 2$  we have  $\sqrt{n / (n + 1)}\geq \sqrt{(n - 1) / n}\geq 1 - \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}n}$ . Thus, for  $n\geq \frac{1}{(\sqrt{2} / 4)\wedge(\sqrt{2}\Delta_{\min} / 8)}$ ,  $\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\kappa}_{-}(\pmb {X},\mathcal{K})]\geq \frac{1}{4}\vee \frac{\Delta_{\min}}{8}$ .

The quantity  $\tilde{\kappa}_{-}(\mathbf{X},\mathcal{K})$  as a function of  $\mathbf{X}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1 / 2}$  is  $\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}$ -Lipschitz with respect to the Frobenius norm. Thus

$$
\mathbb {P} (\tilde {\kappa} _ {-} (\boldsymbol {X}, \mathcal {K}) \leq \mathbb {E} [ \tilde {\kappa} _ {-} (\boldsymbol {X}, \mathcal {K}) ] - t) \leq e ^ {- n t ^ {2} / 2}.
$$

Taking  $t = (1 / 8)\lor (\Delta_{\mathrm{min}} / 16)$  completes the proof.

LEMMA B.15. Under Assumption (A1), there exist constants  $C, C_0, c_0 > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that

$$
(1 0 6) \mathbb {P} \left(\forall \lambda \in \left[ \lambda_ {\min }, \lambda_ {\max } \right]: \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \left| \left\| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \right\| _ {1} - \left\| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \right\| _ {1} \right| \leq C\right) \geq 1 - C _ {0} e ^ {- c _ {0} n}.
$$

PROOF OF LEMMA B.15. The proof follows almost exactly that for [56, Proposition C.4], using Lemma B.14. The primary difference is the approximation of  $\theta^{*}$  by  $\bar{\theta}^{*}$ .

Let  $\theta^{*}$  be as in Assumption (A1)(d). Note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \geq \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {1} - 2 \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \\ \geq \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) - \frac {2 \lambda M}{\Delta_ {\min }}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We show that the high probability event (106) is implied by the event

$$
\mathcal {A} := \left\{\| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {1} - \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \leq 0 \Rightarrow \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \geq a \| \boldsymbol {w} \| _ {2} \right\} \bigcap \left\{\| \boldsymbol {z} \| _ {2} \leq 2 \sqrt {n} \right\},
$$

where  $a$  is as in Lemma B.14. On this event,  $\mathcal{C}(\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{\lambda})\leq \mathcal{C}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{0}) = \sigma^{2}\| \pmb {z}\|_{2}^{2} / (2n)\leq 2\sigma^{2}$ , whence

$$
\frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \hat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \leq 2 \sigma^ {2} / \lambda_ {\min } + 2 M / \Delta_ {\min },
$$

which further implies

$$
\frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}\right) \leq 2 \sigma^ {2} / \lambda_ {\min } + 4 M / \Delta_ {\min }.
$$

Let  $\widehat{\pmb{w}}^{\lambda} = \pmb{\Sigma}^{-1 / 2}\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{\lambda}$ . On the event  $\mathcal{A}$ , we also have

$$
\begin{array}{l} 2 \sigma^ {2} \geq \mathcal {C} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda}) \geq \frac {1}{2 n} \| \sigma \boldsymbol {z} - \boldsymbol {X} \widehat {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {1} - \frac {2 \lambda M}{\Delta_ {\min }} \\ \geq \frac {1}{4 n} \| \boldsymbol {X} \hat {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{2 n} \| \boldsymbol {z} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n / p}} \| \hat {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {2} - \frac {2 \lambda M}{\Delta_ {\mathrm {m i n}}} \\ \geq \frac {a}{4} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - 4 \sigma^ {2} - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {\Delta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {w}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {2} - \frac {2 \lambda M}{\Delta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We conclude that  $\| \widehat{\boldsymbol{w}}^{\lambda}\|_{2}\leq C$ , for  $C$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ . Because  $\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\big|\| \pmb {\theta}^{*} + \widehat{\pmb{w}}^{\lambda}\|_{1}-$ $\| \pmb {\theta}^{*}\|_{1}\big|\leq \| \widehat{\pmb{w}}\|_{1} / \sqrt{n}\leq \| \widehat{\pmb{w}}\|_{2} / \sqrt{\Delta_{\min}}$ , the result follows.

Lemma B.4 follows from Lemma B.15 by exactly the same argument in the proof of [56, Lemma C.5].

B.8.4. Proof of Lemma B.5. Recall from the proof of Lemma A.2 (in particular, Eq. (50)) that  $\widehat{\pmb{v}}^{f,\lambda}$ , where the latter is viewed as a function of  $\pmb{g}$  and hence an element of  $L^2 (\mathbb{R}^p;\mathbb{R}^p)$ , is the unique minimizer of  $\mathcal{C}^{\lambda}$ . By optimality,

$$
\frac {\sigma^ {2}}{2} = \mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda} (\mathbf {0}) \geq \mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda}\right) \geq \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1}, \right\}. \tag {107}
$$

Take  $\bar{\theta}^*$  as in Assumption (A1). Note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\} \geq \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {1} - 2 \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\} \\ \geq \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \hat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\} - \frac {2 \lambda M}{\sqrt {\Delta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

By the definition of Gaussian width (see Eq. (13)), either

$$
\frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \hat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {\lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\} \geq 0,
$$

or

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{2} = \mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda} (\mathbf {0}) \geq \mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda}) \geq \frac {1}{2} \left(\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}} - \frac {\mathcal {G} (\boldsymbol {x} , \boldsymbol {\Sigma}) \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f , \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}}}{\sqrt {n / p}}\right) _ {+} ^ {2} - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {1} \right\} - \frac {2 \lambda M}{\sqrt {\Delta_ {\min}}} \\ \geq \frac {1}{2} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} \left(1 - \sqrt {\frac {p}{n}} \wedge \sqrt {1 - \frac {p}{n} \Delta_ {\min }}\right) ^ {2} \\ - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {\Delta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {2} \right\} - \frac {2 \lambda M}{\sqrt {\Delta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}} \\ \geq \frac {1}{2} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} \left(\frac {1}{4} \wedge \left(1 - \frac {\Delta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}{4}\right) ^ {2}\right) - \frac {\lambda \kappa_ {\operatorname* {m i n}} ^ {- 1 / 2}}{\sqrt {\Delta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}} - \frac {2 \lambda M}{\sqrt {\Delta_ {\operatorname* {m i n}}}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality uses that  $\mathcal{G}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\leq 1\vee \sqrt{n / p - \Delta_{\mathrm{min}}}$ , and the last inequality considers separately cases  $p / n\leq 1 / 4$  and  $p / n\geq 1 / 4$ . The previous display implies  $\| \widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^{f,\lambda}\|_{L^2}\leq C$  for some  $C$  depending on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ , in which case (108)

$$
\frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\} \geq - \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {1} \right\} \geq - \frac {\kappa_ {\min } ^ {- 1 / 2}}{\sqrt {\Delta_ {\min }}} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}} \geq - C,
$$

for some possibly new value of  $C$ . Combining Eqs. (107) and (108), there exists  $C$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that

$$
\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \mathbb {E} \left\{\| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {1} - \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| _ {1} \right\} \right| \leq C.
$$

By Lemma 4, the solutions to the fixed point equations (9a) and (9b) are bounded by  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ -dependent constants. By the proof of Lemma A.4, there exists  $r, a > 0$  depending only on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$  such that  $\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}$  is  $a$  strongly-convex in the neighborhood  $\| \pmb{v} - \widehat{\pmb{v}}^{f,\lambda}\|_{L^2} \leq r$  around its minimizer. Thus, we conclude for any  $\pmb{v} \in L^2$  we have

$$
\mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq \mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda}\right) + h \left(\| \boldsymbol {v} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}}\right)
$$

where  $h(x) \coloneqq \min \{ax^2 / 2, ar|x| / 2\}$ . It worth emphasizing that this bound holds for with the same  $a, r$  for all  $\lambda \in [\lambda_{\min}, \lambda_{\max}]$ . Then direct calculations further give

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda^ {\prime}}\right) \geq \mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda}\right) + h \left(\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda^ {\prime}} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}}\right) \geq \mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda^ {\prime}} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda}\right) + h \left(\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda^ {\prime}} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}}\right) - C | \lambda^ {\prime} - \lambda | \\ \geq \mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda^ {\prime}} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda^ {\prime}}\right) + 2 h \left(\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda^ {\prime}} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}}\right) - C | \lambda^ {\prime} - \lambda | \\ \geq \mathcal {E} ^ {\lambda} (\widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda}) + 4 h \left(\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda^ {\prime}} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {f, \lambda} \| _ {L ^ {2}}\right) - 2 C | \lambda^ {\prime} - \lambda |, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality holds by the same string of manipulations justifying the first three. Take  $c' = ar^2 / C$ . If  $|\lambda - \lambda'| < c'$ , we have  $h(\| \widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^{f,\lambda'} - \widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^{f,\lambda} \|_{L^2}) \leq ar^2 / 2$ , whence in fact  $h(\| \widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^{f,\lambda'} - \widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^{f,\lambda} \|_{L^2}) = \frac{a \| \widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^{f,\lambda'} - \widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^{f,\lambda} \|_{L^2}^2}{2}$ . We conclude  $\| \widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^{f,\lambda'} - \widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}^{f,\lambda} \|_{L^2} \leq K |\lambda - \lambda'|^{1/2}$  for appropriately chosen  $K$ .

B.9. Control of the empirical distribution: proof of Corollary 6. In the fixed design model, let  $I$  be uniformly distributed on  $[p]$  independently of  $\pmb{g}$ . Let  $\mu_{*}$  be the distribution of  $(\theta_I^*,\widehat{\theta}_I^f)$ . For any  $k$ , we have  $\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i = 1}^{p}\phi_k(\sqrt{n}\theta_i^*,\sqrt{n}\widehat{\theta}_i^f)$  is  $\tau \kappa_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1 / 2} / \sqrt{p}$ -Lipschitz in  $\pmb{g}$ , so that by Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \phi_ {k} (\sqrt {n} \theta_ {i} ^ {*}, \sqrt {n} \widehat {\theta_ {i} ^ {f}}) - \mathbb {E} \left[ \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \phi_ {k} (\sqrt {n} \theta_ {i} ^ {*}, \sqrt {n} \widehat {\theta_ {i} ^ {f}}) \right] \right| > t\right) \leq 2 e ^ {- 2 p \kappa_ {\min} t ^ {2} / \tau_ {\max} ^ {2}},
$$

whence  $\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i = 1}^{p}\phi_k(\theta_i^*,\widehat{\theta}_i^f) - \mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i = 1}^{p}\phi_k(\theta_i^*,\widehat{\theta}_i^f)\right]\right|\right] \leq C / \sqrt{p}$ , for some  $C$  depending on  $\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}$ . Summing the above inequality over  $k = 1,\ldots ,\infty$ , we obtain that

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ d _ {w ^ {*}} \left(\frac {1}{p} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \delta_ {\sqrt {n} \theta_ {i} ^ {*}, \sqrt {n} \widehat {\theta} _ {i} ^ {f}}, \mu_ {*}\right) \right] \leq C / \sqrt {p}.
$$

Note further that  $d_{w^*}\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i = 1}^{p}\delta_{\sqrt{n}\theta_i^*,\sqrt{n}\widehat{\theta}_i^f},\mu_*\right)$  is  $\tau_{\mathrm{max}}\kappa_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1 / 2} / \sqrt{p}$ -Lipschitz in  $\pmb{g}$ . Applying Gaussian Lipschitz concentration in the fixed design model, we conclude the second inequality in Corollary 6. Because the function is  $\tau_{\mathrm{max}}\kappa_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1 / 2}\sqrt{\Delta_{\mathrm{max}}}.$  -Lipschitz in its argument, applying Theorem 5, we conclude the first inequality in Corollary 6.

# C. Auxiliary results and proofs.

C.1. Unbounded risk below the Donoho-Tanner phase transition: Proof of Proposition 14.

PROOF OF PROPOSITION 14. By Proposition 2, we can prove the result with  $\mathcal{G}_d$  in place of  $\mathcal{G}$ , at the cost of changing constants. Recall  $\mathcal{C}(\cdot)$  and  $\mathcal{L}(\cdot)$  as defined in Eqs. (31) and (32). First note that

$$
\mathcal {C} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq \mathcal {C} ^ {\prime} (\boldsymbol {v}) := \frac {1}{2 n} \| \sigma \boldsymbol {z} - \boldsymbol {X} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \frac {\lambda}{\sqrt {n}} \| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {1}.
$$

Because  $\mathcal{C}'(\cdot)$  is continuous almost surely, for all  $r > 0$ , there exists  $T > 0$ , depending on  $r$  but not on  $\theta^{*}$ , such that

$$
\mathbb {P} \Big (\inf  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq r} \mathcal {C} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq - T / 2 \Big) \geq \mathbb {P} \Big (\inf  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \leq r} \mathcal {C} ^ {\prime} (\boldsymbol {v}) \geq - T / 2 \Big) \geq 1 - C e ^ {- c p \epsilon^ {2}}.
$$

To complete the proof, it suffices to show that for appropriately chosen  $\pmb{\theta}^{*}$  such that  $\mathrm{sign}(\pmb{\theta}^{*}) = \mathrm{sign}(\pmb{x})$ , the minimum value of  $\mathcal{C}(\pmb{v})$  is smaller than  $-T$  with high probability.

By Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions, with probability at least  $1 - 2e^{-p\epsilon^2 /8}$

$$
\sup_{\substack{\| \boldsymbol {v}\|_{2}\leq 1\\ F(\boldsymbol {v};\boldsymbol {x},\boldsymbol {\Sigma})\leq 0}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\langle \boldsymbol {v},\boldsymbol {g}\rangle >\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}} +\frac{\epsilon}{2}.
$$

Thus, let  $\pmb{v} = \pmb{v}(\pmb{g})$  be a random variable such that  $\| \pmb{v}\|_2 = 1$  and  $F(\pmb{v};\pmb{x},\pmb{\Sigma}) \leq 0$  always, and  $\langle \pmb{v},\pmb{g}\rangle / \sqrt{p} > \sqrt{n/p} + \epsilon/2$  with probability at least  $1 - 2e^{-p\epsilon^2/8}$ . For some  $0 < \epsilon_1 \leq 1$ , let

$$
\boldsymbol {v} _ {1} = \boldsymbol {v} - \frac {\epsilon_ {1}}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {x}. \tag {109}
$$

Note that  $\| \pmb{v}_1\|_2 \leq 1 + \kappa_{\max}^{1/2}\epsilon_1$ ,  $F(\pmb{v}_1; \pmb{x}, \pmb{\Sigma}) = -\frac{\epsilon_1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\pmb{x}\|_2^2$ , and on the event that  $\langle \pmb{v}, \pmb{g} \rangle / \sqrt{p} > \sqrt{n/p} + \epsilon/2$  and  $\langle \pmb{\Sigma}^{1/2}\pmb{x}, \pmb{g} \rangle/p < \epsilon/4$ , which has probability at least  $1 - Ce^{-c\epsilon^2}$ , we have

$$
\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \langle \boldsymbol {v} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {g} \rangle > \sqrt {\frac {n}{p}} + \frac {\epsilon}{4}.
$$

Note that for  $\pmb{\theta}^{*} = t\kappa_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1 / 2}(1 + \kappa_{\mathrm{max}}^{1 / 2}\epsilon_{1})\pmb{x}$  and  $S = \operatorname {supp}(\pmb {x})$  , we have sign  $\bigl ((\pmb {\theta}^* +t\pmb {\Sigma}^{-1 / 2}\pmb {v}_1)_S\bigr) =$ $\operatorname {sign}(\pmb {x}_S)$  , whence  $\| \pmb {\theta}^{*} + t\pmb{\Sigma}^{-1 / 2}\pmb{v}_{1}\|_{1} - \| \pmb{\theta}^{*}\|_{1} = tF(\pmb{v}_{1};\pmb {x},\pmb {\Sigma})\leq -t\epsilon_{1}\| \pmb {x}\|_{2}^{2} / \sqrt{p}$  . Denoting  $\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol {v};\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})$  the objective (32) as a function also of  $\pmb{\theta}^{*}$  , and choosing  $\pmb{\theta}^{*}$  as above, we have

$$
\mathcal {L} (t \pmb {v} _ {1}; \pmb {\theta} ^ {*}) \leq \frac {1}{2} \left(\sqrt {\sigma^ {2} + t ^ {2} (1 + \kappa_ {\max} ^ {1 / 2} \epsilon_ {1}) ^ {2}} - t \Big (1 + \sqrt {\frac {p}{n}} \frac {\epsilon}{4} \Big)\right) _ {+} ^ {2} - \frac {t \epsilon_ {1} \| \pmb {x} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{\sqrt {p}},
$$

on the event (109). Now we see that if we take  $\epsilon_{1}$  small enough that  $1 + \kappa_{\max}^{1/2}\epsilon_{1} < 1 + \sqrt{p / n}\epsilon / 4$ , then the right-hand side above goes to  $-\infty$  as  $t \to \infty$ . Take  $\epsilon_{1}$  this small, and take  $t$  large enough so that the right-hand side above is smaller than  $-T$ . Thus, by Gordon's lemma (Lemma 6.1), and choosing  $\theta^{*}$  as above for this value of  $t$  and  $\epsilon_{1}$ , we conclude that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\inf  _ {\boldsymbol {v} \in \mathbb {R}} \mathcal {C} (\boldsymbol {v}) \leq - T\right) \geq C e ^ {- c p \epsilon^ {2}}.
$$

This completes the proof.

# C.2. Gaussian width under correlated designs: proof of Proposition 3.

PROOF OF PROPOSITION 3. Parts (a) and (b) of Proposition 3 now follow from the following constructions.

(a) We may take  $\bar{\theta}^{*} = \theta^{*}$ . This is then an immediate consequence of Lemma 1.  
(b) If  $\| \pmb{\theta}^{*}\|_{q}^{q} / p\leq \nu^{q}$  for some  $\nu >0$  and  $q > 0$ , take  $\bar{\theta}^*$  to be supported on the largest (in absolute value)  $s$  coordinates of  $\pmb{\theta}^{*}$ , and take  $\bar{\theta}_j^* = \theta_j^*$  for  $j$  on this support. The Gaussian width bound for  $\bar{\theta}^*$  of Assumption (A1)(d) holds by part (a). Because  $\| \pmb{\theta}^{*}\|_{q}\leq \nu$ , the  $s^{\mathrm{th}}$ -largest coordinate of  $\pmb{\theta}^{*}$  in absolute value is no larger than  $\nu / s^{1 / q}$ . Thus,  $\| \pmb{\theta}^{*} - \bar{\pmb{\theta}}^{*}\|_{1}\leq (p - s)\nu / s^{1 / q}$ , whence the  $\ell_1$ -approximation of (A1)(d) holds with  $M = \sqrt{n}\nu (1 - s / p) / p^{1 / q}$ .  
(c) As in part (b), take  $\bar{\theta}^*$  to be supported on the largest (in absolute value)  $s$  coordinates of  $\theta^*$ , and take  $\bar{\theta}_j^* = \theta_j^*$  for  $j$  on this support. Call this support  $S \subset [p]$ . As above, the Gaussian width bound for  $\bar{\theta}^*$  of Assumption (A1)(d) holds by part (a). Because  $\sum_{j=1}^{n} \min(1, \sqrt{n} |\theta_j^*| / \lambda) \leq s$ , there are at most  $s$  coordinates of  $\theta^*$  with  $\sqrt{n} |\theta_j^*| / \lambda \geq 1$ . In particular,  $\sqrt{n} |\theta_j^*| / \lambda < 1$  for all  $j \in S^c$ . Thus,  $\|\theta^* - \bar{\theta}^*\|_1 = \|\theta_{S^c}^* - \bar{\theta}_{S^c}^*\|_1 = \sum_{j=1}^{p} \min(1, \sqrt{n} |\theta_j^*| / \lambda) \leq s$ . Thus, the  $\ell_1$ -approximation of (A1)(d) holds with  $M = \lambda s / p$ .

The proof of Proposition 3 is complete.

![](images/8d943dae9e0dfb44af8159a8e93228c16cce6f1a5ddbafa4c7f8a467c26db96d.jpg)

C.3. Properties of the design matrix. Given every integer  $j \in \{1, \dots, p\}$ , each row of our design matrix is sampled independently from a multivariate Gaussian distribution, namely

$$
\text {f o r} i = 1, \ldots , n \quad (X _ {i, j}, \boldsymbol {X} _ {i, - j}) \sim \mathsf {N} \left(0, \frac {1}{n} \boldsymbol {\Sigma}\right) \qquad \boldsymbol {\Sigma} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \Sigma_ {j, j} & \boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {j, - j} \\ \boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {- j, j} & \boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {- j, - j} \end{array} \right),
$$

where the  $1 / n$  factor is due to the normalization of the design matrix. Here  $\mathbf{X}_{\cdot ,j}$  stands for the covariate corresponding to the  $j$ -th coordinate of  $\pmb{\theta}$  and  $\mathbf{X}_{\cdot , - j}\in \mathbb{R}^{(p - 1)}$  stands for covariates corresponding to rest of  $\pmb{\theta}$ .

Let us further define  $X_{j}^{\perp} \coloneqq X_{j} - \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{j, - j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{-j, - j}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{-j}$  for every  $j \in \{1, \ldots, p\}$  and the sampled version  $\check{x}^{\perp} \coloneqq \boldsymbol{x}_{j} - \mathbf{X}_{-j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{-j, - j}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{-j,j} \in \mathbb{R}^{n}$ . Then the linear model can be written as

$$
\boldsymbol {y} = \check {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {\perp} \theta_ {j} ^ {*} + \boldsymbol {X} _ {- j} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {- j} ^ {*} + \theta_ {j} ^ {*} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {- j, - j} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {- j, j}\right) + \sigma \boldsymbol {z}.
$$

In addition, we state without proof the following straightforward properties.

-  $X_{j}|X_{-j}\sim \mathsf{N}(\pmb{\Sigma}_{j, - j}\pmb{\Sigma}_{-j, - j}^{-1}\pmb{X}_{-j},\frac{1}{n} (\pmb{\Sigma}_{j,j} - \pmb{\Sigma}_{j, - j}\pmb{\Sigma}_{-j, - j}^{-1}\pmb{\Sigma}_{-j,j}))$  
$X_{j}^{\perp}|X_{-j}\sim \mathsf{N}(0,\frac{1}{n} (\Sigma_{j,j} - \Sigma_{j, - j}\Sigma_{-j, - j}^{-1}\Sigma_{-j,j})).$  
$X_{j}^{\perp}\sim \mathsf{N}(0,\frac{1}{n} (\Sigma_{j,j} - \Sigma_{j, - j}\Sigma_{-j, - j}^{-1}\Sigma_{-j,j})).$  
The entries of  $\check{\pmb{x}}^{\perp}$  are i.i.d with distribution  $\mathsf{N}(0,\frac{1}{n} (\Sigma_{j,j} - \Sigma_{j, - j}\Sigma_{-j, - j}^{-1}\Sigma_{-j,j})).$

# C.4. Proof of Proposition 13: restricted eigenvalues and the DT phase transition.

PROOF OF PROPOSITION 13. Because  $\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{RE}}$  is increasing in  $c$ , the restricted eigenvalue  $\theta(S, c)$  is decreasing in  $c$ . Thus, it suffices to show the result for  $c = 1$ . Note that for any  $\pmb{x}$  with  $\pmb{x}_S \in \{-1, 1\}^s$  and  $\pmb{x}_{S^c} = 0$ ,  $F(\pmb{w}; \pmb{x}, \mathbf{I}_p) = \langle \pmb{x}, \pmb{w} \rangle + \| \pmb{w}_{S^c} \|_1 \leq 0$  implies  $\| \pmb{w}_S \|_1 \geq \| \pmb{w}_{S^c} \|_1$ , whence  $\pmb{w} \in \mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{RE}}(S, 1)$ . Thus, for any such  $\pmb{x}$ ,

$$
\operatorname {RE}(S,1) = \inf_{\substack{\boldsymbol {\theta}:F(\boldsymbol {\theta};\boldsymbol {x},\mathbf{I}_{p})\leq 0\\ \| \boldsymbol {\theta}\|_{2} = 1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\| \boldsymbol {X}\boldsymbol {\theta}\|_{2}.
$$

Thus, our proof strategy is to find a  $\pmb{x}$  such that  $\inf_{\substack{\boldsymbol{\theta}: F(\boldsymbol{\theta}; \mathbf{x}, \mathbf{I}_p) \leq 0 \\ \| \boldsymbol{\theta} \|_2 = 1}} \frac{1}{\sqrt{n}} \| \boldsymbol{X} \boldsymbol{\theta} \|_2$  is 0 on some finite interval of sampling rates above  $\delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\boldsymbol{x}^*, \boldsymbol{\Sigma}) \coloneqq \mathcal{G}(\boldsymbol{x}^*, \boldsymbol{\Sigma})^2$ .

We will simplify the optimization Eq. (13) defining the Gaussian width for any  $\pmb{x}$  whose first  $s$  coordinates consist of  $s/2$  copies of some  $\pmb{x}^{(1)} \in \{-1,1\}^2$  and whose remaining coordinates are 0. First consider any  $\pmb{v} \in L^2$  (recall that this is a random variable in  $\mathbb{R}^p$ ). For  $j = 1, \dots, s/2$ , let  $\Pi_j$  be the marginal joint distribution of  $(\pmb{v}_{[2j-1,2j]}, \pmb{g}_{[2j-1,2j]})$ , and let  $\Pi^{(1)} = \frac{1}{s/2} \sum_{j=1}^{s/2} \Pi_j$  be the mixture of the joint distributions. For  $j = s+1, \dots, p$ , let  $\tilde{\Pi}_j$  be the joint distribution of  $(v_j, g_j)$ , and let  $\Pi^{(2)} = \frac{1}{p-s} \sum_{j=s+1}^{p} \tilde{\Pi}_j$  be the mixture of these joint distributions. Construct a new random vector  $\pmb{v}' \in L^2$  such that  $(\pmb{v}', \pmb{g})$  has joint distribution  $(\Pi^{(1)})^{\otimes s/2} \otimes (\Pi^{(2)})^{\otimes (p-s)}$ . The value of the objective and constraints in Eq. (13) are not changed by this replacement, so that we can compute the Gaussian width by optimizing over the distributions  $\Pi^{(1)}$  and  $\Pi^{(2)}$ . Writing in this way, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {G} (\boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {\Sigma}) = \sup  \left\{\mathbb {E} \left[ (\varepsilon / 2) \langle \boldsymbol {g} ^ {(1)}, \boldsymbol {v} ^ {(1)} \rangle + (1 - \varepsilon) G V \right]: \right. \\ \mathbb {E} \big [ (\varepsilon / 2) \| \boldsymbol {v} ^ {(1)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + (1 - \varepsilon) V ^ {2} \big ] \leq 1, \mathbb {E} \big [ (\varepsilon / 2) \langle \boldsymbol {K} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(1)}, \boldsymbol {v} ^ {(1)} \rangle + (1 - \varepsilon) | V | \big ] \leq 0 \big \}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $s / p = \epsilon$ , and  $\pmb{g}^{(1)} \sim \mathsf{N}(0, \mathbf{I}_2)$ ,  $G \sim \mathsf{N}(0, 1)$ , and  $\pmb{v}^{(1)}$  and  $V$  are random variables in  $\mathbb{R}^2$  and  $\mathbb{R}$ , respectively. Because  $\pmb{x}^*$  is of the form under consideration, we have shown that

$\delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\pmb{x}^*, \pmb{\Sigma})$  does not depend on  $p$ . Let  $\tilde{\pmb{g}}^{(1)}$  and  $\tilde{\pmb{v}}^{(1)}$  be the vectors  $\pmb{v}^{(1)}$  and  $\pmb{g}^{(1)}$  written in a basis consisting of the unit vector  $\pmb{K}^{-1/2}\pmb{x}^{(1)}/\|\pmb{K}^{-1/2}\pmb{x}^{(1)}\|_2$  and the unit vector orthogonal to it. In this basis, the optimization above is

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sup  \left\{\mathbb {E} \left[ (\varepsilon / 2) \langle \tilde {\boldsymbol {g}} ^ {(1)}, \tilde {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {(1)} \rangle + (1 - \varepsilon) G V \right]: \right. \\ \left. \mathbb {E} \left[ (\varepsilon / 2) \| \tilde {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {(1)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + (1 - \varepsilon) V ^ {2} \right] \leq 1, \mathbb {E} \left[ (\varepsilon / 2) \xi \tilde {v} _ {1} ^ {(1)} + (1 - \varepsilon) | V | \right] \leq 0 \right\}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\xi = \| K^{-1/2} x^{(1)} \|_2$ . Because  $\tilde{\pmb{g}}^{(1)} \sim \mathsf{N}(0, \mathbf{I}_2)$ , the value of the optimization problem depends on  $\pmb{K}$  and  $\pmb{x}^{(1)}$  only via  $\xi$ .

Moreover, the value of the optimization is strictly increasing in  $\xi$ , as we now show. First, observe that  $(\tilde{\pmb{v}}^{(1)}, V) = (\mathbf{0}_2, 0)$  is not a solution to the optimization problem. Indeed, a better solution which satisfies the constraints is  $(\tilde{\pmb{v}}^{(1)}, V) = (\tilde{\pmb{g}}^{(1)}, 0)$ . Moreover, the second constraint must bind at the solution. Indeed, the unique solution to the optimization when the second constraint is removed is  $(\tilde{\pmb{v}}^{(1)}, V) = (\tilde{\pmb{g}}^{(1)}, G)$  almost surely, which violates the second constraint. Now, consider  $\xi < \xi'$ . Because  $(\tilde{\pmb{v}}^{(1)}, V)$  is not identically 0, the second constraint implies that the solution  $(\tilde{\pmb{v}}^{(1)}, V)$  at  $\xi$  must satisfy  $\mathbb{E}[\tilde{v}_1^{(1)}] < 0$ . The second constraint under  $\xi'$  is strictly feasible when evaluated at this solution. Because the objective and the first constraint do not depend on  $\xi$ , and the second constraint must bind at the solution, the value of the optimization at  $\xi'$  must be strictly larger than at  $\xi$ , as claimed.

For  $\rho > 0$  and taking  $\boldsymbol{x}^{*(1)} = (1,1)^\top$  and  $\boldsymbol{x}^{(1)} = (1, -1)^\top$  by the argument above, both  $\delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\boldsymbol{x}^{*(1)}, \boldsymbol{K}) := \delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\boldsymbol{x}^*, \boldsymbol{\Sigma}) = \mathcal{G}(\boldsymbol{x}^*, \boldsymbol{\Sigma})^2$  and  $\delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\boldsymbol{x}^{(1)}, \boldsymbol{K}) := \delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\boldsymbol{x}, \boldsymbol{\Sigma}) = \mathcal{G}(\boldsymbol{x}, \boldsymbol{\Sigma})^2$  do not depend on  $p$ . Direct evaluation yields  $\| \boldsymbol{K}^{-1/2} \boldsymbol{x}^{*(1)} \|_2 < \| \boldsymbol{K}^{-1/2} \boldsymbol{x}^{(1)} \|_2$ , which implies  $\delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\boldsymbol{x}^{*(1)}, \boldsymbol{K}) < \delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\boldsymbol{x}^{(1)}, \boldsymbol{K})$ . By Proposition 2,  $\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{std}}(\boldsymbol{x}, \boldsymbol{\Sigma})^2 \to \delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\boldsymbol{x}^{(1)}, \boldsymbol{K})$  as  $p \to \infty$ . By [2, Proposition 2.4, Fact 2.8, and Theorem II] (which locate the Donoho-Tanner phase transition based on the standard Gaussian width  $\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{std}}(\boldsymbol{x}, \boldsymbol{\Sigma})$ ), for  $\delta < \mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{std}}(\boldsymbol{x}^{(1)}, \boldsymbol{K})$ , we have that  $\inf_{\substack{\boldsymbol{\theta}: F(\boldsymbol{\theta}; \boldsymbol{x}, \mathbf{I}_p) \leq 0 \\ \| \boldsymbol{\theta}\|_2 = 1}} \frac{1}{\sqrt{n}} \| \boldsymbol{X} \boldsymbol{\theta}\|_2 = 0$  with probability going to 1. Thus, the Proposition holds

by taking  $\Delta = \delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\pmb{x}^{(1)},\pmb{K}) - \delta_{\mathrm{DT}}(\pmb{x}^{*(1)},\pmb{K})$

# D. Additional Simulations.

D.1. Hidden Markov model specification. In the hidden Markov model, the covariates  $x_{ij}$  are conditionally independent given latent states  $s_{ij}$  which are generated according to a Markov chain. In particular, the distribution satisfies  $\mathbb{P}(s_{i(j + 1)}\mid \{s_{i\ell}\}_{\ell \leq j}) = \mathbb{P}(s_{i(j + 1)}\mid s_{ij})$ . The latent states (values for  $s_{ij}$ ) and observed values (values of  $x_{ij}$ ) in the hidden Markov model that we consider here, take values in  $\{1,2,3,4,5\}$ . Both the transition and emission probabilities are given by a symmetric random walk with reflection at the boundary; that is,

$$
\mathbb {P} (s _ {i (j + 1)} = a | s _ {i j} = b) = \mathbb {P} (x _ {i j} = a | s _ {i j} = b) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} 1 / 2 & b \in \{2, 3, 4 \} \text {a n d} | a - b | = 1, \\ 1 & b \in \{1, 2 \} \text {a n d} | a - b | = 1, \\ 0 & \text {o t h e r w i s e}. \end{array} \right.
$$

We initialize this Markov chain (i.e.,  $s_{i1}$ ) from its stationary distribution. In this case, the covariance of  $x_{ij}$  and  $x_{ij'}$  is only a function of  $|j - j'|$ , as plotted below. We see that covari-

ates which are within approximately distance 10 of each other have non-trivial correlation.

![](images/4c2be498235568f81b6f1b1fc6c96a46b985cda7fd95550e86b6e68de089beca.jpg)

D.2. Debiasing under Gaussian AR1 models. Here we collect simulations which repeat those in Figure 1 at different model parameters. These simulations demonstrate the success of debiasing at many settings of the model parameters. In particular, we run the simulations varying the correlation parameter  $\rho = 0, .5, .8$ , the sample size  $n = 500, 750$ , and the sparsity  $s = 20, 100, 200$ . We show the legend for the first two plots. The legend for the remaining plots is the same.
